Cloud sessions can't download Playwright's browsers, so there `PW_CHROMIUM_EXECUTABLE` runs the functional Chromium specs only, and CI is the WebKit, Firefox and visual gate.
    - Visual baselines come only from CI's runner (D-07). A local run neither writes nor compares a PNG (`ignoreSnapshots`), so the auto-push hook can't commit one. On the runner a missing or changed shot fails and writes its actual. A person adopts them from the `check` job's `e2e-report` artifact with `apps/web/e2e/support/adopt-baselines.ts`, looks at each in both themes, and commits them. `check` is pinned to `ubuntu-24.04`, so moving to a newer image is a deliberate PR that adopts them again. A PR that changes how a shot looks (a token, a font file, a Playwright upgrade) fails VS-01, so a person adopts its new baselines before it merges.
    - Shots are compared at `threshold: 0.005` with no pixel allowance (`maxDiffPixels: 0`, no ratio), so changing the colour of a design token a shot paints fails VS-01, not only moving text or shapes. No shot paints `--wall`, `--agent` or `--ok` (no screen uses them yet), `--warn` (only the connection banner does) or `--selection` (selected text), so the shot that first shows one covers it. pixelmatch counts a pixel as changed when its colour difference (YIQ) passes 35215 × threshold². Playwright's default of 0.2 passes a grey shift of up to 52 levels in 255, and with a 0.5% ratio the re-theme (#23) passed four old-look baselines (`chats` and `avatar-menu` at 1440, light and dark). Even 0.01 passes 2 levels: on the runner, `--bg` moved by 2 failed every light shot at 0.005 and none at 0.01, and moved by 3 none at 0.02. At 0.005 a colour moved by 2 levels in every channel fails (or by 2 to 4 in one channel alone: green 2, red 3, blue 4), and ±1 in any channel passes. That covers the drift the pinned runner shows. A probe in October 2026 (#24) compared CI runs on AMD EPYC 7763, 9V45 and 9V74 runners with baselines another run had made, and found two kinds of drift. First, a few edge pixels in some shots were drawn differently (up to 17 in a shot, by up to 23 levels), which pixelmatch takes for anti-aliasing and skips at any threshold. Second, some shots had 1 to 3 other pixels off by one level, sometimes in a single capture and sometimes in every capture of a run (both More shots, in both schemes). `toHaveScreenshot` retakes a shot until two captures agree, which hides the first case but not the second, so threshold 0 would fail at random and 0.005 lets both through. A pixel ratio lets small things through too, since 0.5% of a 1440 × 900 shot is more pixels than a button, a hairline or a line of error text. pixelmatch skips pixels it takes for anti-aliasing at any threshold. The cost is that a Playwright upgrade that changes how Chromium draws fails VS-01, and its baselines are adopted again as for a new image. `scripts/guards/e2e-setup.test.ts` pins the tolerance (ES-17) and holds it, with Playwright's own comparator on a patch of each token's colour, to passing every ±1 rounding and failing a 2-level move (ES-18). ES-17 also refuses the three ways a shot could compare at another tolerance: options of its own, a comparison through `toMatchSnapshot`, which reads its own options (Playwright's default threshold is 0.2), and a project with an `expect` of its own, which replaces the config's whole block.
    - axe finds nothing serious or critical against WCAG 2.2 A and AA on any screen at 320, 390 and 1440 px, light and dark, and again with the wash taken off, on every shell screen, the phone step and the code step with an error at 390 and 1440 px (A11Y-04, which first waits until no `.wash` element draws an image), since axe lists text over a gradient as incomplete, which fails nothing; axe checks 24 px targets, so the house's 44 px has a check of its own; the NFR-02 phone suite checks 16 px inputs, reflow, text spacing and size, reduced motion and forced colours on four phone sizes; and there are keyboard, CSP, sign-in, live-connection and session-end specs. Retries are 0, and in CI the run stops at 15 minutes.
    - Licences: axe-core (MPL-2.0) is a dev dependency only; the fonts (Inter and Newsreader) are OFL-1.1 and lucide ISC.
12. **Bundle budget** (`apps/web/bundle-budget.json`, OD-15): what the build measured, plus about 20% (each figure × 1.2, rounded up to the next 100 bytes). C0.8's budget came from a build that measured 147,766 bytes of initial JS (gzip), 6,750 of initial CSS, 7,214 for the largest lazy chunk and 61,500 of latin fonts; C0.8's last commit measured 148,107, 6,767, 7,536 and 61,500. With finance's look the budget came from a build that measured 148,166 bytes of initial JS (gzip; budget 177,800), 7,507 of initial CSS (9,100), 7,574 for the largest lazy chunk (9,100) and 72,132 of latin fonts (86,600: Inter's 48,256 and Newsreader 600's 23,876); that PR's last commit measured 148,171, 7,556, 7,573 and 72,132. `scripts/check-web-dist.ts` measures them from Vite's manifest after the build, and scans `dist/`: nothing inline in `index.html`, no `data:` URL in CSS, no public npm CDN, model host or Google Fonts, no test hook, and every avatar fill compiled. Raising a figure is a reviewed change that says why.
13. **The sign-in contract** (T20). `apps/web/src/sign-in/api.ts` is the one file that knows it, and reads every answer with `packages/shared`'s schemas: `/otp/request` answers 200 `code_sent`; `/otp/verify` answers `signed_in`, or `code_verified {next: 'set_pin'}`, or `code_verified {next: 'step_up', pin}`; `/pin/set` and `/pin/verify` answer `signed_in`. `sign-in-model.ts`, a pure reducer, decides by the refusal's code alone. The client waits 30 s before offering "Send a new code", gives up on a call after 20 s, and repeats a 409 `try_again` once, which finishes a verified attempt with the same body. When a code or PIN step's answer is lost or doesn't read, the server may still have issued the session and set its cookie, so the client asks `GET /api/session` before showing the retry, and opens the app in a new document when it names a person (item 2). The PIN steps (set, enter, locked) serve PIN-role holders (AQ-02). There is no passkey UI, and nothing on the sign-in screens reads a flag (WS-11). The re-authentication sheet waits for C0.16 (T20 item 6).
14. **Recorded for later steps:**
    - C0.9's notice store (PR 2, `session/notice.ts`) calls `gateway.resume()` once `/notice` is acknowledged, and the notice screen fills `/notice` (built: T22 item 31);
    - C0.12's CDK test (item 8), and its CloudFront origin request policy forwards `Origin` and `Sec-Fetch-Site` to the api, or the Origin hook refuses every state change, Safari's CSP reports (item 9) included;
    - C0.13's RUM endpoint joins `connect-src` through `cspDirectives()` and the CSP guard's allow-list in the same PR, or the browser blocks it;
    - **C0.14's service worker must not cache `index.html` or `/api`, and must pass `session-end.spec.ts`**; it adds `manifest-src` and `worker-src`;
    - C0.15 wraps C0.8's sign-in in the first-run step list, and C0.9's `/notice` after the PIN, with no Skip and the same return path, and adds visual baselines for `/notice` and `/settings*`;
    - C0.16 adds more capability booleans to `GET /api/session` (C0.9 added `registers`) and the re-authentication sheet for `reauth_required {method}`, which replaces the DPO pages' inline PIN step, aligns the older GETs' cookie rule (item 10), and adds more Settings sections to the registry;
    - C1.7 mounts the Search bar;
    - C1.8 adds the unsynced-edits step before `end()`, and the first user-specific browser storage;
    - C2's LiveKit Cloud `wss://` host joins `connect-src` the same way, and C1.6 and C2 open the microphone in `Permissions-Policy`;
    - A0 fills the AI slots (`AI_SLOT_CONTENT`) behind their flags.
15. **The first-week checklist** (OD-18; BUILD-PLAN 1E.4). C0.8's rows, and the specs that meet them: the web shell at 320, 390 and 1440 px (SH-01 to SH-03, NF-10, A11Y-01); sign-in by code on a roster number, the PIN for PIN roles, and the same reply for any other number (SI-02, SI-09, SI-03); the flags reaching the app with every AI slot empty (LV-01, FS-02); and the live connection, closed within 60 s by a sign-out elsewhere (LV-02, SE-04).

## T22: the registers and the purpose and transfer gates, defaults for the owner to confirm or change

The OD ids in this section are C0.9's owner decisions, and the PD ids are the defaults C0.9 took, all listed in the C0.9 PRs; from item 63 the first-Owner bootstrap PR continues both runs (OD-28 to OD-32, PD-114 to PD-136). They are not C0.8's OD or AQ ids (T20, T21), C0.7's OD ids (T19), nor the PRD's decisions. Outside T22 this file writes them as "C0.9 OD-01" and "C0.9 PD-07" (PD-01). Every PD id that code cites is one this section records (`scripts/guards/design-ids.test.ts`). One T22 covers all of C0.9: PR 1 writes items 1 to 20, PR 2 items 21 to 37, PR 3a items 38 to 49, PR 3b items 50 to 62, and the bootstrap PR, which carries C0.9's hand-offs to the first-Owner bootstrap (items 16, 20, 37 and 44), items 63 to 71 (PD-02).

The owner decided on 22 Oct 2026:
- the bootstrap clears Twilio's and AWS's transfer-register entries from facts given at run time, audited (OD-01, item 16);
- C0.9 is built in three PRs, each with its own migration and owner read (OD-02);
- minimal DPO-only pages, reached from the avatar menu and re-authenticating with the PIN, which C0.16 moves into `/admin` (OD-03, item 15);
- the org's employee data-protection policy and the invitation are the prior notice, and v0 states the Twilio transfer and is acknowledged straight after the first sign-in, before anything else (OD-04, items 24 and 29; the DPO to confirm the reading).

The owner decided on 23 Oct 2026, accepting PR 3's decisions together (C0.9 PR 3):
- C0.9 is built in four PRs: PR 3 is split into 3a (0011) and 3b (0012), to keep each within CodeRabbit's file limit (OD-05, item 38);
- one case table, whose request details aren't Rule 9 material (OD-06, item 52);
- every request kind is taken as intake and handled by hand (OD-07, item 56);
- 10 business days for withdrawals, objections, questions and complaints, and 30 calendar days for access and correction, which the DPO alone changes within a range fixed in code, a change making the notice out of date (OD-08, item 55; OD-26 set the range);
- business days are weekdays in the org's time zone (OD-09, item 55);
- the DPO sets their contact in the app with the PIN: a role title and at least one channel outside Hearth (OD-10, item 39);
- a contact change makes everyone acknowledge the next version, as PD-39 stands (OD-11, item 40);
- the contact is on Settings › Privacy now, with Help from C0.18 (OD-12, item 41);
- nobody sends a request before acknowledging the current notice: they use the contact it prints (OD-13, items 41 and 56; the gate is unchanged);
- cases are the DPO's alone, and the complaints procedure names the PDPC for a complaint about the DPO (OD-14, items 46, 52 and 57);
- a requester's tracking shows only cases they made (OD-15, item 56);
- for a requester without an account, Hearth keeps a name, one channel and a note of the identity check (OD-16, item 54, where the check is a step on the case);
- the request-handling purpose starts active and unpinned (OD-17, item 51);
- publishing stops refusing deemed consent by conduct, under a written per-purpose procedure (OD-18, item 42; PD-86 ties each purpose's use of it to its row in that procedure);
- the DPO records acknowledgements of the latest version only, for an active person, with a note of how and when (OD-19, item 43);
- Twilio's recovery is a guarded break-glass re-clear in the bootstrap's function, run by a person (OD-20, item 44);
- every runbook and procedure lives in `docs/runbooks/` (OD-21, item 46);
- before C0.14, an in-app indicator and the runbook's routine tell the DPO of a new case (OD-22, item 57; the routine, item 46).

The owner decided on 26 Oct 2026, accepting PR 3b's recommendations and its defaults together (C0.9 PR 3b, "Accept all"; item 62):
- a request's audit rows reach only a reader who also holds `dpo`, whatever roles the org lets read the log (OD-23, item 59);
- the DPO sees the count of requests needing attention without a fresh PIN: a number, never a name or a word (OD-24, item 57);
- the form keeps at most 10 open requests per person, counted plainly, a bound rather than an exact limit, while the contact outside Hearth always works (OD-25, item 56);
- reply targets range from 5 to 10 business days and from 14 to 30 calendar days (OD-26, item 55);
- no outbox consumer reads an event's actor from PR 3b, and the first migration after PR 3b's worker is deployed revokes `outbox_events.actor_id` from the offboarding and revocation roles (OD-27, item 59; C0.10's PR 1, 0013, carried it, since no environment ever ran a worker older than PR 3b: T23 item 16, LH-49).

The owner decided on 27 Oct 2026, accepting the bootstrap PR's recommendations and its defaults together ("Accept all"; item 71):
- the sole Owner's break-glass PIN reset is its own PR, before C0.15, as a fourth mode of the Owner bootstrap script with its own migration (OD-28, items 63 and 71; C0.10's PR 1 took 0013, T23 item 1);
- the script corrects the founder's number only while they have never signed in and no notice is published, at their own request with them present (OD-29, item 68);
- Qodo's review is no longer a merge gate, in the owner's own words ("always merge without qodo from now on"), and Qodo's GitHub App is to be uninstalled, after which `.pr_agent.toml` goes (OD-30, item 71);
- an environment that holds a real person's data counts as production for coding agents and logs, and non-prod is set up only once it has its own break-glass role, bootstrap login, environment marker and parameter group (OD-31, item 71);
- production runs the script from a checkout of the deployed commit, under the break-glass role, over a network path C0.11 provides (OD-32, items 63 and 71).

The rest are C0.9's choices, each a Default the owner confirms or changes in the PR that made it. They are pinned by:
- **DB tests:** `db/tests/150_registers.sql`, `155_transfers.sql`, from PR 2 `160_notice.sql`, from PR 3a `165_dpo_settings.sql` and `170_recorded_acknowledgements.sql`, and from PR 3b `175_request_cases.sql`, RQD-15 in `050_read_audit_events.sql` and DCD-11 to DCD-15 in `165_dpo_settings.sql`, and from the bootstrap PR BSC-05's new case in `155_transfers.sql`, with `010`, `015`, `020`, `030`, `032` and `040`;
- **Integration:** `apps/server/src/compliance/registers.integration.test.ts` and, from PR 2, `compliance/notice.integration.test.ts`; the transfer-gate cases in `apps/server/src/auth/sign-in.integration.test.ts`, the send-state cases (XFR-I4 to I7) among them; the purpose cases in `apps/server/src/flags/flags.integration.test.ts`, PGT-I1 among them; and from PR 3b `compliance/cases.integration.test.ts` (RQI-01 to RQI-12) and NTI-13 and NTI-14 in the notice suite; and from the bootstrap PR `apps/server/src/auth/first-owner.integration.test.ts` (BSI-01 to BSI-03);
- **Unit, server:** beside the code: `compliance/registers.test.ts`, `compliance/gate.test.ts`, `compliance/routes.test.ts`, `flags/cache.test.ts`, `flags/set.test.ts`, `jobs/registry.test.ts`, `worker/index.test.ts`, `revocation/registry.test.ts`, `outbox/consumers.test.ts`, `auth/sign-in.test.ts`, `api.test.ts` and `web/routes.test.ts`, and from PR 2 `access/world.test.ts`, `compliance/notice.test.ts`, `compliance/transfers.test.ts`, `auth/account.test.ts`, `db/sqlstate.test.ts` and `realtime/index.test.ts`, and from PR 3b `compliance/cases.test.ts` (RQV-01 to RQV-09);
- **Unit, packages:** `packages/shared`'s `registers.test.ts`, `catalogues.test.ts`, `web.test.ts` and, from PR 2, `notice.test.ts` and `notice-text.test.ts`, from PR 3a `dpo-contact.test.ts`, and from PR 3b `cases.test.ts` (RQS-01 to RQS-10), NTG-18 to NTG-22, NTS-08 to NTS-10, DCS-07 to DCS-09, RGS-20 and RQS-11, and from the bootstrap PR `org-name.test.ts` (BSU-01) and `TransferFactsBody`'s RGS-14 case; and `packages/access`'s `roles.test.ts` (from PR 3b RQA-01 to RQA-03) and, from PR 2, `notice.test.ts`;
- **Web:** the DPP cases in `apps/web/src/dpo/` and `apps/web/src/shell/nav.test.ts`, and `apps/web/e2e/dpo.spec.ts`; from PR 2 the NTW cases beside the code (`apps/web/src/notice/`, `settings/`, `session/`, `live/`, `router/`, `dpo/`), `apps/web/src/notice/notice-copy.test.ts`, and SI-10, NTE-01 and SE-05 in `apps/web/e2e/`; from PR 3a the CTW cases (`apps/web/src/dpo/contact-model.test.ts`) and DPP-E7 and DPP-E8; and from PR 3b the RQW cases (`apps/web/src/settings/requests-model.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/dpo/requests-model.test.ts`), CTW-06 and CTW-07, NTW-14 and NTW-15, and DPP-E9, DPP-E10 and NTE-02;
- **Scripts:** check 6 in `apps/server/scripts/check-catalogues.ts`, with its unit cases in `catalogues.test.ts`; the race cases in `apps/server/scripts/check-concurrency.ts` (from PR 3b RQR-01 to RQR-09); the backfill assertion in `scripts/check-revocation-upgrade.sh`, from PR 2 `scripts/check-notice-upgrade.sh` and from PR 3b `scripts/check-cases-upgrade.sh` (RQU-01 to RQU-08); and the guards `scripts/guards/design-ids.test.ts` and `api-test-overrides.test.ts`, and from PR 2 `realtime-readonly.test.ts` and `web-source.test.ts`'s WS-11, from PR 3a `runbooks.test.ts` (item 46), and from PR 3b `source-text.test.ts` (SRC-01, item 59); and from the bootstrap PR the Owner bootstrap script's unit tests (`scripts/owner-bootstrap/*.test.ts`, BSU-02 to BSU-12), its check (`apps/server/scripts/check-owner-bootstrap.ts`, BSS-01 to BSS-16 and BSR-01 to BSR-05) and its guard (`scripts/guards/owner-bootstrap.test.ts`, BSG-01 to BSG-10), item 70.

The register's poll and staleness are the flags' (`FLAG_POLL_MS`, `FLAG_STALE_MS`, `apps/server/src/flags/cache.ts`). The vocabulary and the shared rules live in `packages/shared/src/registers.ts`, and `PINNED_PURPOSES` in `flags.ts`; the notice's vocabulary in `notice.ts`, and its generator and copy in `notice-text.ts`. The limits and the clearing facts live in migration 0009, which the shared rules mirror, the notice's functions in 0010 and 0011 (which re-creates `publish_notice()` and adds `record_notice_acknowledgement()`), the DPO's contact and its limits in 0011, which `packages/shared/src/dpo-contact.ts` mirrors, and the requests' limits and the reply targets' range in 0012, which `packages/shared/src/cases.ts` and `dpo-contact.ts` mirror. No C0.9 PR adds a server variable; the bootstrap PR documents the Owner bootstrap script's two, `OWNER_BOOTSTRAP_INPUT` and `OWNER_BOOTSTRAP_TLS_NAME`, which the server never reads (item 64).

1. **Scope (OD-02).** PR 1 adds:
   - the two registers;
   - the purpose gate and the real transfer gate, in the api, the flags route and the worker;
   - `isProcessingAllowed()` over the `processing_restrictions` hook;
   - the database side of OD-01;
   - check 6;
   - the two DPO pages.

   It leaves out the notice, its publishing, the acknowledgement gate and `/notice` (PR 2), and the DPO's contact, the form, the case queue and the runbooks: PR 3a (the contact, recorded acknowledgements, Twilio's re-clear and the runbooks: items 38 to 49) and PR 3b (the form and the cases). PR 2 (items 21 to 37) adds the notice, publishing, the acknowledgement gate and `/notice`.
2. **The processing register** (PDPA-01; PD-04, PD-11, PD-28).
   - **Versions.** One entry per org and `PURPOSES` key, in versions as `notice_versions` keeps them: a change inserts version n+1 under a lock on version n, and nothing is updated or deleted. The current entry is the highest version. Version 1 is Hearth's and names nobody.
   - **What an entry records:**
     - its status (planned, active or retired);
     - one of PDPA-01's seven bases, with its attachment: the assessment for legitimate interests; the assessment and an opt-out window of 1 to 365 days for deemed consent by notification (s15A); the provision for another exception;
     - its retention;
     - the providers involved (non-AI `PROVIDERS` keys).
   - **Fixed facts.** Three come from `processing_register_defaults()`, and every version copies them: `ai`, `pinned` and `needs_switch`. In code they are `PURPOSES`' `ai` and `needsSwitch`, and `PINNED_PURPOSES`. `PurposeSpec.consent` is gone: the basis the register records decides (PD-28).
   - **The database refuses:**
     - an AI purpose that isn't planned with nothing recorded (the second lock, D41);
     - a pinned purpose that isn't active, or rests on a consent-type basis (consent, or deemed consent by conduct or by notification);
     - a consent-type basis for a purpose whose withdrawal needs a per-person switch. That is PDPA-04's list: recording, transcription, notes, voice-message transcription, Org Brain or AI use; the transcription test set is exempt, being withdrawn by deletion. PDPA-04 names consent and deemed consent by notification; deemed consent by conduct is refused too, since it can be withdrawn and nothing can carry out one person's withdrawal before the switches exist. C2.11 lifts all three together.
     - an active or retired purpose with no basis or retention once the DPO has edited it.
   - **Warnings.** PDPA-01's two warnings show and never block. Business improvement always warns, since the register can't tell use from collection or disclosure, so the DPO checks the purpose collects and discloses nothing on it. A consent-type basis warns for what the recording policy keeps with no opt-out (`NO_OPT_OUT_PURPOSES`: both recordings, the session's slides, transcription and meeting notes; REC-02, REC-04, D11). A third says that until switches exist, a withdrawal for one person is handled by hand. They are `purposeWarnings()`'s codes (`packages/shared/src/registers.ts`); the page words them.
   - "Each overseas transfer" is the transfer register's entries, with no `PURPOSES` key.
3. **The transfer register and its clearances** (PDPA-10, D46; PD-05, PD-12, PD-13, PD-14).
   - **The entry.** One per org and `PROVIDERS` key. Its facts change in place under a revision:
     - services and data types;
     - processing and storage countries (ISO codes), with where and when the provider confirmed them in writing;
     - the safeguard and its reference, with the certificate's verification date (a certification counts only with it);
     - that the agreement binds PDPA-comparable protection, names the countries and requires prompt incident notice;
     - training (off, or not applicable);
     - the zero-retention result;
     - the provider's retention, with its written confirmation;
     - who carries the data onward;
     - a review date.

     `aws` covers hosting and CloudWatch with RUM; `aws_sms` stays not cleared.
   - **History.** Because facts change in place, each change's audit row carries every changed fact's old and new value (`{"changed": {"<fact>": {"from": …, "to": …}}}`). Facts hold nobody's personal data.
   - **Clearing.** A clearance is a row of `transfer_clearances`: who decided (the DPO), how (`app` or `bootstrap`), and, stamped by its trigger, the revision and a snapshot of the facts it rests on. It ends once, by the DPO's decision or because new facts were recorded (`keep_history`). The trigger refuses one until every fact above is recorded, a `pending` zero-retention result isn't one, and the review date is after today in the org's zone. Text of only whitespace (JavaScript's `trim()` set) is no fact, so the trigger and `transferProblems()` refuse the same facts whoever wrote them (RGS-I4), and the table stores text facts trimmed of spaces. Facts can't change while a clearance is open. Recording them ends it, and "Save and keep cleared" clears the new facts in the same transaction, or changes nothing when they're incomplete.
   - **Saving a cleared entry.** On a cleared entry the page's main action is "Save and keep cleared". A plain Save there ends the clearance, so it asks for the same confirmation as "Withdraw clearance", with the same consequence: for Twilio Verify, no sign-in codes until it is cleared again.
   - **Who checks each clearance today.** The page says it for each provider. Twilio Verify's is checked before each sign-in and re-authentication code (item 8). Every other provider's: "Nothing in Hearth sends to it yet". AWS's hosting and logs run whatever the register says; RUM checks AWS's clearance from C0.13, and push its services' from C0.14. Withdrawing `aws` is allowed, and its confirmation says it stops nothing yet, since nothing checks it until C0.13.
   - **Review dates.** A passed review date never ends a clearance. The page marks it overdue, judged in the org's zone, and the worker logs `transfer_review_overdue` (the org, the provider and its review date) at warn, once a day for each org and provider, judged by the UTC day.
4. **Rows for every org** (PD-03, PD-06).
   - **How they're made.** `processing_register_defaults()` and `transfer_register_defaults()` list each key's fixed facts and starting state. A trigger on `orgs` gives a new org its rows, and 0009 inserts them for orgs already there. Hearth's rows name nobody, so an org with no principals gets them. A step that adds a key replaces the function and inserts rows for existing orgs, as T17 item 1 does for flags.
   - **The starting state.** `accounts` and `service_operation` are active and pinned. `security_audit` and `backups` are active and not pinned, because Hearth already processes for them: it has written the audit log since C0.3, and every RDS database is backed up. All four start with no basis recorded, since version 1 is Hearth's and Hearth never asserts a legal basis for the DPO: the DPO records it, and PR 2's publishing refuses while any active purpose lacks a basis or retention. Everything else is planned, and nothing is cleared. Code holds the active list as `ACTIVE_FROM_START` (`packages/shared/src/registers.ts`), and check 6 holds the defaults function to it. The two that aren't pinned still fail the purpose gate in PR 1, since nothing is notified yet (item 6).
   - **Missing rows.** A missing row reads as refused or not cleared.
5. **Pinned purposes** (PD-07).
   - **What they are.** The purposes of every flag locked on: `PINNED_PURPOSES`, from `FLAGS`. Today that is `core.system`'s `accounts` and `service_operation`, which revocation, offboarding and maintenance carry.
   - **How they behave.** The gate allows them in every state: never read, stale or loaded. The database keeps them active and never consent-based. `processingAllowed` is true for them, so no consumer passes over a leaver's event. The providers they list are informational; each outbound client checks its own (item 8).
   - **What this widens.** T19 item 4, from `accounts` to both purposes. `service_operation` stays its own purpose.
6. **When a purpose is allowed** (PD-08, PD-09).
   - **The rule.** Never for an AI purpose (D41). Always for a pinned one. Otherwise: its current version is active, it is notified, and every provider that version lists is cleared.
   - **"Notified"** means the highest published version lists the purpose at its current register version (PD-40, item 27). While no version lists one, no purpose but the pinned ones is allowed, which PGT-04 now states.
7. **Processing restrictions and `isProcessingAllowed()`** (PDPA-04a seam; PD-10).
   - **The hook.** `processing_restrictions` holds a person, a purpose, an effective time, who set it, a case id (a plain id until PR 3b; from 0012 a foreign key to one of the org's own cases, item 52), and its lift (`keep_history`). Its trigger admits only a purpose whose current basis is consent-type. A basis can't leave consent-type while restrictions are live. There is no writer and no `hearth_app` grant until C2.11; `hearth_queue` reads it for the worker.
   - **`isProcessingAllowed(person, purpose)`** (`apps/server/src/compliance/registers.ts`, beside `purposeAllowed()` and `providerCleared()`):
     - pinned → allowed;
     - basis `consent` → not allowed until consent records exist (C1.3; PR 2 publishes no consent-type basis, item 25);
     - deemed consent (by conduct or by notification) → allowed unless a live restriction of theirs has taken effect; refused when the restrictions weren't read;
     - any other recorded basis → allowed;
     - none recorded, an AI purpose, or the register unread → refused.
   - **A stale register never withholds.** An event a consumer withholds is passed over for good, since its checkpoint moves past it. In PR 1 the pinned purposes are always allowed, so nothing is withheld, and G1 (item 11) keeps every consumer on a pinned purpose, since a consumer serves one of its flag's purposes. From PR 2 a round that meets a subject's refusal while the register no longer allows the consumer's purpose rolls back and keeps its place (`RoundRefused`, item 32).
8. **The transfer gate at outbound clients** (PD-15).
   - **How the api checks.** It keeps no copy. `transfer_cleared(provider)` reads the org's open clearance `FOR SHARE` inside the transaction that decides the send: `deliverCode`'s, as the org's system principal, and `sendReauthCode`'s, as the person. From PR 2 both codes ask `transfer_send_state(provider)`, which reads the clearance the same way (item 27). `Api.transfers` is `DB_TRANSFERS` (`apps/server/src/compliance/transfers.ts`), set in `registerApi`, and `ApiDeps.gate` is gone, so production can't miss the gate. Unit tests set their own (`apps/server/src/testing/transfers.ts`), and `scripts/guards/api-test-overrides.test.ts` keeps `transfers` set only in `api.ts`, the server's `testing/` folder and tests, and never named in `main.ts`.
   - **What that gives.** An api that has never served the org sends at once, and a withdrawal applies at the next send. A send still skipped is audited `auth.code_requested {send: 'skipped_not_cleared'}` and logged at error (T20 item 9); from PR 2 a code the notice holds back is audited `skipped_no_notice` or `skipped_unstated` (item 27).
   - **After "Save and keep cleared".** A send that waited for it finds neither the old clearance nor, in its first read, the new one granted in the same commit. So the function reads a second time, also `FOR SHARE`, and a send never reads "not cleared" across it.
   - **The bound.** The lock orders the decision only. A withdrawal waits for a check holding the clearance, and a check that starts during one waits and then reads "not cleared". But the provider is called after that transaction commits, so a withdrawal committing in that moment doesn't stop the one send already decided: at most its starts, each of 4 s a try with the adapter's one retry (T20 item 10), and one more start after a foreign verification (T20 item 2), all for that one code request. And only starting a new code is gated: after a withdrawal commits, checks and cancels of verifications already open go on reaching Twilio for up to a code's lifetime (5 minutes).
   - **From PR 2, the notice must state the provider** (built: item 27). A provider counts as cleared for sends only while it has an open clearance and the latest version is a stand-in or states that clearance's seven notice facts (PD-41), so a "Save and keep cleared" that changes a stated fact suspends its sends until a new version is published. Before any version, `transfer_cleared()` keeps clearance alone. Sign-in and re-authentication codes ask `transfer_send_state()` instead, and go to the DPO alone before any version and while the published one doesn't state the clearance (PD-59), so OD-04's Twilio transfer before v0 is the DPO's own number.
   - **Who else.** C0.14's push sender and C2.2's LiveKit joins call it the same way, in the transaction that decides each send, under the same notice rule. Something that only shows whether a provider is cleared (C0.13's RUM capability) reads it plainly, with no lock, in its own read-only transaction: `transfer_cleared()` takes row locks, which a read-only transaction refuses, and is only for deciding a send.
9. **The worker's copy** (PD-16).
   - **Where it's read.** In `FlagCache`'s own per-org read, as `hearth_queue`, in the same read-only transaction: purpose versions, each provider's review date and revision, open clearances and live restrictions. From PR 2 it also reads, in one statement, the latest notice version (its id and whether Hearth generated it), the purposes it lists at which register version, and whether it states each open clearance (`notice_provider_states()`, so the worker never reads a clearance's facts or the version's providers; items 27 and 33). Only the worker's cache keeps it; the api and realtime keep none (items 8 and 11).
   - **Shared fate.** One `confirmedAt` covers flags and registers, and the 8 s freshness, the 2 s statement bound and the three reads in flight are the flags', so T18 item 8's pool counts stand. A register read that fails fails the whole read, and the org goes unconfirmed: no claims, the watchdog, `/ready`.
   - **Propagation.** There is no channel: a change, the bootstrap's included, reaches the worker within one poll (2 s ±10%). A digest of the purpose versions, the providers' revisions, the open clearances and the live restrictions tells a change, which logs `register_changed` at info and calls no flag listener; from PR 2 it also holds the latest notice version and whether each open clearance is stated, so a publish logs it too. So C0.12's ACL list and `scripts/check-server.sh` are unchanged.
   - **The gate.** `registerGate()` (`apps/server/src/compliance/gate.ts`) over that copy is the worker's default (`worker.gate`), a plain object of closures, so a test's spread copy keeps every check. Tests that need no register use `coreOnlyGate`, a gate over a register never read (`apps/server/src/testing/gates.ts`).
10. **Runner and consumer outcomes** (PD-18; T15 item 10).
    - **Unchanged code.** A gate refusal still cancels a job (`purpose_refused`, `provider_not_cleared`, `processing_not_allowed`), `over_cap` waits 5 minutes, and a consumer's refused round keeps its place.
    - **Why that is safe (G3).** `validateRegistries()` refuses a job kind whose work waits while its flag is off (`onFlagOff` `requeue`, or unset under an essential flag) unless it serves a pinned purpose. It also refuses a kind that names a provider when its work waits or its flag is essential or locked on, whatever its `onFlagOff`. So a stale register or a withdrawn clearance can cancel only optional work, and cancelling optional work is T15 item 9's rule.
    - **Who must change it.** The first step that needs otherwise (C0.14's push if it is essential, C2.2's LiveKit handler, A1) makes claims skip refused waiting kinds, requeues an essential refusal and reloads before deciding, before G3 can go.
    - `cancelled` covers more than migration 0007's comment says.
11. **The interlock** (PD-18, PD-19; T17 item 9).
    - **Reaching someone new.** `POST /api/admin/flags/set` reads the flag's row first. A change that lets the feature reach anyone it doesn't reach now reads the registers in the same transaction and answers 409 `purpose_refused` when any of the key's own purposes isn't allowed (item 6). That is turning it on, or, while it is on, going from a cohort to the whole org, or naming a person or role the cohort didn't. Turning a flag off, narrowing it and a change that reaches nobody new are never refused. A change that leaves the flag on, judged to reach nobody new on a row another change has since moved, answers 409 `try_again`; one that leaves it off reaches nobody whatever the row holds, so it never does.
    - **The other way round.** No register or clearance change is ever refused because a flag is on: a flag never gates a privacy control.
    - **Why no more is needed yet.** G1: every flag outside `ai.*` names only pinned purposes, so `verdict()` and `GET /api/flags` can't show "on" for a refused purpose. G2: no flag outside `ai.*` has a flag under it, so a flag's own purposes are the whole check. Both are tests in `packages/shared/src/catalogues.test.ts` whose failures name the work they defer. Work in flight stops at its next admission whatever the flags say. The step that adds the first flag naming an unpinned purpose (C1.3's presence) makes the served value ask the gate, and the step that adds a flag under one makes the route check its descendants.
12. **No fence for register changes** (PD-17; T17 item 8). Running work sees a change at its next `step()`, `read()` or `commit()`, within about 2.2 s. No C0.9 PR 1 work can be refused. C0.14 decides a fence for the first provider-naming work.
13. **Writes, refusals, events and the lock order** (PD-21, PD-22, PD-24, PD-25).
    - **The functions.** The registers change only through `set_register_purpose()`, `record_transfer_entry()`, `clear_transfer_entry()` and `withdraw_transfer_clearance()`, after `can()`, plus the bootstrap (item 16). No role holds INSERT, UPDATE or DELETE on the registers' tables. Each function re-reads the person and their `dpo` role `FOR SHARE`, locks its entry, refuses a stale edit (HF005, 409 `register_changed`) or a rule (HF006, 400 `register_invalid`), writes nothing for a no-op, and writes the audit event (ADM-06: `register.purpose_set`, `register.transfer_recorded`, `register.transfer_cleared` or `register.transfer_withdrawn`). A withdrawal names no revision, so it is never refused as stale.
    - **The routes.** `GET /api/admin/registers` (read only) and `POST /api/admin/registers/purposes/set`, `/transfers/record`, `/transfers/clear` and `/transfers/withdraw` (`apps/server/src/compliance/routes.ts`). Besides HF005 and HF006, they answer a fact's shape a register's own table refuses (23514 on a constraint named for `processing_register`, `transfer_register` or `transfer_clearances`) as 400 `register_invalid`, an unknown key (22023) as 404 `not_found` and an actor the function refuses (42501) as 403 `forbidden`; any other 23514 is a fault (500, logged). The purpose route runs the shared rules (`purposeProblems()`) after `can()`, so a problem answers 400 before the database, and the page shows the specific problems. A transfer entry's facts are stored, so whether they are complete enough to clear is the clearance trigger's to say, by `transferProblems()`'s rules in their order (RGS-I4), and the page shows those problems before it offers to clear.
    - **The events.** The route appends `register.purpose_changed` or `register.transfer_changed` (`service_operation`, ids only) in the same transaction. The `registers` suite owns them.
    - **The lock order:** … `role_assignments` → `cases` (from PR 3b, item 59) → `processing_register` → `processing_restrictions` → `transfer_register` → `transfer_clearances` → `dpo_settings` → `orgs` (publishing only) → `notice_versions` → `notice_acknowledgments` → `feature_flags` …. Sends take people (and, at sign-in, phone_numbers), from PR 2 read whether the person holds `dpo` with no lock, then take the clearance (item 27). Nothing locks the two registers together. PR 2's notice locks are item 33's, PR 3a's (the contact, a recorded acknowledgement and the re-clear) item 45's, and PR 3b's (the cases and the reply targets) item 59's.
14. **Access and re-authentication** (PD-20, PD-30).
    - **Who.** The DPO alone, through `can()`'s `register.read`, `register.purpose.set` and `register.transfer.set {record | clear | withdraw}` (PRD PDPA-01, PDPA-13). Each needs an active person and a re-authentication from the last 5 minutes, which for the DPO is the PIN; reads need it too. AI purposes and providers are refused until A0. Owners, Admins and the Compliance Officer are refused, so only the DPO withdraws a clearance (item 19).
    - **Not Rule 9.** Nothing here imports `privileged.ts`.
    - **The capability.** `GET /api/session` answers `capabilities.registers`: whether `can()` allows `register.read` once re-authenticated, so it shows without a fresh stamp (T21 item 10).
15. **The DPO pages** (OD-03; PD-26, PD-27, PD-31).
    - **Where.** `/dpo/purposes`, `/dpo/transfers` and, from PR 2, the Notice tab `/dpo/notice` (item 31), in the shell but in no navigation (T21 item 5), reached from "Data protection" in the avatar menu when the capability is true. The server refuses everyone else, and their deep link shows "This page is for the Data Protection Officer." From PR 2 both register pages also carry the notice's markers and banner (item 31).
    - **What they show.** Non-AI purposes and providers only, until A0 (PD-27, which the owner confirms on its own, item 19), one card each. A purpose's card shows its version, status, basis with what the basis takes, retention, providers and who recorded it; a provider's shows whether it is cleared, by whom and when (or at set-up), its review date, its main facts and "What checks this" (item 3). Problems (`purposeProblems()`, `transferProblems()`) hold the action, with the reason as its description; warnings sit in a polite live region and never block.
    - **Actions.** Edit opens a dialog. A purpose's offers only the statuses and bases that save (`statusesFor()`, `basesFor()`). A provider's holds Save and, on a cleared entry, "Save and keep cleared" as the main action, held while any fact is missing; a plain Save that changes a cleared entry's facts asks first (item 3). "Clear for use" and "Withdraw clearance" sit on the provider's card, act on the saved facts, and each asks first. A stale write shows `register_changed` with Reload.
    - **Re-authentication.** A refusal naming the PIN shows an inline PIN step through `POST /api/auth/reauth/pin`, then sends the refused read or write once more; until C0.16's sheet. The PIN is never the mutation's variables, so Query's cache never holds it (`pinStepOptions()`, `apps/web/src/dpo/queries.ts`). A refusal naming another method shows a sign-in-again notice, since only a new sign-in mends it (T20 item 6).
    - **Bundle.** The menu's label and the two titles live in `strings/shell.ts`. The pages' own copy (`strings/dpo.ts`, shared by both, and `dpo-purposes.ts` and `dpo-transfers.ts`, one per page) loads only with their lazy chunks, as does their model, so the boot's bundle carries none of it and each page's chunk carries only its own.
    - **Checks.** No visual baselines, since C0.16 moves the pages. Axe (in both schemes) and `noAiCopy` run at 320, 390 and 1440 px. At 320 and 390 on a phone, both pages and every dialog of the edit, clear and withdraw flows have 44 px targets and 16 px inputs, scroll nothing sideways and pass axe, and the avatar menu's items fit at 320 × 568 and 768 × 480 (DPP-E5): from PR 2 three, Settings, Data protection and Sign out (item 31).
16. **The bootstrap's database side** (OD-01; PD-23).
    - **The function.** `bootstrap_transfer_clearance()` is security invoker, with execute revoked from PUBLIC and granted to nobody, so only its owner can run it. The reviewed first-Owner bootstrap, `scripts/owner-bootstrap.ts`, calls it in its own transaction (item 66), acting as the org's Hearth principal, for `twilio` and `aws` only, with the facts, the DPO it names and a reason, all read at run time.
    - **What it writes.** The facts (Hearth as `updated_by`), each text fact and the reason trimmed as the api trims the DPO's, so text of only whitespace is missing and the trigger refuses it. A clearance (`cleared_via 'bootstrap'`, `cleared_by` that DPO), which item 3's trigger checks. An audit row with Hearth as the actor, the reason, and details `{"by": "bootstrap", "decided_by": <the DPO>, "revision": n, "was_revision": m, "changed": {"<fact>": {"from": …, "to": …}}, "clearance": <its id>}`, so facts the DPO recorded before it ran keep their history (item 3); it leaves `via` and `on_behalf_of_id` empty, which migration 0004 keeps for A4 and A5. And `register.transfer_changed`, with Hearth as the actor.
    - **Once, then only through the break-glass re-clear.** For Twilio Verify alone, once an earlier clearance has ended and none is open, while no holder of `dpo` has a live session, and only with the emergency marker set (OD-20, item 44). Any other clearance after a withdrawal is the DPO's, in the app.
    - **Who sees it.** Every process sees the clearance at its next send or poll.
17. **Copy** (PD-27, PD-31). Every string the pages show is British, never "ask" and never an AI word (`strings/ai-copy.ts`, which from PR 2 re-exports `AI_COPY` from `packages/shared/src/ai-copy.ts`, item 24; decision 8). Every label the pages can render, including the shared ones ST-02 can't see, is held to `AI_COPY` by a unit test (DPP-06). So the PIN step words `pin_reset_needed` itself, and `pin_locked` when only an Owner can unlock it (`pin.state` `admin_locked`), since the server's messages for both say "Ask".
18. **What CI checks** (PD-29).
    - **Check 6:** `PURPOSES` and `PROVIDERS` equal the defaults functions both ways (keys, `ai`, `pinned` = `PINNED_PURPOSES`, `needs_switch`, and the starting status, active exactly for `ACTIVE_FROM_START`), and every org's rows equal them, each later version keeping its version 1's fixed facts. It also makes an org in a transaction it rolls back, so the trigger on `orgs` is checked even on a database with none. From PR 2 it also holds `NOTICE_TRANSFER_FACTS` (`packages/shared/src/notice.ts`) to `notice_facts()`'s keys (item 27).
    - **Guards:** G1 and G2 in `packages/shared`, G3 in `validateRegistries()`, the transfers seam in `scripts/guards/api-test-overrides.test.ts`, and the PD ids in `scripts/guards/design-ids.test.ts`.
    - **The upgrade run.** 0009 gets no upgrade step of its own. It changes no existing row; it widens `outbox_events_type` to a superset validated in place, as its last statement, so its ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock on the outbox lasts only until the commit; and its trigger on `orgs` only adds rows. `scripts/check-revocation-upgrade.sh` applies it over the seed, an org with no principals and one outbox event of an existing type, and asserts that the event is unchanged under a validated check and that the backfill gave each org exactly the defaults' rows, since the digest compares no rows. 0010 has its own step (item 35).
    - **The seed** writes only tables 0005's schema has, so the upgrade checks can load it at that floor. A later table's rows come from its own trigger on `orgs`, its backfill or a test's fixture.
    - **Races:** the cases in `check-concurrency.ts`, for the transfer gate against a withdrawal and a "Save and keep cleared", a clearance against a change of its facts, two edits of one purpose, a DPO deactivated or losing the role mid-change, and a restriction against a basis leaving consent; from PR 2, NTR-01 to NTR-07 (item 35).
19. **Open points for the owner** (listed in the PR):
    - **PD-27, AI rows hidden from the DPO until A0.** An unticked "Before merging" confirmation of its own: it departs from BUILD-PLAN C0.9's "AI planned, DPO-only", PDPA-01's "listing every purpose" and PDPA-13's "access to the registers", reading them under decision 8, which is itself a default. The alternative: read-only AI rows for the DPO, under neutral copy, with an `AI_COPY` exception for `/dpo/*` that a guard keeps narrow.
    - **Recovery when Twilio is withdrawn and no DPO is signed in.** Nobody can then get a sign-in code, and the bootstrap clears only a provider never cleared (PD-23). The choice: widen the bootstrap to a guarded break-glass re-clear (only while no DPO has a live session, audited with a reason; T14 item 21 already makes the script the sole Owner's break-glass PIN reset), or another reviewed path. Never a direct database write. Decided by the owner on 23 Oct 2026 (OD-20): the guarded break-glass re-clear, built in PR 3a (item 44).
    - **PD-20, withdrawal.** DPO-only, or an Owner too.
    - **The DPO's readings:** PD-06's starting state; PD-10's split between consent and deemed consent; PD-11's switch list, with deemed consent by conduct refused beside PDPA-04's two and the test set exempt; the seven basis codes; the purposes PDPA-01's no-opt-out warning covers (item 2); and PD-13's review dates, which never lapse.
    - **PD-15's bound** (the starts of the one send already decided, its restart after a foreign verification included, and open verifications' checks and cancels; item 8) and **PD-17** (no register fence), to accept.
    - **PD-23, the bootstrap's audit shape:** Hearth the actor, `details.by` and `decided_by` naming the DPO, and the clearance's `cleared_by` the DPO.
    - **The bootstrap's timing.** Before C0a's non-prod exit, with Twilio's non-prod secret and egress (C0.12) moved into C0a, or C0a's exit rows 2, 4 and 7 read as shown by local e2e. The script supports `nonprod`; it runs there once C0.11 and C0.12 give non-prod its break-glass role, the bootstrap's login, the environment marker and the parameter group (item 71, C0.9 OD-31).
    - **Twilio's written facts.** Its retention and storage countries in writing now come before its first clearance, which moves part of C0.19's checklist (T20 item 11) earlier.
    - **The doc edits:** the PRD's PDPA-10 provider list and PDPA-01's note, and BUILD-PLAN's timing for the push services' entries (before C0.15, not the C1 pilot).
    - **PD-30.** The PIN every 5 minutes, even to read.
20. **What PR 2, PR 3 and the bootstrap PR inherit.** The later steps' hand-offs are in their BUILD-PLAN rows (C0.12, C0.13, C0.14, C0.15, C0.16, C0.19, C2.2, C2.11 and A0.1); C0.10 recorded the clean-up's exemption in the retention registry, which the DPO confirmed (T23 item 15, LH-48), and not in `service_operation`'s basis reference, which exists only for three bases and which the notice prints; and C1.3's first flag naming an unpinned purpose trips G1.
    - **PR 2 (0010):**
      - fill `RegisterState.notified` from the highest published version's purposes, in the worker's read and the flags route, and rewrite PGT-04; the predicates don't change (built: item 27);
      - record each published version's purposes by `processing_register` version id (a new table, such as `notice_version_purposes`) (built: item 23);
      - publishing refuses while an active purpose lacks a basis or retention (item 4's four included) or a listed provider isn't cleared, and applies PDPA-04's refusal (built: item 25);
      - a provider counts as cleared for sends only while the latest published notice lists it with its open clearance's facts (item 8) (built: item 27);
      - a stale or unloaded register skips or rolls back a consumer's whole round, never withholds (item 7) (built: item 32);
      - the pre-v0 exemption (OD-01) covers, for a DPO-capable session only, `/api/admin/registers*`, `/api/auth/reauth/pin`, `GET /api/session`, `GET /api/flags` and sign-out, and the gateway admits nobody while no version exists (built: item 29, which departs from it: PD-44);
      - `notice.*` become emitted events carrying `accounts`, and consent items extend PD-10's `consent` branch (built: item 33; consent items wait for C1.3, item 25);
      - a "notice out of date" banner after a `register.*` event, and a decision on whether withdrawing a clearance prompts a new version (built: items 25 and 31; nothing publishes by itself, PD-39);
      - OD-04 becomes the notice's copy (built: item 24).
    - **PR 3a (0011):** the runbooks, "Twilio withdrawn and no DPO signed in" included (built: item 46).
    - **PR 3b (0012): built.** A purpose key for handling requests, `request_handling` (a new defaults function, and rows for existing orgs: item 51), and a foreign key from `processing_restrictions.case_id` (item 52).
    - **The bootstrap PR:** in one transaction, as `hearth_owner`, it creates the org (whose triggers give it flags and registers), its Hearth principal and the first Owner and DPO; sets `app.org_id` and `app.user_id` (the Hearth principal) with `set_config(…, true)`; and calls `bootstrap_transfer_clearance()` for `twilio` and `aws` with facts and a reason read at run time. It needs the "Before merging" read, and BSC-02 is its contract. It lands before C0a's non-prod exit if that exit needs real sign-in (item 19). (Built: items 63 to 71.)
21. **Scope of PR 2 (OD-02; PD-32).** PR 2 adds:
    - the notice generator, `publish_notice()` and `acknowledge_notice()`;
    - the version links, "notified" by register version, and the provider-notice rule with its send state;
    - the A-12 round rule;
    - the acknowledgement gate on REST and on sockets, with OD-01's exemption;
    - `/notice`, `/settings` and `/settings/privacy`, and `/dpo/notice`;
    - `notice.*` as emitted events;
    - OD-04's copy.

    It leaves out the DPO contact, the form, cases and runbooks (PR 3a with 0011, and PR 3b with 0012), consent items (C1.3, PD-38), acknowledgements recorded by the DPO (PR 3a, PD-42), exporting the records (C0.16, PD-57), the first-run wrapper (C0.15) and notice v1 (C1.13).
22. **What a version is (PD-33).**
    - **The words.** `body` is the exact text people acknowledge, in a fixed line format: `## ` a section, `### ` an entry, `- ` an item, any other line a paragraph, with blank lines between blocks. It holds no version number and no date; the screens show both from the row, and render the body as React text, never HTML (WS-07). The DPO's text and the org's name are flattened to one line and never start one, so stored text can't forge a heading or an item.
    - **The generator.** `generator` names the code that wrote it (`NOTICE_GENERATOR`). It is null on a stand-in (the seed's v0, the DB fixtures', the integration harness's), which only ever comes before the first generated version (`notice_versions_generated()`). Production never gets a stand-in.
    - **Never changed.** A trigger refuses an UPDATE of a version or of either link table for every writer, the superuser included (`notice_unchanged()`). No role holds INSERT, UPDATE or DELETE on any of them.
    - **Numbering** continues from the latest version: production's first generated version is v0, and the seed's org gets v1.
23. **What a version lists, and its links (PD-34, PD-35, PD-58, PD-60).**
    - **What it lists.** Every active non-AI purpose at its current version; every retired non-AI purpose, in a section of its own (PD-60); and every non-AI provider with an open clearance, whether or not a purpose lists it, so a cleared Twilio is always named. Never a planned or AI purpose.
    - **The links.** `notice_version_purposes` holds each listed purpose's key and `register_version`, with a foreign key to `processing_register`, so its status at that version is the linked row's. `notice_version_providers` holds each open clearance's id, with a foreign key to `transfer_clearances`, whose provider and facts are the clearance's own snapshot, so 0009's tables don't change. Only `publish_notice()` writes them, from the rows it locked, never from the caller's arrays.
    - **The sections, in order:**
      - *About this notice*: what it is, and D28's sentence: acknowledging records that you were told, and isn't consent to any purpose;
      - *Why your data is processed*: one entry per active purpose, with its legal basis, how long its data is kept and who receives it;
      - *No longer in use* (PD-60): one entry per retired purpose, with one item, "Data already collected is kept for: <retention>.", and its basis not stated; left out when there are none;
      - *If you withdraw consent*: which purposes rest on deemed consent by conduct and stop if it is withdrawn, and that the rest continue (item 42); with none, that they all continue; both only of the purposes under *Why your data is processed*, since a retired purpose continues for nobody;
      - *What Hearth doesn't do* (PD-58, below);
      - *Who else receives your data*: one entry per cleared provider, saying what it does, what it receives, where it processes and stores the data, how long it keeps it, who it passes it to, and whether it trains models on it;
      - *Your first sign-in code* (OD-04): only when Twilio is listed, naming Twilio Verify's countries;
      - *Access to production systems* (PRD-101): the separate emergency role, each use logged and every use reviewed by a second person each quarter;
      - *Your rights*: what people may request, and the DPO's contact (item 40).
    - **What Hearth doesn't do (PD-58).** Built from the register being published, never a fixed sentence. `DOES_NOT` (`packages/shared/src/notice-text.ts`) holds three clauses, each with the purposes that would make it false:
      - "record calls, meetings or drop-in rooms": `recording_meetings`, `recording_drop_in`;
      - "transcribe anything": `transcription`, `voice_message_transcription`, `transcription_test_set`;
      - "summarise what you write or draft text from it": `meeting_notes`, `org_brain`, `ai_writing`, `people_inference`, `assistant`, `agents`.

      A clause is left out once any of its purposes is active, and the section once none is left. Each clause left is an item ("Hearth doesn't transcribe anything."), followed by "None of these happens unless a new version of this notice says so and you have acknowledged it." So no clause contradicts a purpose the same notice lists, and the sentence stays true beside a retired entry. Every purpose is in exactly one clause or in `DOES_NOT_EXEMPT`, with its reason (NTG-10), so a new purpose forces a decision; sensitive-data detection is exempt, being listed as a purpose once active (PDPA-17). Until A0 the last two clauses' purposes are all AI and never active, so both always print: decision 5's line, written without the word "AI" (decision 8).
24. **The generator and its copy (PD-36, PD-53, PD-62).**
    - **Where it runs.** `generateNotice()` is pure and deterministic. It runs on the server, and on the web only in tests. Country names come from its own fixed table, `NOTICE_COUNTRY_NAMES`, falling back to the code, never from the runtime's ICU, so a Node upgrade renames no country. The table holds every code ICU 78.3 (CLDR 48) named in en-GB when `c09.1` was written, as it named them; a change to it is a change to the output.
    - **Its id.** `NOTICE_GENERATOR` (`c09.1` in PR 2, `c09.2` from PR 3a) changes with any change to the output, which NTG pins with a digest.
    - **Preview is publish.** The DPO's preview is the server's own draft. A sha-256 digest of its body, generator id and sources ties the preview to the publish (item 25).
    - **The copy.** British and short; never "ask", "I agree" or an AI word; the verb is "acknowledge". NTW-10 holds every branch of the generator and every copy constant to `AI_COPY`, which now lives in `packages/shared/src/ai-copy.ts`; `apps/web/src/strings/ai-copy.ts` re-exports it, so ST-02 and the e2e `noAiCopy` check are unchanged. Each basis has its own wording: employment, the employment exception, with no consent needed; legitimate interests, PDPA-02's statement with the assessment's reference and "contact the Data Protection Officer to learn more"; business improvement, data already held; another exception, the provision cited; and, from PR 3a, deemed consent by conduct, that you give the data yourself for the purpose and may withdraw it (item 42).
    - **The DPO's free text (PD-62).** Publishing refuses a draft whose basis reference, retention or a provider's stated text facts, or from PR 3a the DPO's contact's title, email address or postal address (PD-65, item 39), match `AI_COPY` (`free_text_ai_copy`). The problem names the purpose or provider and the field, never the matched word, so the Notice tab stays clean under `noAiCopy`. Only the publish route checks it: the route always regenerates the draft and is `publish_notice()`'s only caller (a guard in `scripts/guards/api-test-overrides.test.ts` keeps it so), and `AI_COPY` is code that changes with the copy, which SQL doesn't repeat. The org's name isn't checked at publish, since the DPO can't change it: a unit test holds the seed's name to `AI_COPY` (`apps/server/src/compliance/notice.test.ts`), and the Owner bootstrap script refuses a matching name, its NFKC twin and hidden characters through `orgNameProblems()` (item 64), as C0.16's org settings will (item 37). The generator still prints "your organisation" for a name that matches (NTG-13), so a name `AI_COPY` later grows to match is covered too.
    - **For the DPO to confirm:** OD-04's paragraph, the production-access paragraph, the three "doesn't do" clauses and every basis wording, and from PR 3a *Your rights* with the contact (PD-74) and the deemed-consent wording and its withdraw branch (PD-73).
25. **Publishing (PD-37, PD-38, PD-39).**
    - **The routes.** `GET /api/admin/notice` (`notice.read`, read only) answers the draft with its problems, warnings, changes, digest and `upToDate`; the latest version, with how many people have acknowledged it (a count, never names); and up to 100 versions of history. `POST /api/admin/notice/publish {seen, digest}` (`notice.publish`, the DPO with a fresh PIN) reads and regenerates the draft in the same transaction, then refuses, in this order: a `seen` that isn't the latest version's number (null while none exists), 409 `notice_changed`; any problem, 400 `notice_invalid`; a digest other than the draft's, 409 `notice_changed`. Then it calls `publish_notice()`, appends `notice.published` and answers `{notice, version}`.
    - **The function.** `publish_notice()`:
      - re-checks the DPO (`notice_check_dpo()`: the person, then their `dpo` role, `FOR SHARE`);
      - locks every non-AI purpose's latest version `FOR SHARE`, again until the row held is still the latest, and every non-AI transfer entry `FOR SHARE`, so no purpose changes and no clearance starts or ends while it runs (from PR 3a it then locks the DPO's latest contact too, item 40);
      - locks the org's own `orgs` row, then the latest version, `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, so two publishes, the first two included, take turns;
      - refuses a `seen` that isn't the latest version, or sources other than those it locked (HF007);
      - applies the rules (HF008);
      - inserts the version, numbered after the latest, and the links from the locked rows;
      - writes `notice.publish`, with ids and numbers only.
    - **The rules, which block,** in this order: an active purpose has a basis and a retention (item 4's four included); no active purpose rests on a consent-type basis the notice can't state (consent until C1.3's consent items, deemed consent by notification until C2.11's opt-out windows: PD-38; deemed consent by conduct is stated from PR 3a, OD-18, item 42), and, at the route, a purpose on deemed consent by conduct is one `CONDUCT_WITHDRAWABLE` lists (`purpose_conduct_unstoppable`, item 42); every provider an active purpose lists has an open clearance; the DPO's contact is recorded (item 40); the DPO's free text holds no AI wording (the route alone, item 24); the body is there and at most 100,000 characters, and the generator id is well formed; and a draft identical to the latest version (the same words, generator and sources) isn't published again. A retired purpose is listed, but no rule applies to it: 0009 already requires its basis and retention.
    - **Warnings, which never block:** a cleared provider no active purpose lists; a passed review date; `sends_waiting`, a cleared provider whose stated facts the latest generated version doesn't state; `same_text`, the words unchanged but the sources moved, so everyone would acknowledge the same words again; and `production_access_pending`, until C0.19 (PD-57).
    - **Two first publishes at once.** The second waits on the org's row, then finds the first's version and gets HF007 (409 `notice_changed`), never a 23514 or a 500.
    - **Nothing publishes by itself (PD-39).** A register change or a withdrawn clearance never creates or forces a version, and every version is acknowledged again by everyone. The DPO's pages say the notice is out of date and what changed (item 31). Meanwhile the gates hold: a changed purpose is no longer notified, and a withdrawn clearance stops sends at once (item 27).
26. **Acknowledging (PD-42).**
    - **The routes.** `GET /api/notice` (`notice.read_own`, read only, `touch: false`, since `/notice` polls it) answers whose answer it is (`person`), the standing, the current version, what changed since the last generated version you acknowledged before it (null when there is none, or the current version is a stand-in), and up to 50 of your own acknowledgements, newest version first. It is registered through `keepCookie`, as `/api/session` and `/api/flags` are, so a poll's 401 never clears a newer session's cookie, and the runtime ends the document when `person` isn't the session's. `POST /api/notice/acknowledge {notice}` (`notice.acknowledge_own`) calls `acknowledge_notice()` with the session's own device, appends `notice.acknowledged` when something changed, and answers `{version, changed}`.
    - **The function.** `acknowledge_notice()`:
      - re-reads the person (still at the org) and the session's own device (theirs, not signed out) `FOR SHARE`, never a device the request names;
      - refuses an id that isn't one of the org's versions (22023);
      - takes the latest version `FOR SHARE`, again until it is still the latest, and refuses any other version (HF007);
      - writes `in_app` once (`on conflict do nothing`), with one audit row, `notice.acknowledge`; a second call writes nothing and answers `changed: false`.
    - **No direct writes.** `hearth_app` no longer holds INSERT on either notice table (0003's grants are revoked). From PR 3a the DPO records one through `record_notice_acknowledgement()` (item 43). There are two writers of acknowledgements, and both admit only the latest version under their own lock, so there is still no acknowledgement trigger: 070's superuser fixtures acknowledge older versions, and a data-only restore needs nothing turned off.
27. **"Notified" and the provider-notice rule (PD-40, PD-41, PD-59, PD-61; A-01).**
    - **"Notified."** A non-pinned purpose is notified only while it is active and the latest version links it at its current register version, so any edit after publishing stops it until the next publish (PGT-12). Pinned purposes are unaffected (PD-07), and `purposeAllowed()` doesn't change.
    - **The stated facts.** Seven: services, data types, processing countries, storage countries, training, the provider's retention and onward recipients (`notice_facts()`, which `NOTICE_TRANSFER_FACTS` mirrors and check 6 compares). No role may run `notice_facts()`: only definer functions owned by `hearth_owner` call it.
    - **The send state.** `transfer_send_state(provider)` reads the open clearance `FOR SHARE`, a second time when the first read finds none, as PR 1's `transfer_cleared()` did (item 8), then the latest version with no lock, and answers:
      - `not_cleared`: no open clearance;
      - `no_notice`: an open clearance, and no version at all;
      - `cleared`: an open clearance, and the latest version is a stand-in or links a clearance of this provider whose notice facts equal the open one's;
      - `unstated`: an open clearance that the latest generated version doesn't state.
    - **Who gets a code** (`codeMaySend()`, `apps/server/src/compliance/transfers.ts`; PD-59). On `cleared` everyone; on `no_notice` or `unstated` a holder of `dpo` alone; on `not_cleared` nobody.
      - Sign-in reads whether the person holds `dpo` after `stillHere()` has read their row `FOR SHARE`, with no lock on `role_assignments` (`holdsRoleNow()`, `apps/server/src/access/world.ts`, which lets `packages/access` decide). `hearth_app` can't lock that table, and a `dpo` grant, like every PIN-role grant, takes the person's row `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, so the person's row is where the two meet. Re-authentication reads it from `actingBy()`'s World.
      - A code held back is audited `skipped_no_notice` or `skipped_unstated`, beside `skipped_not_cleared`, and sign-in's reply is the same as for any other number. `no_notice` is logged `sms_provider_no_notice` at info, since it is expected before v0, and `unstated` `sms_provider_unstated` at error: sign-in codes have stopped for everyone but the DPO until a new notice is published.
      - A DPO re-authenticates with the PIN, so a code re-authentication in either state is skipped and answers 503. A request held back spends none of the person's day of code requests (T20 item 6).
    - **Why.** Before v0 only the DPO can use Hearth (OD-01), so nobody else's number goes to Twilio for nothing. After a stated Twilio fact changes, the DPO can still sign in and publish, so the change can't lock the org out.
    - **`transfer_cleared(provider)`** keeps its signature and grants, and answers true for `cleared` and `no_notice`: PR 1's meaning before v0, for every other sender (C0.14's push, C2.2's LiveKit). The worker's `providerCleared()` agrees: an open clearance, and no version, a stand-in as the latest, or the clearance stated (`notice_provider_states()`, item 9). No job names a provider yet (G3).
    - **What stops sends.** Re-clearing on the same facts, or changing only a review date, a safeguard or a confirmation, keeps them going. Changing a stated fact stops them, the DPO's codes aside, until the next publish.
    - **The bound.** A version that commits during a send lists every clearance open when it was made, the one the send holds `FOR SHARE` among them, so either answer agrees with a version the send could see. A change of facts waits for the send (PD-15).
    - **Stand-ins.** Production never gets one, so there the rule applies from v0. The seed's stand-in v0 keeps e2e sign-in working for every persona.
    - **Pinned purposes after publishing (PD-61).** A pinned purpose never stops, so once its entry changes after a publish, the published notice is out of date for everyone until the next one. `readRegisterState()` marks it `stale` when the latest version is generated and doesn't link it at its current version; with no version, or a stand-in as the latest, nothing is stale. The DPO's pages say so (item 31), and the worker logs `notice_pinned_out_of_date {org, purposes}` at warn, at most once a UTC day per org while any are stale and at once again when the set changes, until a new notice is published (PGT-16).
28. **The acknowledgement gate on REST (PD-43, PD-56).**
    - **The rule.** `noticeAdmits(world, me, use, standing)` (`packages/access/src/notice.ts`) decides, from one plain read of the latest version and whether the person has acknowledged it (`noticeStandings()`, `apps/server/src/compliance/notice-standing.ts`): `unpublished` while no version exists, else `acknowledged` or `required`. The read takes no lock and calls no function, runs in the request's own transaction, and is skipped for the exempt actions.
    - **Where it is asked.** In `consider()`, after the World loads and before `can()` and any target lock, for the request's action (`requireNotice()`, `apps/server/src/access/world.ts`); and in `actingBy()` with the use `'reauth'`, before the method guard, so an unacknowledged session can't make the provider send a code. A refusal is 403 `notice_required`. Only `access/world.ts` and `auth/account.ts` call `actingSession()` (NTS-05).
    - **Exempt in every standing** (`NOTICE_FREE`): `session.read_own` (both shell GETs), `devices.list_own`, `devices.sign_out_own`, `notice.read_own` and `notice.acknowledge_own`. None reaches anyone else's data, and a lost phone can always be signed out.
    - **The bound.** The read is plain, at READ COMMITTED: a request decided before a publish commits finishes within its 30 s, and the next one is refused.
    - **Never a flag.** The gate is a rule, and no flag reads it.
    - **The capability.** `capabilities.registers` stays `decide()`'s, which applies no gate, so the DPO keeps the register pages before v0: `/notice` offers their links and the shell's guard lets `/dpo/*` through. `GET /api/session` answers the standing as `notice` (T21 item 10).
    - **Background work (PD-56)** isn't gated per person by acknowledgement until A0.2 and A2.2 exclude by it. Purposes are gated per org, by "notified".
29. **Before the first version (OD-01; PD-44).**
    - **The setup actions** (`NOTICE_SETUP`). While no version exists, a holder of `dpo` also passes `register.read`, `register.purpose.set`, `register.transfer.set`, `notice.read`, `notice.publish`, from PR 3a `notice.contact.set` (item 41), and PIN re-authentication. Everyone else gets only the exempt actions. Once a version exists, the DPO acknowledges it like everyone else.
    - **A stated departure from item 20.** The exempt actions are open to every session, not only a DPO's: a boot answer other than 200 or 401 reads as "Can't reach Hearth", `/notice` must load, and sign-out must always work.
    - **The gateway** admits nobody, the DPO included (item 30).
    - **Sends.** Only the DPO gets a sign-in code (item 27), so OD-04's transfer before v0 is the DPO's own number. From v0, everyone's first code goes before they can read the notice, which v0's "Your first sign-in code" section states.
30. **The gateway's admission (PD-45; T19 items 8 and 10).**
    - **The rule.** `admitsSocket()`, acknowledged only, over the same read as REST, asked inside the gateway's read-only transactions as the org's system principal (`NOTICE_ADMISSION`, `apps/server/src/realtime/admission.ts`). So while no version exists the gateway admits nobody.
    - **Always wired.** `registerRealtime` uses `NOTICE_ADMISSION` unless `RealtimeDeps.admissionForTests` says otherwise. A test may set another hook, or `'none'`, which turns admission off and queries nothing (C0.7's behaviour). The integration harness's stack and realtime apps (`startStack()`, `RealtimeOptions` in `apps/server/src/testing/stack.ts`) default to `'none'`, so the C0.7 gateway suite and every other suite keep their timing; the notice suite asks for `admissionForTests: 'notice'`, which leaves `registerRealtime`'s unset and so runs production's hook (NTI-02, NTI-07), and NTS-04 proves that unset means on. `scripts/guards/api-test-overrides.test.ts` lets only `realtime/index.ts` (which defines it), the server's `testing/` folder and tests name `admissionForTests`; `main.ts` never does.
    - **When it applies.** At accept, in each subscribe batch and in each periodic check: 4403 `notice_required` within one check (at most 25 s) of a new version, and nothing pushed.
    - **Fail closed.** A failed read grants nothing and closes nothing; T19's lapse bound applies.
    - **No new channel**, so C0.12's ACL list and `scripts/check-server.sh` are unchanged.
31. **The web (PD-46, PD-47, PD-48).**
    - **`/notice`** sits outside the shell. No Skip; Sign out is offered. A checkbox, "I've read the privacy notice, version N.", then Acknowledge; a callout says what changed since the version you last acknowledged. Before any version, the DPO sees "Publish the privacy notice", with links to the Notice tab, from PR 3a the contact page (item 41), and the purposes page, and everyone else "Hearth isn't open yet".
    - **The gateway** opens a socket only once the session answer says `acknowledged`, and `resume()` resumes only a notice pause, never a 4426's (GW-25). A document that isn't acknowledged starts paused for the notice (`startPaused()`, GW-26): no socket, and the paused probe of `GET /api/flags`, at a random point in the first 30 s and every 30 s after, so a sign-out from another device or a deactivation is heard within about 30 s on `/notice` and on the DPO's pages before v0 alike, with no REST request needed.
    - **Signals.** A 403 `notice_required` from any query or mutation, or a 4403, is a hint, never retried: the tab goes to `/notice`, whose `GET /api/notice` decides. The shell's guard sends a document there while its standing doesn't allow the path (`noticeAllowsPath`: every path once acknowledged, and `/dpo/*` for the DPO before any version), keeping the path to return to. An unreadable standing reads as `required`. Each answer that states the standing (`GET /api/notice`'s or the session's) carries the mark its request was sent at (`session/notice.ts`, `mark()`): one saying `acknowledged` whose request was sent before the last move to `required` changes nothing, since it may speak of a version since replaced, and any other whose request was sent before the last acknowledgement here changes nothing either; a session answer saying `required` resets the cached notice answer, as a hint does, or, while the standing is `required` already, fetches it again in place, cancelling a request already in flight, which may have read the older version, so `/notice` moves to a newer version without dropping what someone is reading.
    - **Other tabs.** Same-browser tabs hear `{t:'notice'}` on the identity channel, which carries no data, and older tabs ignore it. Other devices learn from `/notice`'s poll of `GET /api/notice` every 30 s while visible, and on focus. The probe stays sign-out only (GW-23).
    - **The return path** stays in the runtime's memory, never in storage (WS-01). The notice store (`session/notice.ts`) reads no flag (WS-11).
    - **Settings.** `/settings` lists the registry's sections (`settings/sections.ts`), starting with Privacy; `/settings/privacy` shows, from PR 3a, the DPO's contact first (item 41), then the current notice and your own acknowledgements, with their version, time and method, and for one the DPO recorded its day and note (item 43) (PDPA-02's "always available in Settings"). The avatar menu is Settings, Data protection (when shown), Sign out.
    - **The DPO's Notice tab** (`/dpo/notice`, titled "Publish the notice", so the move to `/notice`'s "Privacy notice" after publishing and back reads as one) shows whether the notice is up to date and what would change, how many people have acknowledged the latest version (a count, never names), the problems, grouped under the purpose or provider each names, or from PR 3a under "Your contact details", and followed by a link to each register page, or the contact page (item 40), that fixes them, the warnings, the preview, Publish behind a confirmation and the PIN, and the history; from PR 3a it also offers "Record an acknowledgement" beside the count (item 43). The confirmation says: "Everyone, you included, acknowledges version N before using Hearth again. People using Hearth now are taken to it within half a minute." Once it lands, the DPO is taken to `/notice` at once, whose "Version N · published <date>" under its focused heading is the confirmation (no toast: the shell's would go before anyone saw it).
    - **The register pages.** From PR 3a their links are Purposes, Transfers, Contact (`/dpo/contact`, item 41) and Notice, and the `none` banner says to record the contact as well as each active purpose's basis and retention. The registers' answer gains each purpose's `notified` and `stale`, each provider's `sendsWait`, and `notice` (`up_to_date`, `out_of_date` or `none`: none before any version, up to date while the latest version says what the draft would, with the same words, purposes at the same register versions and each provider's stated facts (so a provider cleared again on the same facts, a review date alone, only warns `same_text`), out of date otherwise, a stand-in as the latest always). While the notice isn't published or is out of date, the Notice link carries a badge saying so ("Not published", "Out of date") and the pages show a banner.
