A purpose card says "Features that serve it stay off until a published notice lists this version." when the purpose is active, unpinned and not notified, and a stale pinned purpose's card that the published notice states an older version, which applies to everyone already; a provider card says that nothing is sent to it until a new notice is published when `sendsWait` is true (Twilio Verify's: only the DPO gets sign-in codes until then). The dialogs, once a generated version is published: saving a change that stops a notified unpinned purpose notes that its features stay off until the next publish; the record dialog warns before saving a stated fact of a provider the latest generated version names; and saving a pinned purpose first confirms "This applies to everyone at once. Publish a new notice afterwards so it says so." For a stale pinned purpose (item 27), while `notice` isn't `none`, the banner reads "The published notice no longer matches how these are handled, and everyone is affected now. Publish a new notice.", naming each stale purpose below it. The pages infer whether the latest version is generated from the answer itself: a pinned purpose, always active, is notified or stale exactly when it is.
    - **Bundle.** `/notice`, Settings and the Notice tab are lazy chunks. The banner, badge, card markers and their copy live in `dpo/notice-markers.tsx` and `strings/dpo-notice.ts`, which only the screens that show them import; `RegisterLinks` gains only the plain "Notice" link, its badge passed in by the screen. The shared `RegisterPage` chunk is measured against the 9,100-byte budget, and any raise is a reviewed change that says why (T21 item 12). PR 2 raised none: its build measured 152,887 bytes of initial JS (gzip) and 6,868 for the largest lazy chunk, `RegisterPage`'s, which `strings/dpo-notice.ts` joins because the Notice tab shares `RegisterLinks` with both register screens.
    - **Baselines.** "Settings" changes VS-01's six `avatar-menu` shots, which a person adopts from CI's artifact before merging. The new screens get none until C0.15.
32. **A stale register never withholds (PD-51; item 7).**
    - **Consumers.** When a subject's switch refuses an event and `purposeAllowed()` no longer allows the consumer's purpose, the round throws `RoundRefused`. The round rolls back, keeps its checkpoint, logs `outbox_round_refused {reason: 'register_unconfirmed'}` at info and answers `refused`; the next round's admission pauses the consumer until the purpose is allowed again. A refusal under an allowed purpose still withholds (PGT-15).
    - **Unchanged:** the `Gate` shape (A-17), `admit()` and the jobs, which G3 keeps safe.
33. **Writes, refusals, events and the lock order (PD-50, PD-52).**
    - **Writers.** `publish_notice()`, `acknowledge_notice()` and, from PR 3a, `record_notice_acknowledgement()` are the notice tables' only writers, called after `can()` allows `notice.publish`, `notice.acknowledge_own` or `notice.acknowledgement.record`, and from PR 3a `set_dpo_contact()` is the contact's, after `notice.contact.set` (items 39 and 43); each re-checks its actor in tables and writes its audit row (ADM-06). `notice_facts()` reads only its argument.
    - **Definer functions that take no acting user.** `transfer_send_state()` and `notice_provider_states()` skip `can()`: they answer only about the org's own registers and write nothing. `notice_provider_states()` answers, for the org the server set (none: no rows), each open non-AI clearance's provider and id and whether the latest version, whatever wrote it, links a clearance of that provider with equal notice facts (`stated`, false while no version exists), with no lock and never a clearance's facts. Whether a stand-in or no version makes that moot is the reader's to decide (item 27's `providerCleared()`), as `transfer_send_state()` decides it for a send. Coverage exempts it as a read, and `transfer_send_state()` beside `transfer_cleared()` as a check.
    - **Grants.** `hearth_app` loses INSERT on both notice tables and keeps SELECT on them (the standing read and the gateway's admission use it), reads both link tables, and runs `publish_notice()`, `acknowledge_notice()`, `transfer_send_state()` and `notice_provider_states()`; from PR 3a it also reads `dpo_settings` and runs `set_dpo_contact()`, `record_notice_acknowledgement()` and `publish_notice()`'s new signature (item 45). `hearth_queue` reads the latest version's `org_id`, `id`, `version` and `generator` and the purpose links, and runs `notice_provider_states()`: never a version's text, who published it, anyone's acknowledgement, the provider links or a clearance's facts. No role may run `notice_facts()` or `notice_check_dpo()`.
    - **Refusals.** HF007 is 409 `notice_changed`: a newer version, the registers moved since the draft, two publishes at once, or an acknowledgement of a version that is no longer the latest. HF008 is 400 `notice_invalid`: a rule refused publishing. 42501 is 403 (to publish) or 401 `signed_out` (to acknowledge); 22023 is 404. The SQLSTATEs are in `apps/server/src/db/sqlstate.ts`, and the three codes, with `notice_required`, in `packages/shared/src/api-errors.ts`, none of them saying "ask".
    - **Events.** `notice.published` (entity the version, ids `{version}`) and `notice.acknowledged` (entity and subject the person, ids `{notice, version}`), both `accounts`, owned by the new `notice` suite. 0009's outbox type check already names both, so 0010 leaves the outbox alone. The audit actions are `notice.publish` and `notice.acknowledge`.
    - **The lock order:** … `transfer_register` → `transfer_clearances` → `dpo_settings` → `orgs` (publishing only) → `notice_versions` → `notice_acknowledgments` → `feature_flags` …. Publishing reads the DPO's rows `FOR SHARE`, locks the registers `FOR SHARE` (and from PR 3a the latest contact, item 45), then the org's own row and the latest version `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`. Nothing else takes a row lock on `orgs`, and a foreign key's KEY SHARE doesn't wait for it. Acknowledging takes the person and the device, then the latest version, `FOR SHARE`. A code's send reads the person `FOR SHARE` (at sign-in through `stillHere()`, at re-authentication as the acting session), whether they hold `dpo` with no lock, then the clearance `FOR SHARE` and the notice with no lock.
34. **Access (PD-49).**
    - **Self:** `notice.read_own` and `notice.acknowledge_own`, for any active person, with no re-authentication.
    - **Admin:** `notice.read` and `notice.publish`, for the DPO alone, with a fresh re-authentication (PD-30). Owners, Admins and the Compliance Officer are refused, and `roles.test.ts` holds every persona's row.
    - **Not Rule 9.** Nothing here imports `privileged.ts`, and no flag reads any of it.
35. **What CI checks (PD-54, PD-55).**
    - **DB tests:** `160_notice.sql` (NTD-01 to NTD-10: row level security on both link tables for every runtime role; each role's privileges; `publish_notice()` for every ACC-10 role, its HF007 and HF008 refusals, the numbering, links and audit row; both triggers; `acknowledge_notice()`; the four send states; `notice_facts()`'s keys), with 015 (two more policies), 020 (the fixture's links), 030 (`hearth_app`'s four executes and two SELECTs, and no execute of `notice_facts()`) and 032 (`hearth_queue`'s column grants and `notice_provider_states()`, and nothing on `notice_version_providers`, a clearance's facts or `notice_facts()`).
    - **Upgrade:** `UP_TO=0010 scripts/check-migrations.sh && scripts/check-notice-upgrade.sh`, over the seed and orgs holding stand-ins v0 and v1, acknowledgements made in the app (one outliving its device) and recorded by the DPO, and Twilio cleared by the bootstrap's function with no version and under a stand-in. NTU-01: every notice row unchanged, and nothing linked; NTU-02: the schema equal to a fresh build's; NTU-03: `hearth_app` writes neither notice table; NTU-04: an older version refused and the latest acknowledged; NTU-05: `transfer_cleared('twilio')` keeps PR 1's answers. The two 0006 runs and the 0008 step also apply 0010, over the seed.
    - **Races** (`apps/server/scripts/check-concurrency.ts`): an acknowledgement against a publish, both orders (NTR-01); a publish against a purpose change (NTR-02), and against a "Save and keep cleared", a withdrawal and a clearance (NTR-03); two publishes from one `seen`, and two first publishes, both with the first committed before the second starts and with the first holding the org's row while the second waits, the second refused HF007 each time (NTR-04); an acknowledgement against a deactivation and a device sign-out (NTR-05); a publish against the DPO being deactivated or losing the role (NTR-06); and a send against a publish, never "not cleared" for a stated clearance (NTR-07).
    - **Catalogues:** check 6 also holds `NOTICE_TRANSFER_FACTS` to `notice_facts()` (item 18), and coverage is checked both ways, with both link tables matched by `notice_version_id` (item 33).
    - **Guards:** realtime-readonly (`notice-standing.ts`, beside `realtime/admission.ts`), api-test-overrides (`admissionForTests`, and `publish_notice()`'s one caller), web-source's WS-11 (`session/notice.ts`) and design-ids (PD-01 to PD-62, and PD-01 to PD-86 from PR 3a, item 47).
    - **Integration** (`apps/server/src/compliance/notice.integration.test.ts`, suite `notice`). The harness (`apps/server/src/testing/stack.ts`) gives each org a stand-in v0 unless `s.newOrg({notice: 'none'})`, and `s.person()` acknowledges the latest version as the superuser unless `{acknowledged: false}`, so the suites before PR 2 test what they tested. A test that publishes uses an org of its own, through `s.publishNotice(org, dpo, {acknowledge})`, which first records a basis and a fictional retention for each active purpose that has none, and `s.acknowledge(person)`.
      - NTI-01, "C0.9 Done: no access before ack": every `/api` route the harness collects, each with a sample request, answers an unacknowledged session 403 `notice_required`, the free and pre-session routes aside, and passes once acknowledged.
      - NTI-02, "C0.9 Done: a new version forces re-ack": 403 at once, 4403 within one check, admitted again after acknowledging.
      - NTI-03: before v0 the DPO reaches the registers, publishing and PIN re-authentication; an Owner, whose session the harness builds, gets 403 on everything but the free routes; a code-method re-authentication request sends nothing; and the gateway admits nobody.
      - NTI-04 to NTI-06: publishing, acknowledging and `GET /api/notice`. NTI-07: a C0.7-style subscribe and removal under admission still drops the topic within its bound.
      - In the sign-in suite, on `FakeVerify` only: XFR-I4 to I7 (before any version the DPO gets a code and an Owner doesn't, `skipped_no_notice`; stated, everyone; a stated fact changed, the DPO alone, `skipped_unstated`; a review date alone, everyone). In the flags suite, in its own org: PGT-I1 (`it.unnotified` turns on once published, and is refused again after an edit).
    - **Unit:** NTG-01 to NTG-13 (the generator and vocabulary: determinism, every section and basis, the first-code section only with Twilio, free text that never starts a line, the problems and warnings, `DOES_NOT`'s coverage and every subset of active non-AI purposes (NTG-10, NTG-11), the retired section (NTG-12), free text with AI wording (NTG-13), a pinned digest per `NOTICE_GENERATOR`, the schemas); NTA-01 to NTA-05 (every action in every standing, DPO or not); NTS-01 to NTS-05; XFR-03 rewritten over the four send states and `codeMaySend()`; PGT-04 rewritten, and PGT-12 to PGT-16.
    - **Web:** NTW-01 to NTW-10; GW-25; RT-05 and RT-10 rewritten (RT-10 starts a second runtime, as a reload does); RO-01, RO-03 and RO-06; NV-04; DPP-06; and, for the new avatar menu (Settings first; three items for the DPO), SH-08, KB-04, DPP-E4, DPP-07, SHM-03 and DPP-E1.
    - **e2e:** SI-10 (Nadia's first sign-in goes through `/notice`, with axe, `noAiCopy`, 44 px targets and no sideways scroll at 320, 390 and 1440 px), NTE-01 (Settings), DPP-E5 (three items fit) and DPP-E6 (the Notice tab, its free-text problem shown through a fulfilled route response, so nothing is written to the seed's org). SE-05 runs last in the serial session-end spec, within a 150 s budget: the four register writes run first in an API setup helper (`e2e/support/registers-api.ts`); Ashton publishes v1 from `/dpo/notice` at 390 px; Kelvin's two tabs reach `/notice`, one acknowledges and goes live, and the other follows; the expected 403 `notice_required` answers are declared. The run spends 23 of its 25 codes. No new visual baselines; the changed `avatar-menu` shots are adopted.
36. **Open points for the owner (PR 2)**, listed in the PR:
    - **PD-41, the seven facts.** A stated Twilio change stops codes for everyone but the DPO until a new version is published, which everyone then acknowledges. The alternative is A-01's three facts only.
    - **PD-39.** Every version, even one that only reflects a withdrawal, is acknowledged by everyone.
    - **PD-38.** No consent-type basis can be published yet (lifted for deemed consent by conduct by OD-18, item 42).
    - **PD-44's departure** from item 20 (item 29).
    - **Protective admin actions wait for the actor's acknowledgement.** An Owner can't deactivate a leaver until they acknowledge. The option is to exempt `person.deactivate` and `person.sign_out_devices`, which C0.16 decides.
    - **PD-59.** Codes go to the DPO alone before v0 and while the notice doesn't state the provider; the owner can widen this.
    - **PD-60, retired purposes for good.** Register entries are never removed (0009 lets no role delete one, and every org keeps every purpose), and setting a retired purpose back to planned would misstate it, so a retired purpose stays in every later notice. "None kept: deleted on <date>" as its retention is how the DPO says the data is gone.
    - **PD-61's bound** (item 27), and **PD-62:** the org's name held to `AI_COPY` by the bootstrap and C0.16, not at publish.
    - **The DPO's copy:** OD-04's paragraph, the production-access paragraph, the three "doesn't do" clauses (PD-58, written without "AI", under decision 8) and the basis wordings (PD-53).
    - **PD-57.** Production's v0 is published after PR 3b (PD-84) and after C0.11 and C0.12 (production and its break-glass exist). From PR 3a a missing contact blocks it (item 40), and `production_access_pending` still warns, because the production-access paragraph states the emergency role, its log and a second person's quarterly review of every use, which C0.11 and C0.19 put in place. Exporting the records comes with C0.16.

    The PR's unticked "Before merging" steps also cover the owner's read of migration 0010, the DPO's confirmation of the copy, and VS-01's six `avatar-menu` baselines, adopted from CI's artifact and looked at in both themes.
37. **What later steps inherit from PR 2.** Each is also in its BUILD-PLAN row.
    - **PR 3a (0011): built, items 38 to 49, except the rights section's form and targets, which pass to PR 3b (item 49; built: item 58).** The DPO contact in the generator (a new generator id, so the notice reads out of date), and `dpo_contact_pending` becomes a problem; publishing stops refusing deemed consent by conduct (withdrawal by hand); a writer for acknowledgements the DPO records, a definer function that re-checks the DPO, the person and the latest version; and the runbooks "a stated Twilio fact changed, and codes go to the DPO alone" and "Twilio withdrawn, and no DPO signed in".
    - **The bootstrap PR (built: the org's name, item 64; no notice, item 66; the recovery mode, item 67):** it writes no notice, it refuses an org name that matches `AI_COPY`, and it carries its recovery mode, which runs item 44's re-clear. In production, in order: the DPO signs in before v0 (the only person a code goes to then), records the contact and the bases, publishes v0 after PR 3b (PD-84), and acknowledges it.
    - **C0.11:** the emergency-role wording is PRD-101's; if C0.11's build differs, it changes the generator and the DPO publishes again.
    - **C0.19:** once its break-glass drill has run and the quarterly review by a second person is in place (the DPO confirms both), it drops the `production_access_pending` warning.
    - **C0.13:** alarms on `sms_provider_unstated` and `notice_pinned_out_of_date`, beside `transfer_review_overdue`, and decides B-16, deferred from PR 2: both log lines are judged and keyed by the UTC day (item 27), since the worker's `hearth_queue` reads no org row and so not the org's zone; judging them by the org's own date needs a grant of `orgs.time_zone` (or a definer function) and a case with two orgs in different zones; RUM's capability reads the provider-notice rule plainly, with no lock, as it reads the clearance (item 8).
    - **C0.14:** push sends call `transfer_cleared()`, under the provider-notice rule; clearing the push services after v0 means a new version that everyone acknowledges; the service worker caches nothing under `/api`, `/api/notice` included.
    - **C0.15:** first run wraps `/notice` after the PIN, with no Skip and the same return path, and adds visual baselines for `/notice` and `/settings*`.
    - **C0.16:** moves `/dpo/notice` under `/admin`; exports the acknowledgement records (PDPA-03's "exportable") and decides whether to name those outstanding; the re-authentication sheet; whether protective admin actions are exempt from the gate; more Settings sections; and org settings that refuse a name matching `AI_COPY`.
    - **C1.3:** consent items, which lift publishing's `consent` refusal and `isProcessingAllowed()`'s consent branch; its first flag naming an unpinned purpose trips G1.
    - **C1.13:** v1 is the DPO's publish from the Notice tab, whose count measures the pilot's "100% ack". It lists sensitive-data detection as a purpose once active, and keeps the "transcribe" and "summarise what you write or draft text from it" clauses, decision 5's line. C1.7's keyword search indexes what people write, so no clause claims Hearth doesn't index it.
    - **C2.5 and C2.11:** v2's recording copy, whose active recording purposes drop the recording clause by themselves; the switches and per-person opt-out windows lift the s15A refusal.
    - **A0.1, A0.2 and A2.2:** the AI sections and their re-acknowledgement (a new generator id), and per-person exclusion by acknowledgement (PD-56).
38. **Scope of PR 3a (OD-05).** PR 3 is built in two, because #33 reached 157 changed files against CodeRabbit's 150-file review limit. PR 3a, with migration 0011, adds:
    - the DPO's business contact: `dpo_settings`, its writer, and `/dpo/contact`, before v0 too (items 39 and 41);
    - the contact in the notice under a new generator id, and publishing refused without it (item 40);
    - the contact on Settings › Privacy for everyone (item 41);
    - deemed consent by conduct in the notice, for each purpose whose written procedure says how one person's withdrawal is carried out (item 42);
    - acknowledgements the DPO records (item 43);
    - Twilio's break-glass re-clear through `bootstrap_transfer_clearance()` (item 44);
    - the runbooks and the DPO's procedures in `docs/runbooks/`, and CLAUDE.md's one exception for answering an access request until C6.1's console replaces that procedure (item 46).

    It leaves to PR 3b, with migration 0012 (built: items 50 to 62): the request-handling purpose (OD-17, item 51); the case table (OD-06), which the DPO alone reads (OD-14), the target of a foreign key from `processing_restrictions.case_id` (item 52); the form and its kinds (OD-07, item 56); the DPO's queue and its indicator (OD-22, item 57); reply targets and business days (OD-08, OD-09, item 55); requester tracking (OD-15, item 56); the record of a requester without an account (OD-16, item 54); and the rights section's form and targets (items 49 and 58). 0011 changes no existing row: every check it adds or widens passes the rows 0003 to 0010 allow and is validated in place, and its new columns are empty on every row already there.
39. **The DPO's contact (OD-10; PD-63, PD-64, PD-65, PD-71, PD-72).**
    - **What it holds (PD-64).** A role title (1 to 120 characters, one line), never a person's name, and at least one way to reach the DPO outside Hearth, so that leavers, people without an account and people who haven't acknowledged can write (OD-13):
      - an email address, in a strict shape the notice prints exactly and a `mailto:` link carries unescaped (no space, quote, `?`, `&`, `%` or `#`), of at most 254 characters;
      - a phone number in international format: + and 7 to 15 digits, with single spaces or hyphens between them;
      - a postal address of at most 500 characters, its lines kept, with no empty line, no control character but the line break, and at least one letter or digit, so the notice never prints an empty "Post:" (`postal_no_text`).

      There is no field for a name. The page advises a role title and a shared mailbox or business line, so the details outlive whoever holds the role. The value lives only in the database, because the repository is public; every fixture's contact is fictional, on a reserved `.example` domain.
    - **Where it lives (PD-63).** `dpo_settings`: one row per org and version, with who recorded it and when, under row level security with the one org policy. A version never changes: `dpo_settings_unchanged()` refuses every UPDATE, the owner's and a superuser's included, and no role holds DELETE. The current contact is the highest version. No org has a row until its DPO records one, so there is no Hearth-made version 1, no trigger on `orgs`, no backfill and no change to check 6. It is never on `orgs`, whose row only publishing locks. PR 3b's reply targets join it as columns (item 55), and each writer copies the columns it doesn't change, so every version is whole. `hearth_app` reads it, only its two definer functions write it (`set_dpo_contact()` and, from PR 3b, `set_reply_targets()`), and no job role reads it.
    - **The writer.** `set_dpo_contact(seen, title, email, phone, postal, request_id, device_id, ip)` returns `(version, changed)`. The server calls it after `can()` allows `notice.contact.set` (the DPO, with a fresh PIN). In order, it:
      - re-checks the DPO (`registers_check_dpo()`: the person, then their `dpo` role, `FOR SHARE`);
      - takes the latest version `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` and refuses a `seen` that isn't it (HF005, 409 `register_changed`). With none recorded there is nothing to lock: of two first records, the second waits on the unique `(org_id, version)` index and is refused the same way, never with 23505, or records version 1 if the first rolled back;
      - trims each field of JavaScript's whitespace, reads empty as none, and applies the rules in `contactProblems()`'s order (HF006, 400 `register_invalid`);
      - writes nothing for the same contact, answering `changed: false`;
      - otherwise inserts the next version, with one audit row, `notice.contact_set`, naming the fields that changed, never their values: the versions are the history.

      A shape the table's own checks refuse (23514 on a `dpo_settings_*` constraint, a backstop) is also 400 `register_invalid`.
    - **The routes.** `GET /api/admin/notice/contact` (`notice.read`, read only) answers the contact, the notice's status and whether the latest version prints it (item 41). `POST /api/admin/notice/contact/set` (`notice.contact.set`) parses `SetContactBody`, which trims each field and normalises the postal address's lines, and runs `contactProblems()`, `AI_COPY` included, before the database, so the page and the route refuse what the function refuses (NTI-12 holds the two to one list of samples). It sits in `apps/server/src/compliance/routes.ts` beside the register writers and reuses their refusal map, since `routes.ts` already imports `notice.ts`.
    - **No new code (PD-71).** The contact reuses the registers' HF005 and HF006 (`register_changed`, `register_invalid`), whose shared copy fits it, and `registers_check_dpo()` unchanged, message included. There is no new SQLSTATE, api code or DPO helper, so `REGISTER_ERRORS` and `NOTICE_ERRORS` don't move.
    - **No outbox event (PD-72).** Nothing acts on a contact change: it reaches people only through the next published version, which `notice.published` covers. `coverage.ts` exempts the writer with that reason, and `outbox_events_type` isn't widened, so 0011 takes no lock on the outbox.
    - **`AI_COPY` (PD-65).** The notice and Settings show the contact, so it is held to `AI_COPY` at save (the page and the route: `contactProblems()`'s `ai_copy`, which names the field and never the word) and again at publish (`free_text_ai_copy` on `contact_title`, `contact_email` and `contact_postal`), because the list may grow after a contact was saved. SQL repeats neither (item 45).
40. **The contact in the notice (OD-11; PD-66, PD-67, PD-68, PD-74).**
    - **`c09.2`.** `NOTICE_GENERATOR` becomes `c09.2`, which NTG-09 pins by digest beside `c09.1`. Its *Your rights* reads (PD-74; the DPO confirms the words):
      - "You can request a copy of the personal data <org> holds about you, have it corrected, or withdraw consent where a purpose rests on it." (`c09.1`'s first paragraph, unchanged);
      - "To do any of these, or to raise a question or a complaint about how your personal data is handled, contact the Data Protection Officer of <org>. You don't need a Hearth account for this, so it still works after you leave.";
      - an item for the role and each channel recorded: "Role: <title>", "Email: <email>", "Phone: <phone>" and "Post: <the postal address on one line, joined with commas>", with no full stop, so an address reads exactly;
      - "If something has gone wrong, <org> wants to hear about it and to put it right. If you're unhappy with how it's handled, or would rather not raise it with <org>, you can contact the Personal Data Protection Commission (PDPC), Singapore's data protection regulator." (the owner, 24 Oct 2026, item 48; reworded by the owner on 26 Oct 2026, so it also gives the PDPC as the route for a complaint someone would rather not raise with the org, OD-14's complaint about the DPO included).

      Each item is flattened and never starts a line (PD-33), and `<org>` falls back to "your organisation" for a name that matches `AI_COPY` (item 24). The pointer to the employee data-protection policy goes; the first-code section keeps its mention of it (OD-04). One function, `rightsBlocks(org, contact)`, writes the section, so PR 3b adds its form and targets in one place.
    - **`dpo_contact_pending` is a problem (PD-68).** It leaves the warnings for `NOTICE_PROBLEMS`, in SQL's refusal order, and keeps its inherited name. It is unwritable: no preview until a contact is recorded, since *Your rights* can't be written without one, and its fix link opens `/dpo/contact`. In SQL it is publishing's fourth rule (HF008, item 25).
    - **A source of every version (PD-66).** `publish_notice()` is dropped and created with a seventh argument, `contact_version`, the settings version the draft printed (null while none is recorded). After the transfer entries and before the org's row it takes the latest `dpo_settings` version `FOR SHARE`, again until the row held is still the latest. It refuses a `contact_version` that isn't that row's (HF007, 409 `notice_changed`, with a message of its own, so 0010's assertions of the registers' message don't move), refuses while none is recorded (HF008), links the row it locked in `notice_versions.dpo_settings_version` (never the argument alone), and names that version in the audit row's details. A version made before 0011 links none, and a foreign key keeps each link to the org's own settings. An overload would have left a contact-blind publisher callable, so 0010's signature is dropped.
    - **What the drop costs.** The pre-deploy migrate drops 0010's signature before the new api is live, so PR 2's api answers 500 (42883) to a publish in that window, and a server rolled back past 0011 can't publish. There is no production until C0.11 and C0.12, the non-prod deploy replaces the api straight after the migrate, and publishing is the DPO's alone and rare. PD-66's alternative, a stub of the old signature that refuses with HF008, fixes it if the owner wants rollback across 0011 (item 48).
    - **Out of date and unchanged (PD-67).** A new settings version counts as a purpose's new register version does: the notice is out of date until it is published, never "already says this", and it warns `same_text` when the words match. `sameWords()` compares the settings version, and the digest carries it. `changes.contact` compares the linked versions' contacts: `added` when the earlier version linked none (the first `c09.2` version after a `c09.1` one or a stand-in, which gives the contact for the first time), `changed` when their facts differ, and `same` otherwise. `reworded` needs `same`, so a contact-only change doesn't also claim new wording. `/notice`'s callout says "The Data Protection Officer’s contact details have changed.", or that the notice now gives them.
    - **Every org's notice reads out of date once PR 3a deploys,** since the generator id changes and publishing needs a contact (OD-11 and PD-39 accept it). In non-prod and dev the Notice tab says so until the DPO records a contact and publishes.
41. **Where people see it, and before v0 (OD-12, OD-13; PD-69, PD-70).**
    - **The DPO's page (PD-70).** `/dpo/contact`, titled "Contact details", in a lazy chunk of its own, is the fourth of the registers' links (Purposes, Transfers, Contact, Notice) and shows the Notice link's badge. It reads `GET /api/admin/notice/contact` under `notice.read`, whose `stated` is one of four, shown as a pill:
      - `unpublished`: no version yet ("Not published yet");
      - `stated`: the latest version prints this contact ("In the published notice");
      - `not_printed`: the latest prints none, being a stand-in, a `c09.1` version, or published before any contact was recorded ("Not in the published notice yet");
      - `not_stated`: the latest prints an earlier contact ("Not in the published notice yet", with "The published notice still gives your earlier details.").

      With no contact it offers "Record contact details", the title filled in as "Data Protection Officer". The dialog holds Save back while `contactProblems()` finds anything in the form as it would be sent, takes the PIN inline, and, once a version is published, warns that saving makes the notice out of date. Writing it is `notice.contact.set`: the DPO alone, with a fresh PIN.
    - **Before v0.** `notice.contact.set` joins `NOTICE_SETUP` (item 29), and the page sits under `/dpo/*`, which the shell already opens to the DPO before v0. Without both, the new problem would make production's first publish impossible. In order: the bootstrap gives the org no contact; the DPO signs in (only they get a code, PD-59); `/notice` offers "Record your contact details" beside the purposes page and the Notice tab; the DPO records the contact, the bases and the push services' clearances, publishes v0, which links the contact, and acknowledges it. NTA-02 pins the set, and NTI-03 walks it on the real stack. After v0 nothing new can lock anyone out: a contact change makes the notice only out of date, never required; recording an acknowledgement needs a version; and a contact, once recorded, can't be removed.
    - **Settings › Privacy (PD-69).** Its first section, "Contact the Data Protection Officer", shows the contact the latest version printed, read in the same statement as the version shown, so the two never disagree: the role title, then each channel as a row at least 44 px high (the email a `mailto:` link and the phone a `tel:` link, each link's text the value; the postal address on its lines), long values breaking so nothing scrolls sideways at 320 px, then "You don’t need Hearth for this, so it still works after you leave." With none printed it says the current notice doesn't give the contact yet and that the employee data protection policy does; unreadable, it says so. A new contact reaches Settings once it is published, which the contact page's pill and the out-of-date banner press the DPO to do. The order (the contact, the notice, your acknowledgements) is pinned, so PR 3b inserted its form and "Your requests" after the contact and rearranged nothing (item 56).
    - **Bundle (T21 item 12).** The contact page and its copy (`dpo/ContactScreen.tsx`, `strings/dpo-contact.ts`) are a lazy chunk of their own, and the record dialog and its copy (`dpo/RecordAcknowledgement.tsx`, `strings/dpo-record.ts`, item 43) load with the Notice tab, so neither joins the `RegisterPage` chunk every DPO page shares. PR 3a raised no budget: its final build, on main's zod 4.6.7, measured 153,386 bytes of initial JS (gzip), 7,347 for the largest lazy chunk, `RegisterPage`'s (PR 2: 6,868), 5,287 for the Notice tab's, 3,553 for the contact page's and 1,404 for Settings › Privacy's.
    - **Help waits for C0.18,** which mirrors the contact (OD-12). The avatar menu is unchanged (Settings, Data protection, Sign out), so no VS-01 baseline moves.
    - **The gate is unchanged (OD-13).** Nobody sends a request before acknowledging the current notice. Anyone outside the shell, a leaver or someone without an account included, uses the channel the notice prints, which is why OD-10 requires one outside Hearth. `NOTICE_FREE` doesn't change.
42. **Deemed consent by conduct (OD-18; PD-73, PD-86).**
    - **What publishing now allows.** `publish_notice()` and `packages/shared`'s held bases (`NOTICE_HELD_BASES`, `isNoticeHeldBasis()`) stop refusing deemed consent by conduct. They still refuse consent until C1.3's consent items, and deemed consent by notification (s15A) until C2.11 tracks each person's opt-out window. 0009's `processing_register_switch` still refuses every consent-type basis for a switch purpose until C2.11, so publishing never sees deemed consent by conduct on one.
    - **The words (PD-73; the DPO confirms them).** The basis item reads "Legal basis: your deemed consent. You give this data yourself, for this purpose, and it is reasonable that you would. You can withdraw it, as “If you withdraw consent” below says." *If you withdraw consent* speaks of the active purposes on the basis, in registry order:
      - with none: "None of the purposes under “Why your data is processed” rests on your consent, so they all continue if you withdraw it.";
      - with some: "These purposes rest on your deemed consent, so they stop for you if you withdraw it:", an item for each, then "The other purposes under “Why your data is processed” don't rest on consent, so they continue if you withdraw it." and "To withdraw, contact the Data Protection Officer, as “Your rights” below says. Hearth can't take a withdrawal in the app yet, so they carry it out by hand: they tell you first what withdrawing changes for you, and confirm once it has taken effect."

      Both branches speak only of the purposes under *Why your data is processed*, which Hearth still processes data for: a retired purpose is listed in every later notice (PD-60), may keep a consent-type basis, and continues for nobody. The "other purposes" sentence is true whenever it prints, since publishing refuses the two held bases for an active purpose and the pinned purposes never rest on consent. No reply target was stated until PR 3b, whose `c09.3` states them once, in *Your rights*, and whose withdraw branch drops "Hearth can't take a withdrawal in the app yet" and names the form (item 58).
    - **By hand.** `isProcessingAllowed()` is unchanged, and no restriction can be recorded until C2.11 (`processing_restrictions` has no writer), so the DPO carries out each withdrawal under `docs/runbooks/withdrawal-by-hand.md`, whose table says, for each purpose, what stops for the person and how. The purposes page's `withdrawal_by_hand` warning stands.
    - **Only where a stop is real (PD-86).** The notice tells people such a purpose stops for them, so a purpose may rest on the basis only once its row says how. `CONDUCT_WITHDRAWABLE` (`packages/shared/src/registers.ts`) lists those purposes. The publish route holds back any other active purpose on the basis (`purpose_conduct_unstoppable`: writable, so the preview shows the words, with a fix link to the purposes page), and SQL doesn't repeat the list, as it doesn't repeat `AI_COPY`. A purpose joins the list in the PR that writes its row, and the runbooks guard (RB-05) holds each one to its row. In C0 the list is empty: `security_audit` and `backups`, the only unpinned purposes active in C0, can't be stopped for one person. So the lift is right but unused until a purpose exists whose stop by hand is real, and the procedure says why.
43. **Acknowledgements the DPO records (OD-19; PD-75, PD-76).**
    - **What is kept (PD-75).** `notice_acknowledgments` gains `acknowledged_on`, the day the person acknowledged the version outside Hearth, in the org's zone, and `recorded_note`, how they did (one line of 1 to 500 characters). The two come together, and only on `recorded_by_dpo` rows. `acknowledged_at` stays the moment of recording. Rows from before 0011 have neither, which the checks allow (none was written in production). The person sees both in Settings: the day as the acknowledgement's day, and the note on a line of its own. The audit row keeps the day, never the note.
    - **The function.** `record_notice_acknowledgement(person, notice_id, on_day, note, request_id, device_id, ip)`, the notice tables' third writer, returns `(version, changed)`. It is an admin action on someone else, so the DPO's rows come first and the person's after. In order, it:
      - re-checks the DPO (`notice_check_dpo()`, unchanged, message included);
      - refuses yourself (you acknowledge in Hearth; 0003's `notice_acknowledgments_not_self_recorded` check backs it);
      - reads the person `FOR SHARE`: not the org's is 22023, and someone who has left is refused (42501), so a deactivation in progress makes it wait and then refuse;
      - refuses an id that isn't one of the org's versions (22023), then takes the latest version `FOR SHARE`, again until it is still the latest, and refuses any other (HF007), so a publish in progress makes it wait and then find the newer version;
      - checks the day, from the version's publication day to today, both in the org's zone, and the note, trimmed (HF008);
      - writes once per person and version: if they already acknowledged it, in the app or as recorded, it writes nothing and answers `changed: false`; otherwise the row (`recorded_by_dpo`, the DPO, the day, the note, no device) and one audit row, `notice.acknowledgement_recorded`, naming the person, the version and the day.
    - **The route.** `POST /api/admin/notice/acknowledgements/record {person, notice, acknowledgedOn, note}` (`notice.acknowledgement.record {person}`) holds the note to `AI_COPY` first (400 `notice_invalid`), since the person sees it, then calls the function and, when something changed, appends `notice.acknowledged`, the event an acknowledgement in the app appends (its description now says "in the app or as the DPO recorded it"), answering `{version, changed}`. The refusals: 403 `forbidden` (not the DPO, yourself, someone who has left, or a person not at the org: all `can()`'s, over a World of the session's org only, so the function's 42501 is only a race); 404 `not_found` (22023: an unknown version, or another org's); 409 `notice_changed` (HF007); and 400 `notice_invalid` (HF008), which the dialog words itself (PD-71).
    - **The action.** The DPO alone, with a fresh PIN, never for themselves and only for an active person of the session's org. It isn't a setup use, so never before v0: there is nothing to record then, and after it the DPO acknowledges the latest version first, like everyone (PD-44).
    - **The list of people (PD-76).** The DPO picks the person in the Notice tab's "Record an acknowledgement" dialog, from `GET /api/admin/notice/people`, read only when the dialog opens, under an action of its own, `notice.people.read` (the DPO, a fresh PIN, not a setup use), so `notice.read` still names nobody (PD-49). It answers today, the latest version, and every active person but the DPO, by name, with their department, manager and the day they were added, so the dialog can tell apart two who would read the same (and, when they still do, warns and records neither, since the DPO can't know which of the two a record would let in; they acknowledge in Hearth), at most 2,000 in name order, with `more` when cut. Never a number, a device or who has acknowledged: C0.16 decides whether to name those outstanding.
    - **The accepted residual (PD-76).** Recording answers `changed: false`, and the count doesn't move, when that one person had already acknowledged, so the DPO learns their standing. The DPO already holds the counts, recording is audited, and a neutral answer would hide nothing, since the count moves only on a change (item 48).
    - **It lets the person in at once.** `noticeStandings()` counts any method: REST at their next request, sockets within one check. Their Settings shows the day and the note.
    - **Two writers, still no trigger.** `acknowledge_notice()` and `record_notice_acknowledgement()` each admit only the latest version, under their own lock, so item 26's reasoning holds: no acknowledgement trigger, 070's superuser fixtures may acknowledge older versions, and a data-only restore needs nothing turned off.
44. **Twilio's break-glass re-clear (OD-20; PD-77, PD-78, PD-79, PD-80).**
    - **Two modes, chosen explicitly (PD-77).** 0011 replaces `bootstrap_transfer_clearance()` with the same signature, owner and grants (none: security invoker, run by its owner alone, BSC-01). Without the emergency marker it is 0009's function, whole and in 0009's order, messages included: it clears only a provider never cleared in the org (item 16), so a set-up run, a retry or a script reused against an existing org never becomes an override. With the marker, `set_config('hearth.break_glass', 'twilio_reclear', true)` in the same transaction (any other value counts as unset), it runs the re-clear, and only for a provider cleared before: the marker on one never cleared is refused, so no first clearance is ever recorded as an emergency. `hearth.*` is a placeholder setting that no server code sets. The marker records intent and is no privilege, since only `hearth_owner` or a superuser can run the function at all.
    - **The re-clear's checks, in order,** after 0009's own (the org's Hearth principal, `twilio` or `aws`, and a reason of 1 to 2,000 characters). Each refusal writes nothing.
      1. A clearance of the provider was recorded in the org before, else HF006.
      2. Twilio Verify only, else HF006 with 0009's message: losing AWS locks nobody out, since a DPO can still get a code and clear it again in the app.
      3. **Its own bounds (PD-79).** Before its first lock it sets a 2 s `lock_timeout` and a 30 s `transaction_timeout`, transaction-local, as `withOrg()` does, because it then holds every DPO's row and an uncommitted outbox row and so must not outlive a server transaction. Postgres starts a transaction's timer only when none is running, lowering the setting never shortens a running one, and SQL can read only the setting, never the timer, so the function always switches the timer off and starts it again at 30 s from that point, whatever the caller's session or transaction set before (BSC-04; BGR-06 on a connection whose session set a longer one). That can lengthen a shorter timer by the time spent before the call, so the recovery mode sets its own 30 s at connection, which also bounds that time. A lock it can't get within 2 s answers 55P03, and the caller runs it again; a transaction left open after the call is ended 30 s on, and the re-clear rolls back. Both settings stay set when it returns.
      4. **The review date (PD-78):** at most 90 days ahead in the org's zone (read plainly), else HF006 ("The DPO sets a later one in Hearth."); the clearance's own check still wants it after today.
      5. **Every current DPO's person row `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` (PD-79),** in id order, again until the set of `dpo` holders still at the org is stable, so a grant or a departure committing meanwhile is seen.
      6. The DPO named is one of them, their role row read `FOR SHARE`, else 42501 (0009's message), so a revocation or a deactivation of them in progress makes it wait and then refuse (BGR-05).
      7. **No live DPO session (PD-79):** none of theirs that isn't revoked or expired, on a device not signed out, read plainly once their rows are held, else HF006 ("A Data Protection Officer is signed in, so they clear Twilio Verify again in Hearth."). Idle time isn't counted, which fails closed.
      8. The Twilio entry `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, as every clearance writer locks it, with no clearance open, else HF006 ("Twilio Verify is cleared already.").
      9. The facts, the clearance, the audit row and the event (below).
    - **Why the rows are locked (PD-79).** A code sent before a withdrawal can still finish a sign-in after it: `verifyCode()` asks the provider by its reference with no transfer gate, and `finish()`, `setPin()` and `verifyPinStep()` issue sessions for an attempt that stays verified for up to 10 minutes (`apps/server/src/auth/sign-in.ts`). Each first reads the person `FOR SHARE` (`stillHere()`), which waits for `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, so a DPO sign-in in progress finishes first and its session is then seen, and one that starts later waits for the re-clear. Every transaction that ends a DPO's sessions locks the person first too. A plain read of sessions would miss one being issued at that moment.
    - **The facts (PD-78).** Given in full, as the first clearance takes them, trimmed, written with Hearth as `updated_by` and audited from and to, so the re-clear can repair facts a plain Save left incomplete; `transfer_clearances_check()` then stamps the snapshot and refuses incomplete facts. The recovery mode fills them, not the person: by default from the facts snapshot of the Twilio clearance the latest published version links (`notice_version_providers`), else from the last ended Twilio clearance's, so they are what the notice states and codes go to everyone again. It takes the DPO's record only for a fact the DPO decided changed, and sends then stay the DPO's alone until a version states it. The linked snapshot comes first because a "Save and keep cleared" that changed a stated fact ends one clearance and opens another, so the last ended one can hold facts the notice doesn't state.
    - **What it records (PD-80).** A clearance in the named DPO's name with `cleared_via 'break_glass'`, which a check allows for Twilio alone (`transfer_clearances_break_glass`); one audit row, `register.transfer_cleared`, with Hearth as the actor, the reason, and `details` `{"by": "break_glass", "decided_by": <the DPO>, "revision": n, "was_revision": m, "changed": {…}, "clearance": <its id>, "ended_clearance": <the one that ended last>, "ended_reason": "dpo_decision" | "facts_changed"}`, never `via` or `on_behalf_of_id`; and `register.transfer_changed`, as the first clearance appends it. The Transfers page says "Cleared again by the emergency re-clear on <day>. Check its facts and review date.", so a DPO coming back sees why Twilio is cleared and that it wasn't them. The facts given move the entry's revision by one, with Hearth as `updated_by`, whenever any differs from the entry's current facts, `review_on` included: so a re-clear on the stated facts with a new review date records `changed` holding `review_on` alone and still reads `cleared`, and the Transfers page shows the entry as set by Hearth; only the review date the entry already holds keeps the revision (BSC-05).
    - **What follows for sends.** `transfer_send_state('twilio')` answers `cleared` when the latest version states the facts given (everyone gets codes), `unstated` when it doesn't (the DPO alone, and `docs/runbooks/twilio-fact-changed.md` applies), and `no_notice` before v0 (the DPO alone). So the re-clear never sends anyone's number but the DPO's to Twilio on facts the published notice doesn't state, as PD-59 already allows.
    - **How misuse is stopped:**
      - a runtime process or the Rule 9 role runs it: it is security invoker and granted to nobody (BSC-01);
      - a set-up run, a retry or a reused script, meant as set-up: without the marker it is 0009's, and the marker on a provider never cleared is refused (BSC-04, NTU-08);
      - overriding a DPO who can still act: refused while any current DPO has a live session, under locks that serialise with sign-in and sign-out (BGR-01, BGR-02);
      - overriding a deliberate withdrawal: the function can't know why it was withdrawn, so the runbook forbids that use; the named DPO and the reason are recorded, the event fires, the Transfers page names the re-clear, and C0.11's break-glass log and the second person's quarterly review cover the credential's use;
      - changing what people are told about Twilio: the recovery mode passes the stated facts by default, any other facts give `unstated` (the DPO alone until a publish), and every change is audited from and to;
      - an interactive transaction left open, holding every DPO's row: the function's own 2 s and 30 s bounds end it, and the runbook forbids running it by hand;
      - AWS, a push service or an AI provider: Twilio alone, by the function and by the check;
      - a review date parked years away: at most 90 days;
      - two runs at once, or a run against the DPO's own clear in the app: the people and entry locks serialise them and the second is refused (BGR-03, BGR-04), with the unique `transfer_clearances_open` index the last line;
      - a coding agent: agents never connect to production (CLAUDE.md).

      A holder of `hearth_owner`'s credential can write any table directly, and no function can stop that. CLAUDE.md forbids it, and this path exists so that nobody needs to.
    - **Who runs it.** A person, as `hearth_owner` under C0.11's logged break-glass role, through the bootstrap script's recovery mode (`node scripts/owner-bootstrap.ts recover --database <name>`, item 67), following `docs/runbooks/twilio-withdrawn.md`: never a coding agent, and never from CI against Twilio.
    - **The bootstrap PR's contract for the recovery mode.** It runs non-interactively, in one transaction, acting as the org's Hearth principal. It connects with a 30 s `transaction_timeout` as a start-up option, so its transaction is bounded from its first statement, and at the transaction's start it sets the marker and the same `lock_timeout` as `withOrg()`. In that transaction it reads the facts snapshot of the Twilio clearance the latest published version links (else the last ended one's) and passes every fact from it, taking from the person only the review date (at most 90 days ahead) and the reason, unless the DPO decided a stated fact changed. It prints the clearance and `transfer_send_state('twilio')`, and it meets BSC-04 and BSC-05. Met by the script's recovery mode (item 67).
    - **The residual.** With one DPO, the same person decides and the person on call runs it, so a second person reviews each use quarterly (C0.19). A deactivated sole DPO can't be named, so the re-clear refuses until C0.16's grant makes a new DPO. A DPO with a live session they can't use blocks it until that session ends; the recovery run prints how many DPOs hold one and when the last ends: the runbook says what ends one now, and a DPO's session, issued under the PIN rule, ends at most 7 days after issue.
45. **Writes, refusals, events, the lock order and access (PR 3a; PD-71, PD-72).**
    - **Writers.** `set_dpo_contact()` is `dpo_settings`' only writer, and `record_notice_acknowledgement()` the notice tables' third, beside `publish_notice()` and `acknowledge_notice()`. Each re-checks its actor in tables and writes its audit row (ADM-06): `notice.contact_set`, `notice.acknowledgement_recorded`, and `notice.publish`, whose details gain the contact's version.
    - **Grants.** `hearth_app` reads `dpo_settings` (for the contact page, the draft and everyone's `GET /api/notice`), keeps SELECT on `notice_acknowledgments`, whose new columns the person's own rows show, and runs `set_dpo_contact()`, `record_notice_acknowledgement()` and `publish_notice()`'s new signature. Every new function has execute revoked from PUBLIC. Nothing goes to a job role or `hearth_worker`, and `hearth_queue`'s column grant on `notice_versions` isn't widened, since the worker never reads the link. `bootstrap_transfer_clearance()` stays granted to nobody.
    - **Refusals.** No new SQLSTATE or api code (PD-71); `apps/server/src/db/sqlstate.ts` gains comments only.

      | SQLSTATE | New in 0011 | Answer |
      |---|---|---|
      | HF005 | `set_dpo_contact()`: stale, or a second first record | 409 `register_changed` |
      | HF006 | `set_dpo_contact()`'s rules; the re-clear's refusals (no route calls it) | 400 `register_invalid` |
      | 23514 on `dpo_settings_*` | the table's checks, a backstop | 400 `register_invalid` |
      | HF007 | `publish_notice()`: the contact moved; `record_notice_acknowledgement()`: not the latest version | 409 `notice_changed` |
      | HF008 | `publish_notice()`: no contact; `record_notice_acknowledgement()`: the day or the note | 400 `notice_invalid` |
      | 22023 | `record_notice_acknowledgement()`: an unknown version, or another org's | 404 `not_found` |
      | 42501 | the DPO re-checks; recording for yourself or a leaver | 403 `forbidden` |
      | 55P03, 40P01 | a lock timeout (the re-clear's own included), a deadlock | 409 `try_again` |
    - **Events and coverage.** No new event type, so `outbox_events_type` and the catalogue are unchanged. `coverage.ts` exempts `set_dpo_contact()` (PD-72) and `dpo_settings_unchanged()` (a check), gives `record_notice_acknowledgement()` `notice.acknowledged`, and replaces 0010's `publish_notice()` key with the new signature, with the same event and writes. `bootstrap_transfer_clearance()` keeps no entry: no runtime role can run it.
    - **The lock order:** … `role_assignments` → `cases` (from PR 3b, item 59) → `processing_register` → `processing_restrictions` → `transfer_register` → `transfer_clearances` → `dpo_settings` → `orgs` (publishing only) → `notice_versions` → `notice_acknowledgments` → `feature_flags` ….
      - A contact change, and from PR 3b a reply-target change (item 55), takes the latest `dpo_settings` version `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` after the DPO's rows, and nothing after it.
      - Publishing takes it `FOR SHARE` after the transfer entries and before the org's row, so a publish and a contact change wait on one row and never on each other in a circle (NTR-08).
      - Recording an acknowledgement reads the DPO's rows, then that person's row `FOR SHARE` (an admin action on someone else), then the latest version `FOR SHARE`, so it meets a publish only on that version (NTR-10).
      - The re-clear sets its own bounds, then takes every current DPO's person row `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, the named DPO's role row `FOR SHARE` and the entry `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, so it meets a sign-in only on the person's row (BGR-01).
      - 0011 itself takes SHARE ROW EXCLUSIVE on `orgs` and `principals` (the new table's foreign keys), which conflicts only with writes to them, and last, before their ALTERs, ACCESS EXCLUSIVE on `transfer_clearances`, `notice_versions` and `notice_acknowledgments`. Requests read those three in both orders: a sign-in send holds `transfer_clearances` and then reads `notice_versions`, while a gated request reads the notice first (the notice gate) and then may read a clearance (a re-authentication code's send, the registers). No single order avoids a deadlock with both, so the migration never waits while it holds one: each attempt asks for all three with `NOWAIT`, a busy table rolls the attempt back and lets go of what it took, and the next follows 0.1 s later, for up to 100 attempts (about 10 s); then it fails with 55P03 and the deploy's migrate step is run again (NTR-13). Once taken, those locks pause each request's standing read and each send's clearance read until the commit.
    - **Access.** `notice.contact.set`, `notice.acknowledgement.record {person}` and `notice.people.read` join `ADMIN_ACTIONS`, so each asks for a fresh re-authentication, and `adminCan()` allows each to the DPO alone, the record only for someone else still at the org. `roles.test.ts` holds every persona's row (NTA-06). `NOTICE_SETUP` gains `notice.contact.set` alone, and `NOTICE_FREE` is unchanged. Nothing here imports `privileged.ts`, and no flag reads any of it.
    - **Each writer's checking route is its only caller.** SQL repeats neither `AI_COPY` nor `CONDUCT_WITHDRAWABLE`, code lists a migration can't follow, so `scripts/guards/api-test-overrides.test.ts` lets only `compliance/notice.ts` name `publish_notice` and `record_notice_acknowledgement`, and only `compliance/routes.ts` name `set_dpo_contact`, apart from the server's `testing/` folder, tests, CI's checks in `apps/server/scripts/` and each signature as a catalogue key, and checks that each route runs its check before its call.
46. **The runbooks (OD-21, OD-14, OD-22; PD-81, PD-85).**
    - **The folder (PD-81).** `docs/runbooks/` holds a README, "Runbooks and procedures", and nine files: setting up the organisation and its first Owner, publishing the notice, a stated Twilio fact changed, Twilio withdrawn with no DPO signed in, a suspected breach, an access request, a correction request, a withdrawal carried out by hand, and complaints. Each but the README has the same sections, in order: *Use this when*, *Who does what*, *Before you start*, *Steps*, *Check it worked*, *Afterwards* and, last, *Code paths*, which lists in backticks the code it relies on. *Don't* may sit between them where a mistake would make things worse, and other sections, such as *Prevention*, may too. Each step is one action, with who takes it.
    - **Public, so roles only.** The repository is public, so a runbook names roles (the DPO, an Owner, an Admin, the person on call for production, who holds C0.11's break-glass role, and the second reviewer), never a person, a number, an address, an account or a Console user. Facts are read at run time from Secrets Manager or the DPO's own records, and output that holds people's data goes only under `local-data/`. A person runs them; coding agents never do, and nothing calls Twilio from CI.
    - **The guard.** `scripts/guards/runbooks.test.ts` (RB-01 to RB-05) checks that the README links every runbook and that every link resolves; that each has its sections in order; that none holds anything shaped like a phone number (+ and digits however grouped, or a Singapore number written locally), an email address, an AWS account id (run together or in hyphenated fours) or ARN, a Twilio SID, a link other than the PDPC's, or a link by any scheme but http(s); that every name a *Code paths* section lists exists now (a function in `db/migrations/`, a path the web app or the api registers, a repository file, an access action a server handler serves, or another name the code holds), reading only those sections; and that every `CONDUCT_WITHDRAWABLE` purpose has a row in the withdrawal procedure that says how, every row naming a real purpose. These are checks of shape: a person's name or a postal address has none, and "says how" is a heuristic (a cell that doesn't start by saying it can't be done, isn't possible or isn't active yet), so the review of every runbook change backs them up. A code path is listed only once it exists: a step says in words what waits for a later step.
    - **Who keeps them.** Changes land by PR, and the DPO confirms every change to a procedure.
    - **Before C0.16's console.** There is no screen yet for signing out someone else's devices, resetting a PIN, deactivating an account, changing a number or correcting a roster fact. Until then:
      - Sign out in the avatar menu ends the current device's session;
      - the devices routes (`GET /api/devices`, `POST /api/devices/sign-out`) and the admin routes (`POST /api/admin/people/sign-out-devices`, `/reset-pin` and `/deactivate`, and `GET /api/admin/people/:person/devices`) exist with no screen. Each admin route needs a re-authentication from the last 5 minutes and an actor who has acknowledged the latest version (item 36), and is sent from that Owner's or Admin's own signed-in tab, never by a coding agent;
      - a PIN reset or a deactivation refuses an action on yourself, and only an Owner resets a DPO's PIN, so with one founder who is the sole Owner and the DPO (D35), nobody can reset their PIN in the app until the break-glass PIN reset's PR, before C0.15 (T14 item 21, C0.9 OD-28);
      - nothing changes a number or a roster fact until C0.16, and nobody writes to a table instead, except the founder's own number before their first sign-in, which the bootstrap script's correction replaces (item 68).
    - **Reply targets (OD-08, OD-09).** In the app and the notice from PR 3b: the DPO sets them on `/dpo/contact` (`set_reply_targets()`, item 55), by default 10 business days for withdrawals, objections, questions and complaints, counted as weekdays in the org's time zone (holidays count, so a due date errs early), and 30 calendar days for access and correction, within the ranges OD-26 set, and *Your rights* prints them (item 58). The queue shows each request's due day, which never moves later than the targets stated when it arrived; one that arrived when no notice stated targets follows the current ones, never past 10 business days or 30 days. No copy says an alert exists until C0.14 (OD-22).
    - **Records: the queue (PD-85, PD-109).** Every request is a case at `/dpo/requests`, holding what PR 3a's private request log held: the day received, the channel, the kind, the requester, how identity was checked (OD-16's minimum: a name, one contact channel and a note of how, never a copy of an ID document), the due date, the steps with their dates, and the outcome. The log's open entries move into the queue once v0 is published and acknowledged, each with its original day received, and are then closed in the log (item 60).
    - **The daily routine until C0.14 (OD-22).** The count of requests needing attention shows when Hearth is opened (item 57), and each working day the DPO checks the mailbox and any other channel the contact names, and logs what came there.
    - **A complaint about the DPO (OD-14).** There is no second reader in Hearth, so the complaints procedure tells the complainant at once that they can go to the Personal Data Protection Commission, and records that it did.
    - **The access procedure's extraction.** Until C6.1's console replaces the procedure, a PDPA s21 request can be answered in full only by reading one requester's non-content records from production: the ones `access-request.md` step 1 lists (roster facts, number, devices and sessions, sign-in and code audit rows, whether a PIN is set, acknowledgements). That is CLAUDE.md's one exception to "never one person's rows": read-only, by the person on call under a logged break-glass use that names the request by its case reference, never the requester, and that a second person reviews, never through the Rule 9 module's credential and never by a coding agent, with its output only under `local-data/` and deleted once the reply is sent. Content (chat and direct messages, and later recordings, transcripts and docs) is read only through C1.11's Rule 9 service, never under this exception. From C0.16 the audit rows come through `read_audit_events()` once the organisation chooses its readers. It has its own "Before merging" step, and a fallback if the owner declines (item 48).
    - **Later.** C1.11 adds the timed dry runs and updates the procedures for the Rule 9 service, and C6.1's consoles replace the request runbooks.
47. **What CI checks (PR 3a; PD-82, PD-83).**
    - **DB tests:** `165_dpo_settings.sql` (DCD-01 to DCD-10: row level security for every runtime role, each role's privileges, the trigger, `set_dpo_contact()` for every ACC-10 role with its rules, staleness, no-ops and audit row, and `publish_notice()`'s contact comparison, rule and link) and `170_recorded_acknowledgements.sql` (RAD-01 to RAD-08: the new columns' checks, and `record_notice_acknowledgement()` for every ACC-10 role, with its refusals, its range of days, once per person and version, and its audit row); BSC-04 to BSC-07 in `155_transfers.sql` (the re-clear's two modes, its checks, bounds and records, and the sends that follow); and 015, 020, 030, 032, 160 and the fixture.
    - **Races** (`apps/server/scripts/check-concurrency.ts`): a publish against a contact change, both orders (NTR-08); two contact changes from one version, and two first records, with the first committed and with it rolled back (NTR-09); a recorded acknowledgement against a publish, both orders (NTR-10); a record against its person's deactivation, and a record or a contact change by a DPO being deactivated or losing the role (NTR-11); a record against the person's own acknowledgement, both orders (NTR-12); the re-clear against a DPO's sign-in in progress, both orders (BGR-01), a DPO's sessions ending (BGR-02), another re-clear (BGR-03), the DPO's own clear in the app (BGR-04), and the named DPO losing the role or being deactivated (BGR-05); the re-clear's timer started again at 30 s on a connection whose session set a longer one and whose transaction lowered it (BGR-06); and 0011 applied while a gated request reads the notice and then a clearance, neither failing (NTR-13).
    - **Integration:** in the notice suite, NTI-08 to NTI-12 (the contact recorded, refused and changed; a contact change making the notice out of date; recorded acknowledgements letting the person in over REST and sockets; deemed consent by conduct held back by `purpose_conduct_unstoppable`; and the contact's rules agreeing in `contactProblems()` and `set_dpo_contact()` on one list of samples), with NTI-01, NTI-03 (publishing refused before the contact and allowed after it, before v0) and NTI-04 extended; in the sign-in suite, XFR-I8 (the break-glass re-clear) and XFR-I9 (a stated fact reverted with "Save and keep cleared"). The harness's `bootstrapClearance()` takes `breakGlass`, `reviewOn` and `facts`, and `s.publishNotice()` records a fictional contact first.
    - **Unit:** DCS-01 to DCS-06 (`packages/shared/src/dpo-contact.test.ts`), NTG-14 to NTG-17 and the re-pins of NTG-01, 02, 04, 06, 07, 09 and 11, NTA-02 and NTA-06, NTS-06 and NTS-07.
    - **Web:** CTW-01 to CTW-05 (`apps/web/src/dpo/contact-model.test.ts`), NTW-11 to NTW-13, DPP-03, DPP-06 and RO-01.
    - **e2e (PD-82):** DPP-E7 (the contact page) and DPP-E8 (the record dialog) on fulfilled answers, with DPP-E6, DPP-E4, NTE-01 (Settings' contact on a fulfilled `GET /api/notice`) and SE-05, whose api helper records a contact so that version 1 prints it to Kelvin. Nothing in the chromium project writes a contact or records an acknowledgement for someone else (SI-10's own in-app acknowledgement of version 0 aside), the run still spends 23 of its 25 codes, and there are no new visual baselines.
    - **Guards:** RB-01 to RB-05 (`scripts/guards/runbooks.test.ts`), the two new writers' seams in `api-test-overrides.test.ts`, and design-ids to PD-86.
    - **Upgrade (PD-83):** 0011 rides 0010's step, `UP_TO=0010 scripts/check-migrations.sh && scripts/check-notice-upgrade.sh`: NTU-06 (the acknowledgement the DPO recorded before it keeps no day or note and still counts, and both new checks are validated), NTU-07 (no contact and no link on upgraded data, 0010's publisher gone and the new one `hearth_app`'s, and the contact's writer working over it) and NTU-08 (every clearance unchanged under the widened checks, both validated, and the bootstrap's function refusing over upgraded data with the marker and without it). The one row shape 0010 leaves that the step's data lacks, a generated version linking no contact, is DCD-10's.
48. **Open points for the owner (PR 3a)**, listed in the PR. The owner confirmed every default and answered the open questions on 24 Oct 2026, as recommended: no change for the re-clear's grant race (Qodo's finding on #34; C0.16's grants end the grantee's sessions, and nobody signs in while Twilio is withdrawn, so C0.16's PR re-checks it); *Your rights* points to the PDPC (item 40); the bootstrap's first clearance isn't also refused while a DPO is signed in; the PDPC route stands alone for a complaint about the DPO until a second Owner or DPO exists; and the contact is published outside Hearth with v0, the same day (`publishing-the-notice.md`; PRD Q14 and BUILD-PLAN 1E.2 and C0.19 now say so). The points were:
    - **PD-63 to PD-86,** each with its alternative.
    - **PD-84, v0's timing.** Production's v0 waits for PR 3b, so the org acknowledges the rights section's form and targets once; code doesn't enforce it. The alternative is v0 after PR 3a and v1 after PR 3b, acknowledged twice.
    - **PD-66's deploy window** (item 40), and its stub alternative.
    - **PD-78's facts and cap:** the stated snapshot by default, and 90 days.
    - **PD-79's lock and bounds:** every DPO's row locked, an idle session counted as live (fail closed), and the function's own timeouts.
    - **PD-76's residual:** recording tells the DPO one person's standing.
    - **PD-86's code-held list,** against its alternative: a Notice-tab warning that names each such purpose and links the procedure, leaving the choice to the DPO.
    - **CLAUDE.md's new exception for access requests** (item 46). If the owner declines, the sentence is dropped before merge, and `access-request.md`'s step 3 reads instead: "Pending the owner's decision (TECH-SPEC T22 item 48): answer from what the app shows, and tell the requester in writing what is still to come and by when."
    - **Open questions:** whether *Your rights* should also say people may complain to the PDPC (PD-74); whether 90 days is right for the re-clear's review date; whether the bootstrap's first clearance should also be refused while a DPO is signed in (PD-77's alternative); whether the PDPC route alone is acceptable for a complaint about the DPO until a second Owner or DPO exists (OD-14); and when the contact is published outside Hearth, where PRD Q14's M0, BUILD-PLAN 1E.2's "before the C1 pilot" and C0.19's exit differ (`publishing-the-notice.md` says with v0, the same day).

    The PR's unticked "Before merging" steps also cover the owner's read of migration 0011, the widened `bootstrap_transfer_clearance()` included; the DPO's confirmation of the new copy (the notice's under `c09.2`, the contact page, the record dialog and Settings › Privacy); and the DPO's read of the nine files in `docs/runbooks/`.
49. **What PR 3b and later steps inherit from PR 3a.** Each is also in its BUILD-PLAN row.
    - **PR 3b (0012): built, items 50 to 62.** The plan, and where each part landed:
      - the request-handling purpose, active from the start and unpinned (OD-17; item 51);
      - one case table, whose request details aren't Rule 9 material (OD-06) and which the DPO alone reads (OD-14), the target of a foreign key from `processing_restrictions.case_id`, with its place in the lock order (items 52 and 59; its steps are a second table, appended);
      - the form and its kinds, all taken as intake and handled by hand (OD-07), in Settings › Privacy straight after the contact and outside `NOTICE_FREE` (OD-13; item 56);
      - the DPO's queue and its in-app indicator (OD-22), whose count is the session answer's `requests`, which `RegisterLinks` reads itself (`useSession()`) and shows on the Requests link with a `CountBadge`, its `badges` prop still carrying only the Notice link's badge (item 57; the count also shows on the avatar button and "Data protection");
      - reply targets as `dpo_settings` columns, which the DPO alone changes within a range fixed in code, a change making the notice out of date (OD-08), and business days as weekdays in the org's zone (OD-09; item 55);
      - requester tracking, of the cases they made only (OD-15), and a requester without an account kept as a name, one channel and a note of the identity check (OD-16; items 54 and 56: the check is a step on the case, for every requester);
      - the rights section's form and targets, through `rightsBlocks()` under a new generator id (`c09.3`, item 58);
      - the runbooks' "log it" steps and the README's records section, moved to the queue (item 60), and PDPA-04's reply template, for withdrawals only, filled from the published notice (item 58; PRD PDPA-04 now reads "Hearth fills a reply template"). Reopening and search of closed cases are left to C6.1 (item 62).

      PR 3a's seams for them, and how each was used: `dpo_settings` takes the target columns, and `set_dpo_contact()` gains a one-line copy of them; `publish_notice()` already locks, compares and links the settings version, so neither its signature nor the lock order changes; `NoticeChanges` has room for `targets`; `GET /api/admin/notice/people` was not used for intake, which reads its own `GET /api/admin/requests/people` (current and former people), since the notice's list stays active-only (items 43 and 57); `outbox_events_type` is untouched, so PR 3b's case events widen it once; and PR 3a spent no e2e codes, leaving 2 of 25.
    - **The bootstrap PR (built: items 63 to 71):** the re-clear's recovery mode (item 44), and v0 published after PR 3b (PD-84).
    - **C0.13:** an alarm on `register.transfer_cleared` audit rows with `details.by = 'break_glass'`, beside the log alarms.
    - **C0.14:** a DPO alert on a new or nearly due case (OD-22), built on PR 3b's case events; until then, PR 3b's indicator and the runbooks' daily routine (item 46).
    - **C0.16:** whether to name those outstanding (the record dialog shows none); exporting recorded acknowledgements with their day and note; moving `/dpo/contact` under `/admin`; a second DPO, the re-clear's best prevention; and a re-check of the re-clear's grant race (item 48).
    - **C0.18:** Help mirrors the contact (OD-12).
    - **C1.11:** timed dry runs of `docs/runbooks/`, updated for the Rule 9 service.
    - **C2.11:** the restriction writer, which turns a withdrawal by hand into a recorded restriction.
    - **C6.1:** the consoles that replace the request runbooks, and removing CLAUDE.md's access-request exception (item 46).
50. **Scope of PR 3b (OD-05).** PR 3b, with migration 0012, adds:
    - the request-handling purpose, active from the start and unpinned (item 51);
    - one case table and its steps, the DPO's alone and the requester's own, reached only through checked functions, with `processing_restrictions.case_id`'s foreign key (item 52);
    - a reference for every case, which people outside Hearth quote (item 53);
    - requesters current, former or without an account, six kinds, eight steps and six outcomes, with the identity rule (item 54);
    - the DPO's reply targets, and due days computed from raw values in business or calendar days (item 55);
    - the form and "Your requests" on Settings › Privacy (item 56);
    - the DPO's queue at `/dpo/requests`, the case sheet, intake and the count of requests needing attention (item 57);
    - `c09.3`'s *Your rights*, naming the form and stating the targets, and PDPA-04's reply template for a withdrawal (item 58);
    - the case events, the audit filter, and no consumer reading the outbox's actor (item 59);
    - the runbooks moved to the queue (item 60).

    It leaves out, each in its BUILD-PLAN row (item 62): any alert, push, SMS or email (C0.14); a reply template for an objection, and the more-time letter (C6.1); search and paging of closed cases beyond the 100 most recent, and reopening (C6.1); live updates of the count (C0.14); replies written in Hearth (C1's chat or C6.1); attachments (no step yet: a later one, under CLAUDE.md's S3 rules); incidents and investigations with their own readers (C1.11); retention of case records and `isHeld()` over a refused copy (C0.10, C2.9); Help carrying the form (C0.18); and a holiday list (SHEET-12, P2). 0012 updates and deletes no row, but what rows already there read changes: every org gains `request_handling`'s version 1, every `dpo_settings` version reads the targets' defaults, `read_audit_events()` leaves a request's rows to a DPO reader, and `outbox_events_type` accepts two more types. So it has its own upgrade check (item 61).
51. **The request-handling purpose (OD-17; PD-87).**
    - **What it is.** `request_handling`, "Handling questions, complaints and requests about personal data", after `backups` in `PURPOSES`: not AI, needing no switch, unpinned and in `ACTIVE_FROM_START`, like `security_audit` and `backups`. No flag names it, it isn't `CONDUCT_WITHDRAWABLE`, and `DOES_NOT_EXEMPT` lists it when active.
    - **Rows.** 0012 replaces `processing_register_defaults()` with the same signature, so its revoke stands, and gives every org already there version 1, active, recording nothing; a new org gets it from `orgs_add_registers()`. No existing row changes. Check 6 holds it to `PURPOSES` and `ACTIVE_FROM_START` both ways (RGS-19 and RGS-20), and `check-revocation-upgrade.sh` asserts every org's rows after every migration.
    - **Its basis is the DPO's.** Hearth asserts none (PD-06): publishing refuses until the DPO records a basis and a retention, the purposes page's ordinary problem with its fix link. OD-17 expects another exception or requirement in law, citing the provision. The harness and the e2e helper record a fictional one.
    - **No gate asks for it in PR 3b.** The form, the queue and the targets sit behind no flag and no purpose gate, since a privacy control is never gated (T17 item 10) and the right to make a request mustn't depend on the register's state. So in dev and non-prod the form takes personal data under it before the next publish lists it; production doesn't, because v0 follows PR 3b and lists it (PD-84). C0.14's alert consumer serves it, so the gate refuses that consumer until a published version lists it.
    - **It can't be stopped for one person.** Handling their own request needs it, and a refused copy's records are kept (s22A), so its row in `withdrawal-by-hand.md` says so (RB-05 needs nothing more).
52. **Cases (OD-06, OD-14; PD-88).**
    - **Two tables.** `cases` holds what was received: the reference (item 53), a client key (item 56), the kind it came as, how it came (`hearth`, `email`, `letter`, `phone`, `in_person` or `other`), the day received in the org's zone, when and by whom it was logged, the requester (item 54), what they want (the requester's own words, or the DPO's summary for one they logged) and the settings version whose targets were stated when it arrived (item 55); and its closure, written once: the day, the kind it was closed as, the outcome, a note, who and when. `case_steps` holds every later step, numbered from 1 per case and only appended.
    - **What was received never changes.** `cases_fixed()` refuses any change but the closure, once and all together, for every writer, the owner and a superuser included, and `case_steps_unchanged()` refuses every update of a step. A received day moved later would look on time (CLAUDE.md, Database: rows that carry history, and stored context).
