    - **No grant to any role.** Neither table is granted to `hearth_app`, a job role or `hearth_worker`. Every read and write is one of 0012's checked definer functions: the DPO's re-check the DPO `FOR SHARE` through `cases_check_dpo()` (PD-110, its own message: "Only the Data Protection Officer can see or work on requests."), and the requester's filter on `app.user_id`. Both tables have row level security with the one org policy (015 pins it), a second line that would bind any future grantee; each definer filters by the org the server set itself (RQD-13). One route bug can't show every complaint, as it could with a table grant and RLS that is org-wide.
    - **No subject column.** A case names nobody but its requester, so tracking can't show a case to someone it is only about (OD-15), and no index of who was complained about exists.
    - **Kept, never deleted here.** No role holds DELETE, and the foreign keys from `case_steps` and `processing_restrictions` refuse one. Retention is `request_handling`'s, which the DPO records, applied by C0.10's and C2.9's deletion code, which checks holds and logs each deletion (PDPA-09).
    - **`processing_restrictions.case_id`** (items 7 and 20) is now a composite foreign key, `processing_restrictions_case`, to the org's own case, validated in place over a table with no writer. C2.11's writer also checks that the case is a withdrawal or objection whose requester is the restriction's person.
    - **Not Rule 9 material (OD-06).** 0004's and 0005's comments say the case table "comes with the Rule 9 module and has no hearth_app grants". This is that table, and no role holds a grant on it, but request details aren't Rule 9 material, so `hearth_app` reaches it through the DPO's and the requester's checked functions. Content read under a case from C1.11 stays in C1.11's own store and is never written here; a case note holds the DPO's own working notes. Merged migrations can't change, so this item and CLAUDE.md restate it.
    - **The two logs' `case_id`.** `audit_events.case_id` and `data_access_events.case_id` stay plain ids for the logged case of a privileged read, which C1.11 fills with these cases' ids. A case's own audit rows name it by target (`target_type 'case'`) and leave `case_id` empty, so nothing keyed on `case_id` reads them as Rule 9 reads (PD-100). Neither log gets a foreign key: both outlive cases, and `data_access_events` is partitioned.
53. **The reference (PD-104).**
    - **Its shape.** Eight symbols drawn by the database (`new_case_reference()`, the column's default, granted to nobody) from an alphabet with no vowel, no Y and no 0, 1 or L, the first and fifth always a letter. So none spells a word or a name, holds more than three digits in a row, or is shaped like a phone number, a code or an account id (RB-03). It comes from `gen_random_uuid()`'s strong random bytes, never `random()`, which a session's `setseed()` makes repeatable; it says nothing about the case, its order or how many there are; it is unique in the org (`cases_org_reference`) and never changes. The column holds it in upper case with no hyphen (`cases_reference`); screens show `XXXX-XXXX`, unbroken at the hyphen, with `translate="no"` and a spelled-out accessible name.
    - **A clash draws again.** Each writer inserts with `on conflict do nothing` and draws again, up to 10 times, then raises 40001 (409 `try_again`) and writes nothing. There is no 23505, no counter and no lock slot, and a per-org number, which would reveal how many cases exist, was refused for that reason too.
    - **Outside Hearth, never inside its machinery.** People quote the reference: the requester's confirmation and tracking, the DPO's queue, case sheet and replies outside Hearth, the withdrawal template, and the break-glass log's citation ("PDPA access request <reference>", never the requester's name or number). Hearth's own machinery uses the case's uuid: audit rows, outbox ids, job payloads, logs, gateway frames, foreign keys and the one path parameter. Upper case is deliberate: `is_ids_only()` refuses it, so it can never ride in an outbox row's ids or a job's payload, and no catalogue schema can declare one.
    - **Never proof of identity.** Anyone who sees an email can quote it, so the DPO replies only to the channel the case records, or one the organisation already holds for a current member, after checking identity (item 54).
54. **Requesters, kinds, steps and outcomes (OD-16; PD-89, PD-90, PD-91).**
    - **Requesters.** Sent from the form: the session's person, with no channel, since Hearth collects no reply address (PD-96). Logged by the DPO: a person of the organisation, current or former, picked from a list that includes leavers (never the DPO themselves), with one channel to reach them, required once they have left, since a leaver writes from a channel the organisation may no longer hold (PDPA-13, PPL-01), and optional otherwise; or someone without an account, as a name and one channel (email, phone or post, in the DPO contact's shapes: OD-16's minimum). `log_case()` reads the person's row `FOR SHARE` after the DPO's rows, because it refuses a leaver without a channel (CLAUDE.md, Database: a refusal because a row has ended). A member who leaves after their case is logged has no channel on it, since what was received never changes; the DPO records how to reach them in a note.
    - **Six kinds** (OD-07): a question, a complaint, a copy of my data (`access`), a correction, withdrawing consent and an objection. A copy and a correction count calendar days, the rest business days.
    - **Eight steps,** each dated in the org's zone from the day received to today: told them it was received (`acknowledged`); checked who they are (`identity_checked`, one line on how, never an ID number, a copy of an ID document, an old Hearth number or a PIN); replied (the reply the request waits for; a holding message is a note); more time (`extended`); changed the kind (`reclassified`); a note; and, after a refused copy, the PDPC review starting and ending (the step picker's hint for the end says to record it only once the review, any reconsideration and any appeal have all ended, since the refusal's hold is read from it). At most 100 steps a case, a cap that never refuses the closure (DB-03). A case's revision is its number of steps, plus one once closed, and every step is sent from the revision the sheet showed.
    - **The identity rule (PD-91).** A copy or a correction can't be closed as answered until an identity step exists. The check is recorded on the case sheet with the day it was made, never taken at intake (PPL-14), so a check made before the request was logged keeps its real day. Nothing is released or changed on a session alone, since for a Member the SMS code is the only factor (D46).
    - **Reclassification (PD-90).** A misfiled kind is changed by a step, keeping how it came, its received day and its reference. Its due day is never later than under the kind it came as, nor before the next business day after the reclassification (PPL-08, item 55).
    - **More time** only for a copy or a correction, by its kind now, to a day after the current due day and at most 90 days after receipt, recorded only when the requester was told in writing on or before the current due day (regulation 5 of the PDP Regulations 2021, for a request under s21 or s22 alike; PPL-04). A later notice is a note, so a late case never turns on time.
    - **Six outcomes:** answered, refused, withdrawn by them, a duplicate, not a request, and logged in error. Refused only for a copy, a correction or an objection, by the kind it was closed as, with a note saying why: withdrawal is the person's right (s16), and a question or a complaint is answered, not refused. Logged in error only for a case the DPO logged, and it takes the case out of the requester's tracking. A refused copy's closure (`outcome 'refused'`, `closed_kind 'access'`, `closed_on`) and its review steps are the raw facts C0.10's holds read (s22A and regulation 8 of the PDP Regulations 2021; PDPA-09): the data is held for at least 30 days after the refusal and, if the requester applies to the PDPC for a review, until that review, any reconsideration and any appeal have all ended, whichever is later, so the DPO records the review's end only then (`access-request.md` step 7). A reconsideration or an appeal belongs to the open review episode; a new start after an end is a fresh application (T23 item 11). There is no reopening: a follow-up is a new case with its own received day.
55. **Reply targets, business days and due days (OD-08, OD-09, OD-26; PD-92, PD-93, PD-94, PD-112).**
    - **The targets.** Two `dpo_settings` columns: `target_business_days`, from 5 to 10 (default 10), for withdrawals, objections, questions and complaints, and `target_calendar_days`, from 14 to 30 (default 30), for copies and corrections, the ranges OD-26 settled (`REPLY_TARGETS` in `packages/shared/src/dpo-contact.ts`, which RQD-11 and DCS-07 hold equal to 0012's checks). The DPO may promise sooner than OD-08's figures, never later, and never so short that a mistyped figure, once published, makes every request received under it overdue at once. A constant default adds the columns without rewriting a row, so every version recorded before 0012 reads 10 and 30.
    - **Their writer.** `set_reply_targets(seen, business_days, calendar_days, request_id, device_id, ip)` returns `(version, changed)`. The server calls it after `can()` allows `notice.targets.set` (the DPO, with a fresh PIN; a setup use before v0, as the contact is). It re-checks the DPO (`registers_check_dpo()`, beside the contact's writer), takes the latest version `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, refuses before any contact is recorded (HF006: the targets are kept with it) and a stale `seen` (HF005), checks the range (HF006), writes nothing for the same figures, and otherwise inserts the next version with the contact copied and one audit row, `notice.targets_set`, giving each figure's old and new value. `set_dpo_contact()`, replaced with the same signature, copies the targets (a first record takes 10 and 30), so every version is whole. No outbox event: nothing acts on it until the next published version (PD-72's reason). The route, `POST /api/admin/notice/targets/set` (`setTargets()` in `compliance/routes.ts`, beside the contact's), runs `targetsProblems()` first and answers with the contact's codes (`register_changed`, `register_invalid`). `/dpo/contact` shows them in a "Reply targets" card (item 58).
    - **A due day is computed, never stored** (PD-92), by one SQL function, `case_due()`, over the pure `case_due_on()`, from raw values. Business days count weekdays from the day after receipt in the org's time zone, public holidays counted as business days, so a due day errs early (OD-09); calendar days are receipt plus the target. The target is the shorter of the one stated when the request was received and the current one. A reclassified case takes the earlier of its two kinds' days, the current kind's never before the next business day after the reclassification. The latest more-time date replaces it while the kind now is a copy or a correction. Overdue means after the due day, in the org's zone. Every answer that shows, orders by or counts a due day goes through it, so the queue, tracking and the count never disagree, and the web does no date arithmetic: each answer carries the org's `today`.
    - **The promise is stored context** (PD-93). `cases.stated_settings_version`, a foreign key to `dpo_settings` (`cases_stated_settings`), is the settings version whose targets the notice in force at receipt stated: for the form, the latest published version's link; for a logged case, the link of the version published before the start of the day received, in the org's zone; null when that version's generator states no targets (`c09.1`, `c09.2` or a stand-in: `notice_states_targets()` in SQL, `TARGETLESS_GENERATORS` and `printsTargets()` in `packages/shared`, which RQD-09 and NTS-10 pin together).
    - **A shortened target re-applies; a lengthened one only where none was stated** (DB-08). Taking the shorter of the stated and current targets re-applies a shortened target to open requests, as CLAUDE.md's rule on tunable settings asks, but never a lengthened one to a request whose receipt stated targets, so no stated promise moves later. A request whose receipt stated none (logged as received before v0, or sent under `c09.1`, `c09.2` or a stand-in) follows the current recorded (not yet published) target both ways, within the 5 to 10 and 14 to 30 ranges, so never past OD-08's figures. CLAUDE.md names that narrowing.
    - **A reply on time ends the clock** (PD-112). When a case's latest replied or more-time step, by number, is a replied step dated on or before its due day (`case_replied_in_time()`), the case never reads as late or overdue afterwards, in tracking, in the queue ("Replied <day>") or in the count, so a withdrawal kept open to confirm it took effect never turns overdue. More time recorded after the reply starts the clock again; a reply only after the due day leaves the case overdue until it is closed. "Replied" is the DPO's word, and the step picker's hint says a holding message is a note.
    - **Later.** SHEET-12's org holiday list (P2) refines business days, in `case_add_business_days()` (BUILD-PLAN's SHEET-12 row).
56. **The form and tracking (OD-07, OD-13, OD-15, OD-25; PD-96, PD-97).**
    - **Where.** Settings › Privacy, in its lazy chunk (`settings/RequestsSection.tsx`, `strings/requests.ts`), in this order (NTW-15): the DPO's contact, **Questions and requests**, **Your requests**, the privacy notice and your acknowledgements. Nothing that was there moves (item 41). The Privacy line on `/settings` names questions and requests, so someone looking for how to complain or ask for their data opens it. Help links the form from C0.18 (OD-12).
    - **Who.** Any active person once they have acknowledged the current notice: `case.send_own` and `case.list_own` are self actions outside `NOTICE_FREE`, with no re-authentication (OD-13). A leaver, someone without an account or someone who hasn't acknowledged uses the contact the notice prints, and the DPO logs it (item 57).
    - **The form.** Six rows, one a kind, each opening a sheet with the kind's intro; the target line from the published version's `targets` (no line, never an invented figure, when it prints none or can't be read); "They reply outside Hearth, by a channel your organisation already has for you."; a 16 px text area with `dir="auto"`, up to 4,000 characters, optional for a copy, with a hint against ID numbers but no refusal (a requester's words are theirs, PD-102); and "Sent as <name>." There is no reply address, so a copy never goes to an address typed into a borrowed phone, and no re-authentication: a code to the same number adds nothing against a SIM swap, and the identity rule guards release instead. The confirmation, "They aim to reply by <day>." with the reference, stays in the sheet until Done, which focuses the new row; it never says the DPO was told (OD-22). Nothing is kept in browser storage, and closing a written sheet asks before discarding. A sheet the browser closes with no decision (Chrome's second Escape, Android's back gesture) is kept as that kind's draft in the section's memory, with its words, its key and any line saying a send couldn't be confirmed, so reopening the row brings it back and a resend still records at most one request; Discard or a confirmed send clears it, and a reload or leaving Settings drops it (`draftsAfter()` in `settings/requests-model.ts`).
    - **A resend is safe.** A client key, made when the sheet opens and kept while it stays open, is unique per writer (`cases_client_key`), so a resend after a lost answer returns the first case (`changed: false`) and appends nothing. A key returns only a case sent the same way: the form refuses a key that holds a case its sender logged as the DPO, and the intake one that holds their own form request (HF006, 400 `case_invalid`), so a former DPO replaying an old dialog's key gets nothing back (OD-14, OD-15). The sheet says so when it couldn't confirm a send.
    - **The cap (OD-25).** At most 10 open requests per requester, counted plainly with no lock, so a bound against flooding the queue rather than an exact limit: sends at the same moment each count before the others commit, so every one of them may pass, and a burst can leave 9 open plus as many as the api runs at once, at most its pool's 10 transactions per api process (`apps/server/src/db/pool.ts`): 19 with one process, and 10 more for each further one. With 10 open, the rows give way to a line pointing to the contact outside Hearth, which always works.
    - **`submit_case(kind, details, client_key, request_id, device_id, ip)`** reads the requester's row `FOR SHARE`, so a deactivation in progress makes it wait and then refuse; returns a repeat's case; applies the rules and the cap (HF006); stores the day received and the stated settings version; draws the reference; and writes one audit row, `case.submitted`. The route, `POST /api/requests/send` (`sendRequest()` in `compliance/cases.ts`, whose `caseRoutes()` the api registers under `/api/requests`), runs `requestProblems()` first and appends `case.opened` with `{via: 'form'}` when it records.
    - **"Your requests"** (`GET /api/requests`, `read_own_cases()`, in a read-only transaction): the cases where you are the requester, sent in Hearth or logged for you, open first, at most 50 with `more`. Each shows the kind now, a state pill in words (Received, In progress, More time, Closed), the reference, how it came, the day received, the reply's due day while open and the day the DPO last replied; once open past its due day with no reply on time, "Past its target day (<day>).", with the contact and the PDPC when no reply is recorded. Never the words, the DPO's notes, the steps, the outcome or a case closed as logged in error: a member's long session on a shared phone would show them, and the outcome misleads without the reply, which goes outside Hearth. A case that names someone but which someone else made never shows to them. A change shows at the page's next load: there is no frame and no poll.
57. **The queue, the case sheet, intake and the indicator (OD-14, OD-22, OD-24; PD-98, PD-99, PD-102, PD-112).**
    - **Who.** The DPO alone, with a fresh PIN (PD-30): `case.read` covers the reads, and `case.log {person}` and `case.step` the writes, all admin actions. None is a setup use, so the queue opens once the DPO has acknowledged v0. Owners, Admins and Compliance Officers as such see no case, no count and no case's audit row (OD-14, OD-23): every case route answers them 403 `forbidden`, and a deep link shows "This page is for the Data Protection Officer."
    - **The queue.** `/dpo/requests` (`dpo/RequestsScreen.tsx`, a lazy chunk) is the first of the DPO's links (Requests, Purposes, Transfers, Contact, Notice), whose track is now labelled "Data protection". `GET /api/admin/requests` (`read_cases()`, in a read-write transaction because it re-reads the DPO `FOR SHARE`) answers the open cases, soonest due first, at most 200 with the exact total, and the 100 most recently closed with `more`. Each card shows the kind, the reference, the requester (name and department, "Has left", or "No Hearth account"), how it came, the day received, the due line in words ("Due <day>", "Due today", "Overdue since <day>" with an icon, or "Replied <day>") and the state. It never says "No open requests." when it couldn't read them. Search waits for C6.1, and every card shows its reference for the browser's find.
    - **Before v0.** "Data protection" opens `/dpo/purposes`, the first setup task, until the DPO's standing is `acknowledged`, and the queue meanwhile shows "Requests open once the notice is published and you’ve acknowledged it." without sending a case read, which would answer 403 `notice_required` and move the tab to `/notice` in the middle of setup (PPL-02).
    - **The case sheet** (`dpo/CaseSheet.tsx`; `GET /api/admin/requests/:case`, the second path parameter the api registers, always a uuid and never the reference) is held in component state, never a URL (T21 item 5, RO-04), and closing it returns focus to its card. It shows the kind and the reference; the requester, with the channel and address the case records as plain text, and a person requester's id, "Person id (for a break-glass citation)" (PPL-07); how it came; the days; the due line; whether identity has been checked; *What they wrote* or *Your summary*; the steps, oldest first (`read_case_steps()`); "Their other requests" (`read_case_others()`, at most 50, which takes a case and never a person id, so no route carries one); for a withdrawal, "A reply you can copy" (item 58); and **Add a step**, offering only the steps the case allows (`stepsAllowed()`). `add_case_step(the_case, seen, step, on_day, note, extended_to, kind, outcome, request_id, device_id, ip)` takes the case `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, applies item 54's rules and writes one audit row, `case.step_added` or `case.closed`; the route (`POST /api/admin/requests/step`) appends `case.changed` with the new revision. A stale sheet answers 409 `case_changed` and keeps the typed note.
    - **Intake** ("Log a request": `dpo/LogRequest.tsx`, `POST /api/admin/requests/log`, `log_case()`). What it's about; how it came; the day received, from a year back to today; who sent it, either a person picked from `GET /api/admin/requests/people` (current and former, at most 2,000, roster facts only, with "left on <day>" after a leaver's name and, where two read the same, their manager and the day added; two who still read the same can't be picked, and the dialog says to log the request as someone without a Hearth account: PPL-13), with how to reach them, or someone without an account, with a name and one channel; and the DPO's summary. There is no identity field: the new case's sheet opens with "Logged as <reference>. Give them this reference when you reply.", and "Checked who they are" is a step there. A resend with the same client key returns the first case, and the route appends `case.opened` with `{via: 'intake'}` when it records. `notice.people.read`'s list stays active-only (item 43).
    - **People's words are data** (PD-102). A requester's words, an outside requester's details and the DPO's notes are never held to `AI_COPY`, which governs Hearth's own copy and refuses "ask" in every form, so it would refuse ordinary requests. Settings and the DPO's pages are the first screens to show people's words, ahead of C1's chat: as plain text with `dir="auto"`, never markup or links (Rule 8). SQL refuses control characters (but the line break where lines are allowed) and bidirectional-formatting characters in all of them, and an NRIC- or FIN-shaped string in everything the DPO types (OD-16), each refusal naming the field, never the value. Case notes are the DPO's working notes, never content read in Hearth.
    - **The indicator** (OD-22, OD-24; PD-99). `GET /api/session` answers `requests`: from `case_attention()`, the open cases that are new (sent in Hearth with no step) or due within 2 business days, overdue included, leaving out one replied to on time, for a holder of `dpo` whom `can()` would let read the queue and the notice would let through now; null for everyone else, without running the statement. It shows on the avatar button ("Account, <name>, 1 request needs attention"), on the avatar menu's "Data protection" and on the Requests link, each named in words (PPL-05, PPL-12), with no PIN, as the owner decided (OD-24): a number, never a name, a kind or a word. 0 shows nothing and leaves the avatar button's markup byte-equal, so no VS-01 baseline moves. It refreshes with the session and after each DPO case write; nothing pushes it until C0.14. "Within 2 business days" means a case due on a Monday counts on the Thursday before, so a weekend never hides one.
58. **The notice under `c09.3`, and PDPA-04's reply template (PD-95, PD-105).**
    - **`c09.3`.** `NOTICE_GENERATOR` becomes `c09.3`, which NTG-09 pins by digest beside `c09.1` and `c09.2`. `rightsBlocks(org, contact, targets)` still writes *Your rights* in one place. Its second paragraph names the form first: "To do any of these, or to raise a question or a complaint about how your personal data is handled, use the form in Settings › Privacy in Hearth, where you can follow what happens to it, or contact the Data Protection Officer of <org>. You don't need a Hearth account to contact them, so it still works after you leave." A new paragraph after the contact's items states the targets: "The Data Protection Officer aims to reply to a question, a complaint, a withdrawal or an objection within <n> business days, counting Monday to Friday from the day after it reaches them, and to a request for a copy of your data or to have it corrected within <m> calendar days, weekends and public holidays included. If they need longer for a copy or a correction, they tell you in writing within that time when they will reply." It says "aims", because a target isn't a guarantee; it names the form in words only; and it never says the DPO is alerted. Objection is a form kind and named in the targets paragraph, but not a right in the first sentence, since the PDPA grants no general one. The withdraw branch drops `c09.2`'s "Hearth can't take a withdrawal in the app yet" and names the form; no target is repeated there, so item 42's "No reply target is stated until PR 3b" is met with *Your rights* stating it once. The DPO confirms the words.
    - **What a version states.** A version's `sources.targets`, and `GET /api/notice`'s `targets`, are the targets its linked settings version holds, only when its generator prints them (`printsTargets()`), so a `c09.1` or `c09.2` version, or a stand-in, states none whatever its row now reads. `NoticeChanges.targets` is `added` after a version that printed none, `changed` when both print and differ, and `same` otherwise, and `reworded` needs it `same`. `/notice`'s callout says the targets were added or have changed, and the Notice tab's changes list likewise.
    - **Out of date.** A targets change makes a new settings version, which `publish_notice()` already locks, compares and links (item 40), so the notice reads out of date with no new argument or lock; its HF007 message names the contact when only the targets moved, which no screen shows (the route answers `notice_changed`). The contact's pill compares the contact's facts (`sameContact()`), so a targets-only change leaves it "In the published notice", and `targetsStated` answers the same four states for the targets on `/dpo/contact`'s "Reply targets" card, which offers a change (two figures, the PIN inline, and the warning that saving makes the notice out of date once a version is published). Every org's notice reads out of date once 0012 deploys (a new generator, and `request_handling` needs a basis), as with `c09.2`; production's v0 is published after PR 3b (PD-84), so the org acknowledges the form and the targets once.
    - **The withdrawal reply template** (PDPA-04; PD-105). `withdrawalReply()` (`packages/shared/src/notice-text.ts`) fills fixed text, in the DPO's voice, from the latest published version's active purposes and their bases: the reference first; which purposes stop (those on deemed consent by conduct) and which continue, on their stated basis (`REPLY_BASIS_PHRASES`, which NTG-22 holds to the bases `basisItem()` names); the route to object to a purpose on legitimate interests; the PDPC line; and the contact title that version printed. The case sheet shows it for a withdrawal, by its kind now, with Copy, once a version that prints a contact is published. No model writes it (D41); the DPO copies it, edits it outside Hearth and sends it. There is none for an objection, whose reply is the DPO's decision, and the more-time letter is C6.1's. In C0 `CONDUCT_WITHDRAWABLE` is empty, so it always takes the branch where nothing stops, which still states each purpose's consequence, as PDPA-04 asks.
59. **Writes, refusals, audit, events, logs, the lock order and access (PD-100, PD-101, PD-103, PD-106, PD-110, PD-111, PD-113).**
    - **Writers and reads.** `submit_case()`, `log_case()` and `add_case_step()` are the case tables' only writers, and `set_dpo_contact()` and `set_reply_targets()` `dpo_settings`'; each re-checks its actor in tables and writes one audit row (ADM-06). The reads (`read_own_cases()`, `read_cases()`, `read_case()`, `read_case_steps()` and `read_case_others()`) and `case_attention()` write nothing. A read's `max_rows` outside its bounds raises 2201W, which no refusal map maps, so it is a logged 500 and never "This request isn't there".
    - **Grants.** `hearth_app` executes those ten functions and holds nothing on either table. Every new function has execute revoked from PUBLIC; the internal helpers (`new_case_reference()`, `notice_states_targets()`, `case_add_business_days()`, `case_due_on()`, `case_kind_now()`, `case_due()`, `case_replied_in_time()` and `cases_check_dpo()`) and the two triggers are granted to nobody, so `coverage.ts` lists no helper. Nothing goes to a job role or `hearth_worker`. `processing_register_defaults()`, `set_dpo_contact()` and `read_audit_events()` are replaced with their signatures, owners and grants.
    - **Refusals.** No new SQLSTATE (PD-103); `apps/server/src/db/sqlstate.ts` gains comments only. The case routes answer two new api codes, `case_changed` and `case_invalid` (`CASE_ERRORS`, in the sign-in suite's sweep of every code), so a requester never reads a register's copy.

      | SQLSTATE | New in 0012 | Answer |
      |---|---|---|
      | HF005 | `add_case_step()`: a stale revision | 409 `case_changed` |
      | HF006 | the rules of `submit_case()`, `log_case()` and `add_case_step()`: the cap, a leaver without a channel and more time told late among them | 400 `case_invalid` |
      | 23514 on `cases_*` or `case_steps_*` | the tables' checks, a backstop | 400 `case_invalid` |
      | HF005, HF006, 23514 on `dpo_settings_*` | `set_reply_targets()`: stale, or a second writer of one version; no contact, or a figure out of range | 409 `register_changed`, 400 `register_invalid` |
      | 22023 | an unknown case, or another org's; `log_case()`'s person not of the org (a race: `can()` refuses first) | 404 `not_found` |
      | 2201W | a read's bound, a programming error | unmapped: a logged 500 |
      | 42501 | `cases_check_dpo()`; logging yourself | 403 `forbidden` |
      | 42501 | `submit_case()`: the requester left in a race | 401 `signed_out` |
      | 40001 | a reference that clashed 10 times | 409 `try_again` |
      | 55P03, 40P01 | a lock timeout, a deadlock | 409 `try_again` |

      Every message a case function raises is fixed text, never an input (RQD-14). Each form words its own problems from `packages/shared/src/cases.ts`, which mirrors 0012's rules in their refusal order, before it sends, and each route runs the same rules before the database.
    - **Audit (PD-100).** `case.submitted` (the requester), `case.logged`, `case.step_added` (`{step}`), `case.closed` (`{outcome}`) and `notice.targets_set` (each figure's old and new value), each with ids and codes only: never a kind, a word, a name, an address, a note or the reference. A case's rows name it by target and leave `case_id` empty (item 52). As the owner decided (OD-23), `read_audit_events()` returns a row whose target is `case` only to a reader who also holds `dpo`, read `FOR SHARE`, whatever roles the org lets read the log (D45), so a chosen reader never learns who wrote to the DPO, how often or when; C0.16's viewer and export go through it or apply its filter. The PDPC route stands for oversight of the DPO's handling.
    - **Events (PD-101).** `case.opened` (`{via: 'form' | 'intake'}`) and `case.changed` (`{revision}`), whose entity is the case, purpose `request_handling`, with no subjects and no snapshot, appended by the api in the writer's transaction; `outbox_events_type` is widened once, as 0012's last statement. No consumer takes them before C0.14, whose alert re-reads the case. `CONTENT_TABLES` stays empty, and RQS-11 holds that no event allowing `request_handling` carries a snapshot, so no indexer can see a case. `coverage.ts` gives each writer its event, exempts the reads and `set_reply_targets()` (with the contact's writer, `DPO_SETTINGS`) and the two triggers (checks), and matches `cases` by `id` and `case_steps` by `case_id`.
    - **The outbox's actor (DB-02; OD-27, PD-111).** A form submit's event names its requester as actor for 14 days (T16 item 10), as `notice.acknowledged` names a person. `hearth_queue` can't read the column; `hearth_job_offboarding` and `hearth_job_revocation` can, through their table-wide grants, and so can the owner. Neither consumer uses an actor, so from PR 3b `readBatch()` (`apps/server/src/outbox/positions.ts`) selects no `actor_id` or `actor_kind`, and `032_worker_roles.sql` pins exactly which roles can still read the column. As the owner decided (OD-27), the first migration after PR 3b's worker is deployed (C0.10's PR 1, 0013, which carried it, since no environment ever ran a worker older than PR 3b: T23 item 16, LH-49) revokes it from both roles and empties that pin; 0012 can't, because the worker still running during the deploy selects it and would fail every round until replaced. C0.14's consumer must not log it.
    - **Logs and frames.** No route path carries a word or a reference; `parse()` never echoes a body; a check violation's failing row sits in `detail`, which the logger redacts; and `SENSITIVE_KEYS` gains `details`, `requester`, `contact` and `note`. No gateway frame, Valkey key or stream carries anything about a case, and the count reaches the shell only in the session answer. RQI-07 plants a marker in every text field and finds it in no log line, audit row, outbox row or session answer.
    - **The lock order:** … `sessions`, then `role_assignments` → `cases` → `processing_register` → `processing_restrictions` → `transfer_register` → `transfer_clearances` → `dpo_settings` → …. `case_steps` rows are only inserted, so they need no slot.
      - A submit reads the requester's person row `FOR SHARE`, then inserts the case; its foreign keys take only KEY SHARE.
      - The DPO's case writers and reads read the DPO's person and role rows `FOR SHARE` (`cases_check_dpo()`). A log then reads its requester's person row `FOR SHARE` (an admin action on someone else: the DPO's rows first). A step or the closure takes the case `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, so of two from one revision the second waits and is refused (RQR-05).
      - A targets change takes the latest `dpo_settings` version `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` after the DPO's rows, as a contact change does, so the two, and a publish, meet only on that row (RQR-07).
      - `read_audit_events()` reads the reader's `dpo` role row `FOR SHARE` after their other rows (RQR-08).
      - **`case_attention()` is the named exception to the `FOR SHARE` rule** (DB-05). It answers null rather than refusing, and reads the DPO's rows with no lock, because it runs in `GET /api/session`'s read-only transaction, where a `FOR SHARE` read fails with 25006. A DPO losing the role at that instant may see one last count, never a name or a word. `read_own_cases()` refuses nothing, so the rule doesn't reach it either.
      - C2.11's restriction writer reads the case it cites `FOR KEY SHARE` before its insert, so `cases` comes before `processing_register` there too (DB-06).
      - **0012's own locks (PD-106).** Its new tables' foreign keys take SHARE ROW EXCLUSIVE on `orgs`, `people` and `principals`, and its backfill ROW EXCLUSIVE on `processing_register`; neither conflicts with a request's row locks. Last, it takes ACCESS EXCLUSIVE on `dpo_settings` and `outbox_events` together, the two tables live requests touch (a publish holds the latest settings row while it appends to the outbox), under 0011's no-wait retry: each attempt `NOWAIT`, 0.1 s apart, up to 100 attempts, then 55P03 and the migrate step is run again. Only then does it alter `dpo_settings`, add the foreign key to it and widen the outbox's check. A write that arrives meanwhile waits at most its own 2 s and answers `try_again` (RQR-09).
    - **Access.** `case.read`, `case.log {person}`, `case.step` and `notice.targets.set` join `ADMIN_ACTIONS`, so each asks for a fresh re-authentication, and `adminCan()` allows each to the DPO alone, `case.log` only for `person: null` or someone else of the org, current or former. `case.send_own` and `case.list_own` join `SELF_ACTIONS`, for any active person. `NOTICE_SETUP` gains `notice.targets.set` alone, and `NOTICE_FREE` is unchanged. `roles.test.ts` holds every persona's row (RQA-01 to RQA-03), and NTA-02 both sets. Nothing here imports `privileged.ts`, and no flag reads any of it. No new function takes free text Hearth shows anyone but the DPO as its own words, so `api-test-overrides.test.ts` needs no new seam.
    - **The source-text guard** (DB-04, PD-113). 0012's and `packages/shared`'s class of bidirectional-formatting characters is written as escapes, never the raw characters, which could reorder how a reviewer reads the migration (Trojan Source). `scripts/guards/source-text.test.ts` (SRC-01) refuses those raw characters (U+202A to U+202E, U+2066 to U+2069) in every text file the repository holds, skipping a file with a NUL byte in its first 8 KB, since Biome doesn't flag them.
60. **The runbooks (PD-109).**
    - **The queue is the record.** It replaces PD-85's private log: every request is a case at `/dpo/requests`, sent from Settings › Privacy or logged with "Log a request", and a case holds what the log held (the day received, how it came, the kind, the requester, how identity was checked, the due day, each step with its date, and the outcome). Every request runbook's "log it" step becomes "log it as a case", and the identity check is a step on it.
    - **Moving in.** Once v0 is published and the DPO has acknowledged it (case actions aren't setup uses), the DPO logs each of the private log's open entries with its original day received (and a leaver's channel), records its earlier steps with their days on its sheet, writes "moved: <reference>" beside it in the private log, and then closes that log under the DPO's retention.
    - **The reference, never the requester.** Replies outside Hearth and the break-glass citation quote the case's reference, and the case sheet gives the person on call the requester's person id. A reference proves nothing, so the DPO replies only to the channel the case records, or one the organisation already holds for a current member, after checking identity.
    - **Reply targets** are set on `/dpo/contact` and printed in the notice. The README says how a due day is counted, and that the queue's day never moves later than the targets stated when the request arrived (one that arrived when none were stated follows the current targets, never past 10 business days or 30 days).
    - **The daily routine until C0.14.** The count shows new and nearly due requests when Hearth is opened; each working day the DPO still checks the channels outside Hearth and logs what came there. Hearth sends no alert.
    - **What changed:** the README; `access-request.md` (their own requests in the reply, "Their other requests", the identity step, more time, and the refusal and review steps); `correction-request.md`; `complaints.md` (the form, the PDPC note, and a reported breach starting `breach.md`); `withdrawal-by-hand.md` (the reply template, and `request_handling`'s row); and `publishing-the-notice.md` (the targets and `request_handling`'s basis before v0, and the private log moved in after it). The two Twilio runbooks' note in the DPO's log is the DPO's record of a decision, not a request, and stays. RB-03 and RB-04 hold over every change, and the DPO confirms each.
61. **What CI checks (PR 3b; PD-107, PD-108).**
    - **DB tests:** `175_request_cases.sql` (RQD-01 to RQD-14 and RQD-16 to RQD-18: no privilege on either table for any runtime role, and only the ten functions for `hearth_app`; every check and trigger; `submit_case()`, `log_case()`, `add_case_step()` and the reads for every ACC-10 persona, with each rule, the client key, the cap, the identity rule, more time told late and the step cap sparing the closure; the due-day functions on a table of samples; the count; the restriction foreign key; the org boundary; no input in any message; the reference's draws and retry; the purpose's rows; and `cases_check_dpo()`'s message); RQD-15 in `050_read_audit_events.sql` (OD-23's filter); DCD-11 to DCD-15 in `165_dpo_settings.sql` (the target columns, `set_reply_targets()` for every role, `set_dpo_contact()` copying them, a publish linking the version a targets change made, and a `c09.2` version stating none); and 015, 030, 032 (which roles can read the outbox's actor), 150, 160 and the fixture's four case rows.
    - **Races** (`apps/server/scripts/check-concurrency.ts`, an org of its own): a submit against the requester's deactivation, both orders (RQR-01); two submits with one client key, with the first committed and rolled back, and a submit and a log by the DPO under one key, each order (RQR-02); two references drawn alike (RQR-03); a log, a step and a queue read by a DPO being deactivated or losing the role (RQR-04); two steps or two closures from one revision (RQR-05); a log against its member's deactivation, with and without a channel (RQR-06); a targets change against a publish, a contact change and another targets change (RQR-07); an audit read against a `dpo` revocation (RQR-08); and 0012 applied while a publish, a register write and `GET /api/notice` run (RQR-09).
    - **Integration:** the suite `cases` (`apps/server/src/compliance/cases.integration.test.ts`, RQI-01 to RQI-12), whose RQI-01 is named "C0.9 Done: a request reaches the DPO's queue"; in the notice suite, NTI-01's eight new routes, NTI-03's walk before v0 (the targets set, `request_handling`'s basis recorded, v0 published under `c09.3`, then the queue), NTI-13 and NTI-14; and `CASE_ERRORS` in the sign-in suite's sweep. The harness gains `sendRequest()`, `logRequest()` and `addAuditReader()`.
    - **Unit:** RQS-01 to RQS-11, RGS-20, DCS-07 to DCS-09, NTS-08 to NTS-10 and NTG-18 to NTG-22 in `packages/shared`; RQA-01 to RQA-03 and NTA-02 in `packages/access`; and RQV-01 to RQV-09 (`apps/server/src/compliance/cases.test.ts`), with the targets' route, the notice's sources, the session's count and the consumers' batch with no actor beside the code.
    - **Web:** RQW-01 to RQW-11 (`apps/web/src/settings/requests-model.test.ts` and `apps/web/src/dpo/requests-model.test.ts`), CTW-06 and CTW-07, NTW-14 and NTW-15, and the markup, nav, kit and router cases.
    - **e2e (PD-108):** DPP-E9 (the queue, the case sheet and the indicator) and DPP-E10 (intake and the targets card) in `dpo.spec.ts`, and NTE-02 (the form and tracking), on fulfilled answers, so nothing in the chromium project writes a case or a target, the run still spends 23 of its 25 codes, and no visual baseline changes. DPP-E1, E2, E4 to E7, NTE-01 and SE-05 follow the new link, label, purpose and targets.
    - **Guards:** SRC-01 (`scripts/guards/source-text.test.ts`, item 59) and design-ids to PD-113; RB-04 resolves the runbooks' new code paths; `api-test-overrides.test.ts` and `web-source.test.ts` hold unchanged.
    - **Upgrade (PD-107):** 0012 has its own step, `UP_TO=0012 scripts/check-migrations.sh && scripts/check-cases-upgrade.sh`, because the targets' defaults change what every `dpo_settings` version already there reads, which the notice step's data, built at 0010, has none of. Over the seed and two made-up orgs built at 0011 through 0011's own functions, it checks RQU-01 to RQU-08: every org's `request_handling` version 1, with every other register row unchanged; every settings row reading 10 and 30 and otherwise unchanged, with both checks validated; the schema digest; both writers over upgraded rows; the restriction foreign key validated; a Compliance Officer reader's audit rows unchanged; a `c09.2` version still linking its settings and stating no targets, with a submit under it due ten weekdays on; and the outbox's type check validated over every event. The notice, revocation and sign-in upgrade checks also apply 0012.
62. **Open points for the owner (PR 3b), and what later steps inherit.** The owner answered OD-23 to OD-27 on 26 Oct 2026, accepting every recommendation and every default, PD-87 to PD-113, as written ("Accept all"):
    - **OD-23 (a):** a request's audit rows reach only a reader who holds `dpo`, narrowing D45 for those rows alone (item 59). The cost: a Compliance Officer or Owner reading the log can't oversee the DPO's handling of requests in Hearth; the PDPC route stands for that.
    - **OD-24 (a):** the DPO sees the count without a fresh PIN, a number only, on the avatar button and "Data protection"; the queue itself still needs the PIN (item 57).
    - **OD-25 (a):** at most 10 open requests per person in the form, counted plainly, so a burst sent at the same moment may pass it, up to 9 open plus the api's concurrent transactions (10 per api process, item 56); the contact outside Hearth always works, and the cap's line points to it (item 56).
    - **OD-26 (a):** reply targets from 5 to 10 business days and from 14 to 30 calendar days (item 55). PRD PDPA-04, PDPA-05 and PDPA-07 are reworded with it.
    - **OD-27 (a):** PR 3b stops both consumers reading an outbox event's actor, and the first migration after PR 3b's worker is deployed revokes `outbox_events.actor_id` from the offboarding and revocation roles (item 59; C0.10's PR 1 carried it: T23 item 16, LH-49).

    Still open, for the DPO or a later step: whether 2 business days is the right "nearly due" margin for the count (PD-99), and 90 days the right ceiling on a more-time date (PD-91; the law sets none beyond telling the requester in writing within the time); whether *Your rights* should name the objection as a right (the PDPA grants no general one); the current wording of regulations 5 and 8 of the PDP Regulations 2021 (the Regulations have been amended since 2021), and of s20(3) and s21(3A), which the DPO confirms before the notice states the targets and a case records the hold; and that a DPO's own request from the form lands in their own queue, with the PDPC route for a complaint about how they handle it, as for any complaint about the DPO.

    Accepted residuals, each in the PR: anyone holding `hearth_owner`'s credential or C0.11's break-glass role can read the case tables directly (column encryption is PDPA-12's, P1); a DPO could paste content read under Rule 9 into a note, which the note's hint, the README and `access-request.md` forbid; the sole DPO is also the founder and an Owner (D35), so a complaint about them has no second reader in Hearth, and if they leave, nobody reads open cases until C0.16 grants a new DPO, who then reads every case while the due days keep running; 0012's two locks; the count read with no lock and shown with no PIN; the cap a bound, not an exact limit; an in-app request resting on the session, for a Member the SMS code alone; a mis-picked member seeing a case's kind, state and days until it is closed as logged in error; a member who leaves after their case is logged having no channel on it; "Replied" being the DPO's word; the start-of-day rule's residual, which needs two publishes around one request on one day; `publish_notice()`'s HF007 wording naming the contact when only the targets moved; the form taking data under `request_handling` before the next publish, in non-prod and dev only; every org's notice out of date once 0012 deploys; and no retention enforced before C0.10's and C2.9's code.

    The PR's unticked "Before merging" steps also cover the owner's read of migration 0012 (the replaced `read_audit_events()`, `set_dpo_contact()` and `processing_register_defaults()`, the case tables' grants, none, and triggers, and the outbox's widened check); the DPO's confirmation of the new copy (`c09.3`'s *Your rights* and withdraw branch, the form and its confirmation, "Your requests", the queue, the case sheet, the log dialog, the targets card and the withdrawal reply template) and of regulations 5 and 8's current wording; and the DPO's read of the six changed files in `docs/runbooks/`.
    - **What later steps inherit,** each also in its BUILD-PLAN row:
      - **The bootstrap PR (built: items 63 to 71):** v0 published after PR 3b (PD-84), naming the form and stating the targets.
      - **C0.10 (built: T23):** a retention class for `cases` and `case_steps`, with requesters' channels and addresses; `isHeld()` over a refused copy's closure and review steps (at least 30 days after the refusal and, after an application for a PDPC review, until the step that records the review, any reconsideration and any appeal ended, whichever is later: item 54), by the requester's person row where it is linked, a former employee's included; the deletion code's path for cases (with an index on `processing_restrictions (org_id, case_id)` if it needs one); and OD-27's revoke, which it carried (T23 items 11, 13 and 16).
      - **C0.13:** the log canary over a request's marker; RUM never captures a form field.
      - **C0.14:** the DPO's alert on `case.opened` (`via: 'form'`) and on a nearly due request, through a narrow definer its job role may run for due days, since `hearth_queue` reads no case and no `orgs.time_zone`, with a payload of ids only naming neither the kind, the reference nor the requester; the purpose gate refusing it until a version lists `request_handling`; the requester's inbox; pushing the count live; and never logging an event's actor (C0.10 carried OD-27's revoke: T23 item 16).
      - **C0.16:** the audit viewer and export inherit the case filter (OD-23); `/dpo/requests` moves under `/admin`, with a redirect; a second DPO reads every case, one about the other included; and, if the owner wants it, logging the DPO's reads of the queue (PR 3b logs none: cases aren't content, and the DPO reads them under no Rule 9 case).
      - **C0.18:** Help links the form beside the contact (PRD §6.1).
      - **C1.11:** its Rule 9 console picks a case by its reference and records its id in the logs' `case_id`; reads only against a copy, a correction or a withdrawal, by the kind now; adds incidents and investigations to `cases_kind` with their own readers; and gives its role a narrow read of a case's kind and state, never its words.
      - **C2.9:** the sweep for the cases' retention class that C0.10 declares, with `isHeld()` over a refused copy, held as item 54 says (at least 30 days after the refusal, and through any PDPC review, reconsideration and appeal); nothing deletes a case before it. It calls `delete_case()`, which answers `gone`, `open`, `not_due`, `held`, `cited` or `deleted`, and replaces `retention_period_days()` to read its settings (T23 items 5 and 13).
      - **C2.11:** the restriction writer cites a withdrawal or objection case of the same person, read `FOR KEY SHARE` before the insert.
      - **C6.1:** consoles over the cases (PDPA-05's more-time letter, PDPA-07's correction records, an objection's reply, and restrictions from withdrawals), collectors applying s21(3)'s withholding, search and paging of closed cases, reopening if needed, and replies written in Hearth for the requester to read, if wanted (PD-97).
      - **SHEET-12 (P2):** the org holiday list, which refines `case_add_business_days()` (OD-09, item 55). **A2 onwards:** requests are never indexed or retrieved by any AI feature; no grant reads them, and their events carry no snapshot.
63. **Scope of the bootstrap PR (OD-28, OD-32; PD-114, PD-115, PD-130, PD-136).** It carries C0.9's hand-offs to the first-Owner bootstrap (items 16, 20, 37 and 44) and adds no migration.
    - **The script (PD-114).** `scripts/owner-bootstrap.ts`, the entry, and its modules in `scripts/owner-bootstrap/`: `input.ts` (the documents and every check before connecting), `target.ts` (the target, the login and the migrations), `steps.ts` (every statement, as constants, and one function per mode) and `output.ts` (the one printer), each with its unit tests. TypeScript run as it is on Node 24.12 or later (T2), with a `pg` `Client` (no Kysely: each statement is written out and bound), `zod` (a root devDependency at the server's pin, so the lockfile gains no package) and `@hearth/shared`. A person runs it from a checkout of the commit the database was migrated by, installed with `npm ci --ignore-scripts` before the break-glass session opens: none of its packages needs an install script, and a plain install would run every dependency's on a machine about to hold a path to production.
    - **Not in the server image.** `apps/server/Dockerfile.dockerignore` admits nothing under `scripts/`, so no runtime process can load it (BSG-10). It reads only the environment and prints only safe lines, so the same files could run from the image as a one-off task if C0.11 chooses that (OD-32, item 71).
    - **Three modes (PD-115):** `node scripts/owner-bootstrap.ts <set-up|recover|correct-number> --database <name> [--commit]`. `set-up` creates the org and its first Owner and DPO and clears Twilio Verify and AWS once (items 16, 20 and 37; BSC-02; item 66); `recover` is Twilio's break-glass re-clear (item 44; item 67); `correct-number` replaces the founder's number before their first sign-in (OD-29; item 68). Every mode is a plan unless given `--commit`: it runs its whole transaction (every check, every write, the function and the verification reads) and rolls back, so a forgotten flag writes nothing. The mode is the one positional argument, and `parseArgs` takes exactly `--commit` and `--database` (a database name, required); anything else exits 2 before the input is read.
    - **No status mode.** Every recovery run, a plan included, prints how many current DPOs hold a live session and when the last of those sessions ends (item 44 check 7's predicate, read plainly before the function's locked check) and whether Twilio Verify is cleared now, and every mode's rerun answers truthfully (item 69). A status mode's other read, the founder's code requests, would be one person's rows.
    - **Also in the PR:** `orgNameProblems()` (`packages/shared/src/org-name.ts`, item 64); `TransferFactsBody`, the api's facts body, exported from `packages/shared/src/registers.ts`; the check, the `first-owner` integration suite, the unit tests and the guard (item 70); the runbook "Setting up the organisation and its first Owner" (`docs/runbooks/first-owner-set-up.md`) and the changes the script makes true in the README, `twilio-withdrawn.md` and `publishing-the-notice.md` (item 46); and CLAUDE.md's paragraphs that name the script (PD-136): its paths in the Before-merging rule, its reads of which orgs a database holds among ACC-11's exceptions, its own rows as the seams bullet's one exemption from appending an event (item 66), its Database bullet and lock-order sentence, the send-state exception (item 67), and the Testing and CI and Commands entries.
    - **Not in it,** each with its home (item 71): the sole Owner's break-glass PIN reset, its own PR before C0.15 as a fourth mode of this script with its own migration (OD-28, PD-130; T14 items 12 and 21, T20 item 13); how the person on call reaches RDS, and the bootstrap's own login (C0.11, OD-32); the environment marker on each database, the log settings, Database Activity Streams kept off and RDS's CA bundle (C0.12); an alarm on `bootstrap.*` audit rows (C0.13); a second Owner and DPO, and org settings that reuse `orgNameProblems()` (C0.16); v0, which is the DPO's own publish in the app (`publishing-the-notice.md`, PD-84).
    - **No migration.** `bootstrap_transfer_clearance()` stays as 0011 left it (security invoker, granted to nobody, BSC-01; no coverage entry, item 45); the set-up's other writes are plain inserts by the owner, as the seed's are; the once rule is an advisory lock and a read (item 66); the marker is a database comment C0.12 writes (item 65); the correction is plain statements by the owner under the same locks (item 68). So no `npm run db:types` change and no upgrade step; the one DB-test change is BSC-05's new case (item 70).
64. **Inputs and the checks before connecting (PD-116, PD-117, PD-118, PD-119, PD-120, PD-121).**
    - **One document in one variable (PD-116).** `OWNER_BOOTSTRAP_INPUT` holds the mode's JSON document, set for the one command and never exported, so it never stays in the shell. The script reads it once, in `readEnvironment()` (the only reader of `process.env`), and deletes it from `process.env`, so nothing it starts or prints can reach it. The set-up and correction documents come from the environment's bootstrap secret, `OWNER_BOOTSTRAP_INPUT="$(aws secretsmanager get-secret-value --secret-id <the secret> --version-id <the version id> --query SecretString --output text)" node scripts/owner-bootstrap.ts <mode> --database <name>`, at the version id read before the plan, so the commit runs on the document the plan checked, and the history holds only the secret's name and that id (the runbook has the AWS CLI's own history, `cli_history`, off and no `--debug`, which would keep or print the secret's value). A secret that can't be read means the run waits until it can: neither document is ever pasted or typed, because their commits print no summary of what they keep, so only a pinned version binds a commit to its plan (CodeRabbit's review of the bootstrap PR; the owner, 27 Oct 2026, dropping PD-116's paste fallback). The recovery document, which holds no name or number, is typed once with echo on into `doc`, a shell variable never exported (`doc="$(cat)"`, ended with Enter and Ctrl-D: Ctrl-D ends the input only at the start of a line), which the plan and the commit both read (`OWNER_BOOTSTRAP_INPUT="$doc" … recover`), so its commit too runs on exactly what its plan checked (CodeRabbit's third review: the plan shows the reason only as a length); `unset doc` follows the commit. Never a one-line `read`, which takes only the first line and runs a pasted document's other lines as shell commands, echoed and kept in the history; never an argument (shown in `ps`, the history and a one-off task's records), a file (left on disk, and committed by the Stop hook in a source folder, since JSON isn't on its data-shaped list) or a prompt (which would make the run interactive, which item 44 forbids). A lone `{` or `[` is refused as truncated input ("only the first line arrived").
    - **The rest of the environment.** `DEPLOY_ENV` names the target (`local`, `test`, `nonprod` or `prod`, as the server reads it, T14 item 18). `PGHOST`, `PGPORT`, `PGUSER`, `PGPASSWORD`, `PGSSLMODE` and `PGSSLNEGOTIATION` say where and how to connect, as pg reads them; `PGDATABASE` is ignored, since the client connects to `--database`; `OWNER_BOOTSTRAP_TLS_NAME` names the RDS endpoint for a port forward (item 65). `.env.example` documents the script's two variables, commented and with no values; the server never reads them, and no C0.9 PR adds a server variable.
    - **The documents.** Each is a strict object, so an unknown key is refused, a document meant for another mode with it, and so is a `pin` key, since no field takes a PIN. Each names the `environment` it was written for, which must equal `DEPLOY_ENV`, so a non-prod secret loaded for a production run is refused before connecting, and production's clearances and audit rows, which never change, never keep non-prod's reason and facts (PD-121). `set-up`: `organisation`, `founder` (`fullName`, `number`), `reason`, and `twilio` and `aws`, each with the fifteen facts the DPO's Transfers page takes. `recover`: `dpo` (the DPO's person id, in either case, lower-cased before use), `reviewOn`, `reason` and an optional `fromEntry`. `correct-number`: `number` and `reason`.
    - **Every value is checked in code before any SQL runs (PD-119),** so no constraint's DETAIL ("Failing row contains …", "Key (org_id, e164)=(…)") can carry a name or a number to the client or the server's log, which the parameter-length settings don't cover:
      - the org's name through `orgNameProblems()` (below);
      - the founder's name trimmed, 1 to 200 code points (0002's check), with no control, format or lone surrogate character and no run of whitespace inside; refused, never altered, and never held to `AI_COPY` (PD-102: a person may be called Ask);
      - the number through `normalisePhone()`, the parser sign-in uses, so the stored E.164 is exactly what `person_by_phone()` will be asked for; any country (T14 item 2), subject to the fiction rule;
      - the facts through `TransferFactsBody` (text trimmed and blank as missing, countries upper-cased, de-duplicated and sorted) and `transferProblems()` for today in `Asia/Singapore`, so the script and the DPO's page read facts alike, the clearance trigger staying the authority in the org's zone;
      - each stated text field the notice prints (`services`, `data_types`, `provider_retention`, `onward_recipients`) through `aiCopyIn(flatText())`, as publishing checks it (`free_text_ai_copy`, PD-62), so v0 is never refused for text the set-up wrote;
      - the reason, 1 to 2,000 characters once trimmed, as the function trims it;
      - `fromEntry` (below).

      Each refusal exits 2 and names the variable or the field and the problem, never its value: a JSON failure prints "not valid JSON" (V8's message quotes the text), and a zod issue its path and a kind from a closed map of issue codes, never zod's message, naming an unknown key only when it is word-shaped.
    - **One reason, no time zone (PD-117).** The set-up's reason goes on both clearances' audit rows and its own, and the runbook asks for one that names roles, never people. The org starts in 0001's `Asia/Singapore`, which C0.16's org settings change; every review date is checked in the zone the org has.
    - **The org's name (PD-118).** `orgNameProblems(name)` answers, in order, `empty`; `too_long` (more than 200 code points, as 0001's check counts characters); `not_plain` (a control, format or lone surrogate character, which covers line breaks, tabs, bidi controls, zero-width characters and the soft hyphen; any whitespace but a plain space, such as the no-break or thin space, which `flatText()` would print as one; or two spaces together); and `ai_copy` (`aiCopyIn()` matching the name as typed, as the notice prints it through `flatText()`, or the NFKC form of either, so a fullwidth 'ＡＩ' can't print as "AI" where the generator's own test, which reads only the flattened text, would let it through). It trims the name itself, and the script stores it trimmed, so the name stored is the name `orgName()` prints; the database's own check stays the backstop. A refusal names `organisation` and the code, never the matched word. C0.16's org settings reuse it (item 37). A homoglyph from another script passes: the founder's reading of the plan's `organisation` line and the DPO's reading of the v0 preview catch it, and the generator's fallback still covers a name `AI_COPY` later grows to match.
    - **`fromEntry` (PD-127).** At most seven distinct facts, each one the notice states (`NOTICE_TRANSFER_FACTS`), as its snake-case key or as the field name the set-up document and the DPO's page use (`dataTypes` and the rest, through `NOTICE_FACT_FIELDS`), mapped to the key; a fact named twice, in either spelling, is refused. A fact the notice doesn't state (`countries_confirmed`, `safeguard`, `review_on` and the rest) is refused, since item 44 provides only for "a stated fact" the DPO decided changed, and the refusal lists the accepted spellings. A value is never accepted: nobody types a fact.
    - **The fiction rule (PD-120).** Under `local` and `test` only Ofcom's range reserved for drama, +44 7700 900000 to +44 7700 900999, is accepted; under `nonprod` and `prod` that range and the seed's twelve numbers are refused. So a test value never becomes production's founder, and a real number never reaches a local or CI database or its logs.
    - **The environment, before connecting (PD-121).** Also refused, exit 2: `DEPLOY_ENV` missing or unknown; in any environment, a `PGSSLNEGOTIATION` other than unset, `postgres` or `direct` (pg would refuse to make a client); under `local` or `test`, a `PGHOST` that isn't this machine, `PGHOSTADDR`, `PGSERVICE` or `PGSERVICEFILE` set (`db-local.sh`'s refusals), `OWNER_BOOTSTRAP_TLS_NAME` set, or `PGSSLNEGOTIATION` `direct` without a `PGSSLMODE` that turns TLS on; under `nonprod` or `prod`, `PGHOST` missing or a socket, any of `CLAUDECODE`, `CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT`, `AI_AGENT`, `CI` or `GITHUB_ACTIONS` present (their presence only, never their values: IAM is the real control, but a refusal costs nothing and catches a misconfigured session), a `PGSSLMODE` other than unset or `verify-full`, or `OWNER_BOOTSTRAP_TLS_NAME` missing for an IP address, set for a host name, or not a name ending `.rds.amazonaws.com`.
65. **The target, the login and the schema (PD-121, PD-122, PD-123, PD-125).**
    - **The connection.** The role and the bounds are start-up options, with `debug_print_plan` off, `-c role=hearth_owner -c transaction_timeout=30s -c lock_timeout=2s -c idle_in_transaction_session_timeout=5s -c debug_print_plan=off`, as the migrator takes its role and as item 44 asks of the recovery mode, so every transaction is bounded from its first statement and a login that can't take `hearth_owner` fails to connect (42501). `debug_print_plan` is set off because any role may turn it on, a plan it logs carries the bound name and number as literals in its DETAIL, which no parameter-length setting shortens, and a value the client sends at start-up outranks the parameter group's, `ALTER DATABASE`'s and `ALTER ROLE`'s; the log check reads it back (below). The client also sets `application_name` `hearth-owner-bootstrap`, its own query timeout of 31 s, a 10 s connection timeout and TCP keepalive, and an `error` listener, so a connection the server ends never crashes the process.
    - **TLS (PD-121).** pg 8.23.1 reads `PGSSLMODE` only when the config gives no `ssl`, maps `prefer`, `require`, `verify-ca` and `verify-full` alike to a verified connection and `no-verify` to an unverified one, so under `nonprod` and `prod` the script passes `ssl: { rejectUnauthorized: true }` itself, and verification never depends on how pg reads the environment; RDS's CA comes from `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS`. Node checks the certificate's name against `PGHOST`, so for a port forward (an IP address) the script passes `OWNER_BOOTSTRAP_TLS_NAME` as `servername`, which pg keeps for an IP host: the certificate is still checked against RDS's CA and the endpoint's name, with no `/etc/hosts` edit at 2 a.m. Under `local` and `test` pg reads `PGSSLMODE` as it does for every script.
    - **What the server says, checked in each transaction's second statement** against what the person said and what the checkout holds; any problem rolls back and exits 2, writing nothing. The server decides, not the host, since an SSM port forward makes RDS look local:
      - the server: no `rds_superuser` role under `local` and `test`; under `nonprod` and `prod` that role exists (it is RDS) and the backend's `pg_stat_ssl` row says TLS;
      - the database: `--database` equals `current_database()`, and its environment marker (below) is absent or `hearth-env:<DEPLOY_ENV>` under `local` and `test`, and exactly `hearth-env:<DEPLOY_ENV>` under `nonprod` and `prod`;
      - the login (PD-122): `session_user` isn't `hearth_owner` ("connect with the bootstrap's own login": `hearth_owner` is CREATEROLE and owns every table); it is LOGIN and NOINHERIT, not a superuser, without BYPASSRLS, CREATEROLE or CREATEDB, owns no relation, and its only membership, read from `pg_auth_members` with each grant's own options (from Postgres 16 inheritance belongs to each grant, so the NOINHERIT attribute alone proves nothing), is `hearth_owner` with SET and without INHERIT or ADMIN, with `rds_iam` also allowed on RDS. So the RDS master user, a member of `rds_superuser`, is refused, a role switch gains nothing, and the script's power is `hearth_owner`'s, taken for its transaction. Production's login is the bootstrap's own (C0.11, C0.12), never the deploy's migrate login, so the logs tell a break-glass use from a deploy;
      - the session acts as `hearth_owner`, at READ COMMITTED, with the bounds the start-up options set (role defaults set longer change nothing: BSS-06);
      - the schema: the ledger exists, `bootstrap_transfer_clearance`'s 18-argument signature exists, and `orgs` doesn't force row level security, so the owner's read of it sees every org;
      - the log settings, under `nonprod` and `prod`, read from `pg_settings` (which leaves out a row the role can't see, where `current_setting()` would raise 42501): both parameter-length settings 0; `log_statement` `none`, `ddl` or `mod` (with both lengths 0 a logged statement shows only its placeholders, so C0.12's allowed `mod` is as safe as `none`); `log_error_verbosity` `terse` (a failed statement's DETAIL can carry a row's values); `log_destination` naming only `stderr`, `syslog` or `eventlog`, which drop a DETAIL under `terse` (`csvlog` and `jsonlog` write it whatever the verbosity, so an empty or unknown list counts as unsafe too); `debug_print_plan` off, which the start-up option sets for the script's own session, so reading it back checks that the option held; `auto_explain` absent or `auto_explain.log_min_duration` -1 (a custom plan prints the bound name and number as literals in its Index Cond, Filter and, with `auto_explain.log_verbose`, Output lines; `auto_explain.log_parameter_max_length` covers only its Query Parameters line, so 0 is not enough); and `pgaudit.log_parameter` absent or off. A missing core row (any of the six before `auto_explain`) counts as unreadable, by name. `set-up` and `correct-number`, which bind a name or a number, refuse on any finding; `recover`, which binds only an org id, a DPO's id, a day, fact keys and a reason that names roles, prints a `log_warning` line and goes on, since a parameter group must not stop the emergency re-clear. Database Activity Streams record bind values where no SQL can see them, so C0.12 keeps them off on the bootstrap's database while a set-up or a correction runs, and the runbook checks that first. Likewise `auto_explain` is not loaded, or its `log_min_duration` is -1, on that database while a set-up or a correction runs.
    - **The environment marker (PD-121).** The database's comment, `COMMENT ON DATABASE <name> IS 'hearth-env:nonprod'` or `'hearth-env:prod'`, which C0.12 sets when it creates each environment's database, acting as `hearth_owner`, its owner: no placeholder setting, no superuser and no `ALTER DATABASE … SET`, which a role without superuser most likely can't do on RDS. No migration, seed or runtime code sets it, and the script never does (BSG-06). It is a fact written on the server by infra, apart from what the person on call brings, so it ties `DEPLOY_ENV` to the database even when `DEPLOY_ENV` and `--database` come from one mistaken runbook step; the document's `environment` ties the input to `DEPLOY_ENV` (item 64). A restore that keeps database-level objects (an RDS snapshot) carries its source's marker, so a restored copy refuses a run under any other environment until C0.17's drill re-marks it. It is an intent check, not a privilege (item 71's residuals).
    - **`--database`.** The person names the database on the command they run, and the client connects to exactly that one, so a stale `PGDATABASE` is ignored and an unset one can't send pg to a database named after the user; `current_database()` is still compared.
    - **The migrations, read at run time (PD-123).** Each transaction's second statement compares `kysely_migration`'s names with the checkout's `db/migrations/*.sql`, read at run time, both ways, and refuses either difference by name ("This database has applied …, which this checkout doesn't have: run the script from the commit the database was migrated by." / "This database hasn't applied …: run the deploy's migrate step first, or check out the deployed commit."), with no constant in the script for a migration PR to edit. The set-up and the correction compare again under Kysely's lock (item 66). A migration that changes what the script relies on, as distinct from a stale checkout, turns CI red on its own PR: the check's BSS-02 and BSS-03 run in every `migrations` job and the `first-owner` suite in every `check` job (item 70).
66. **The set-up (PD-124, PD-125, PD-126).** In one READ COMMITTED transaction as `hearth_owner`. Its ids are minted before connecting: the org, the Hearth principal, the founder (whose person and human principal share one) and their number's row. Each line is its own statement, and every value is bound:
    - **S1** `begin isolation level read committed read write`, explicit, so no default changes the level and each later statement takes a new snapshot.
    - **S2** sets `lock_timeout` to 2 s and the emergency marker to `''`, both transaction-local, and reads the server's facts and the ledger (item 65). The marker is a bound value: the set-up and the correction pass `''` and only `recover()` passes `twilio_reclear`, so a set-up never reaches the re-clear even if a role or database default sets the marker (PD-125; BSS-16).
    - **S3,** alone in its statement: `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended('hearth.owner_bootstrap', 0))`, then Kysely's migration lock, the key Kysely 0.29.6's migrator holds for its whole run (session-level, which conflicts with a transaction-level lock on the same key; BSU-12 pins it to the installed Kysely). So two set-ups, or a set-up and a correction, take turns, and so do a set-up and a deploy's migration, so a migration that replaces a defaults function and backfills the orgs already there (0012's pattern) can't miss an org this transaction hasn't committed. Each waits at most 2 s, then exit 3; a migration waits for the set-up instead, since Kysely raises its own lock timeout before it takes the lock (PD-124).
    - **S4:** any org at all, and the ledger again. Under READ COMMITTED this snapshot is taken after S3 returned, so it sees an org a competing run committed while this one waited; one statement with S3, or REPEATABLE READ, would see none and both would commit (BSR-01). An org: rollback, "This database already has organisation <id>. The set-up runs once, so nothing was written." (exit 1). A migration that committed between S2 and S3: refused by name (exit 2). The owner skips row level security on `orgs` (it owns the table, and S2 checked it isn't forced), so this read crosses orgs: ACC-11's named exception, org ids only (PD-136).
    - **S5** the org, whose triggers on `orgs` (0007's and 0009's, with 0012's defaults) give it every flag at its default, `ai.*` off and locked, version 1 of every purpose (`request_handling` active) and a revision-1 entry for every provider; **S6** its Hearth principal; **S7** `app.org_id` and `app.user_id` set to the org and Hearth with `set_config(…, true)`, as `withOrg()` sets them and as the function requires (item 20); **S8** the founder's principal; **S9** their person, with no department, manager or profile; **S10** their number, current; **S11** `owner` and `dpo`, granted by Hearth with no second approver, as 0002 allows for "the bootstrap's first Owner and DPO (D35)". People come before role rows and there is no session to end, so T20 item 14's order holds.
    - **S12** one audit row of its own (PD-126): `bootstrap.set_up`, with Hearth the actor, the founder the target, the reason, and `details` `{"by": "bootstrap", "roles": ["owner", "dpo"], "hearth": <Hearth's id>, "number": <the phone_numbers row's id>}`, ids only, never the name or number; `via`, `on_behalf_of_id`, `request_id`, `device_id` and `ip` empty. An imperative code, as T14 item 17's admin actions are.
    - **S13 and S14** `bootstrap_transfer_clearance()` for `twilio`, then `aws`, with the parsed facts mapped field by field in the function's order: 0009's path (the DPO's person and `dpo` rows `FOR SHARE`, the entry `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, never cleared before; the facts with Hearth as `updated_by`, revision 1 to 2; a clearance `cleared_via 'bootstrap'` in the founder's name; its audit row; `register.transfer_changed`), as BSC-02 asserts.
    - **S15,** one verification read, which rolls back and exits 1, naming what differed but never a value, unless: the flags, purposes and providers number `FLAGS`, `PURPOSE_KEYS` and `PROVIDERS`; the org has one Hearth principal; the number is current for the founder alone; there is no notice version and no contact; and `transfer_send_state('twilio')` reads `no_notice`. A plain org-scoped read of `phone_numbers`, not `person_by_phone()`, which is a pre-org sign-in lookup; the server's own lookup is exercised by the check and the `first-owner` suite.
    - **S16** `commit`, after printing "committing organisation <id>"; or `rollback`, for a plan.

    **What it writes, and nothing else** (BSS-03 holds it both ways): `orgs` 1, `principals` 2, `people` 1, `phone_numbers` 1 and `role_assignments` 2; the triggers' `feature_flags`, `processing_register` and `transfer_register` rows; the function's two entry updates, two clearances, two audit rows and two `register.transfer_changed` events; and its own audit row. No `users`, `devices`, `sessions`, `passkeys`, `profiles`, `notice_versions`, `notice_acknowledgments`, `dpo_settings` (item 41: the bootstrap gives the org no contact), `cases`, `audit_log_readers` (D45: empty until chosen) or `processing_restrictions` row.

    **No event of its own (PD-126).** `person.added` and `role.changed` are reserved for C0.16 with no ids schema, so `appendOutbox()` would refuse them, and nothing consumes them. The set-up's own inserts are `hearth_owner`'s, before any runtime role can act, outside the runtime roles the outbox's contract covers (`coverage.ts`, `writes.ts`), as the seed's and the migrations' backfills are. CLAUDE.md's seams bullet names the exception, `coverage.ts`'s comment at the bootstrap's function says so, and BSS-03 pins it, since nothing else in CI checks a write by `hearth_owner`.

    **Lock order.** The two advisory locks come before any row; nothing else takes the bootstrap's, and the migrator takes its own before any row of its own, so neither adds an edge between rows. Then new rows only, then the function's people → role_assignments → transfer_register → transfer_clearances, the order's own. The transaction holds an xid for well under a second.

    **What production does next, with no restart.** The api tracks an org at its first request (`flags/cache.ts`), so the founder's first code request works on a running api; the worker lists the new org within 60 s (T18 item 2) and sees the clearances at its next register poll (item 9). The founder signs in at once on their own phone: one code (only a DPO gets one before v0, PD-59), then a new PIN on the code alone (T20 item 12); then `publishing-the-notice.md` (item 37).
