The leaver reduction's `working_hours` stays unguarded, since a person's own working-hours edit deletes from it unlogged (exempt, LH-48) and a `checked` path may not delete from a guarded table; `deletion_log`'s purge is `exempt` under LH-10, its table carrying `lifecycle_no_truncate()` and `deletion_log_kept()`, never the guard. Each later `guarded` path declares `forgets` (C1.2's message delete) or `notASource` with the id that records why (C2.9's sign-in records, acknowledgements and logs).

10. **Legal holds (LH-15, LH-16, LH-17, LH-36, LH-59).**
    - **The table (PR 1; LH-15).** `legal_holds` holds ADM-08's target vocabulary now (`person`, `channel`, `dm`, `group_dm`, `room`, `session` (C2.4's recorded room session, never a sign-in session), `folder` and `file`; `HOLD_TARGET_KINDS`, check 7f), so no step needs a migration to widen it; a stated reason, and a release reason, of 1 to 1,000 characters under 0012's note rules (trimmed, line breaks kept, no other control character, no bidirectional-formatting character, its class written as escapes, no NRIC- or FIN-shaped string); a client key unique per placer, so a resend makes no second hold; the case it was placed under, as a plain id (a foreign key would make a released hold keep its case, and `delete_case()` fail with 23503 mid-batch); and placed and released times and principals. History: `legal_holds_kept()` refuses any change but the release, once, its three columns together, for every writer. No target name or snapshot is stored, and several holds may cover one target (two matters). No grant to any role, and RLS with the one org policy. In C0.10 nothing writes it but the DB tests, as the owner, after taking the target's row as the writer will.
    - **Its place in the hold decision.** While active, a hold holds its target (a person, current or former: "a former employee's included"), every row anchored on them, any container it names and what is in it (item 12), the case it cites, and itself: an active hold of any kind can't be deleted (DB-08). A released hold can be, logged, by C2.9's purge 731 days after release.
    - **The writers and the read, designed for C1.11 (LH-16).** `place_legal_hold()`, `release_legal_hold()` and `read_legal_holds()`, SECURITY DEFINER with `search_path = ''`, granted to `hearth_app` with C1.11's routes (`POST /api/admin/holds`, `POST /api/admin/holds/:id/release`, `GET /api/admin/holds`), each re-checking its actor through `holds_check_actor()` (a person still at the org holding `dpo` or `compliance_officer`, read `FOR SHARE`; 42501 otherwise) after `can()` has allowed the action and the PIN re-authentication. Placing: the client key; the inputs (a kind not yet holdable, or a reason the rules refuse: HF006); the target through `lifecycle_lock_target(…, 'no key update')` for every kind, a person of the org current or former (22023, "There is no such person."), never the actor (42501, "Nobody places a hold on themselves."); a cited case through `lifecycle_lock_target(…, 'key share')` after the target, which takes turns with `delete_case()`'s `FOR UPDATE` (DB-02, LCR-16; 22023, "There is no such request."); LH-44's answer when no other reader would remain; the insert; and one audit row, `hold.placed`, target `legal_hold`, details `{target_kind}`, never the target's id, the reason or the case. Releasing: the hold `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` (none, another org's, or one on the actor: 22023, "There is no such hold.", as if absent; released already: HF005); the release reason (HF006); one audit row, `hold.released`; no lock on the target, so a deletion that read the hold before the release commits still sees it active. Reading: active holds first, then released ones by release, newest first, 1 to 500 rows (2201W outside), never a hold whose target is the reader, with one audit row, `hold.read`, whose details are `{}`, never a count or a bound (DB-05, PRIV-01). The access actions `hold.place`, `hold.release` and `hold.read` join `ADMIN_ACTIONS`, so each needs the PIN, and `adminCan()` allows the DPO or a Compliance Officer, never an Owner, Admin, AI Admin or Leadership as such, nor an assistant, agent or Hearth (R9-03), the target a person of the org, current or former, and never the actor; with `roles.test.ts`'s rows (LCA-01 to LCA-03), the Rule 9 fixture's, `HOLDABLE_NOW`, LCD-21 to LCD-26, LCR-05 to LCR-09, LCR-16 against the real writer, and LCU-06. Building the definers now, granted to nobody, was the alternative: functions nothing can call, against D18.
    - **The audit filter, designed for C1.11 (LH-17).** `read_audit_events()` is replaced with its signature, owner and grant. After 0012's reads it reads the reader's `dpo` and `compliance_officer` role rows `FOR SHARE` in a statement of its own (LCR-09), and returns a hold's rows only to a holder of either, never one about a hold on the reader. The organisation's chosen audit readers (D45) learn nothing of holds unless they hold one of those roles, and a held DPO or Compliance Officer sees neither the hold nor its rows, so the two reads agree; `hold.read` rows match no hold and carry no count. `delete_case()`'s rows are already behind the case filter (OD-23).
    - **Who (LH-44 (a)).** The DPO or a Compliance Officer, never on themselves, with a stated reason and no second approver: placing only adds protection, and with the sole DPO who is also the founder (D35) a second approver could leave a hold nobody can release; the audit row and the alert stand in for one. A hold that no live reader but its target can release (a Compliance Officer holds the sole DPO, then leaves) is accepted: the next Compliance Officer the Owner appoints sees and can release it, and C1.11's console marks holds whose placer has left (OWN-02). Placing a hold reads no content and imports nothing from `privileged.ts`: it is an admin action, not a Rule 9 read. Hearth never tells the held person.
    - **Confidentiality (LH-17).** No event, ever: `hold.` and `legal_hold.` are forbidden catalogue namespaces (`FORBIDDEN_NAMESPACES`, LCS-09), since every outbox consumer, A2's indexers included, reads every event; reserving `hold.` for C1.11 was the alternative. No gateway frame or Valkey key carries a hold. `legal_holds` has no grant, and `is_held()` answers only the deletion code (item 12). From C1.11 the reason never reaches the audit log, joins the logger's redaction list (`SENSITIVE_KEYS`), and is shown only to the DPO and Compliance Officers, as data (Rule 8), so neither SQL nor a route holds it to `AI_COPY`, as with a case note (PD-102).
    - **Alerts and counts (LH-59).** A hold change alerts every live holder of `dpo` or `compliance_officer` except the actor and the hold's target, through one category, "Legal holds changed", that C0.14 builds and C1.11 wires, with no hold id in it; when nobody else remains, no alert is sent and the audit row is the record. No count or summary over holds, in an alert, a badge or a console, includes a hold on its recipient. C0.10 sends no alert. Sweep counts follow item 18.
    - **No purpose key yet (LH-36).** The `legal_holds` class names no purpose until C1.11 makes the first writer reachable and adds the `legal_holds` purpose key, with the notice's legal-hold clause; adding it now would add a register row per org, and a backfill, for a purpose nothing serves, and hiding holds under `security_audit` would mislead.

11. **Access-request holds (LH-19, LH-20, LH-61).** Computed, never stored (CLAUDE.md, Database), by `case_hold_until(org, case)` from facts the DPO already records on a case, which never change (`cases_fixed()`, `case_steps_unchanged()`), so the hold can't be rewritten; a stored hold row was the alternative, a second truth that could disagree with the case. It answers null (no hold), `infinity` (held with no end) or the last day held, inclusive, in the org's zone:
    - **An open request for a copy or a correction,** by its kind now, holds from receipt until it is closed, since deleting data while a copy is prepared, or before it is corrected, defeats the request (LH-20, LH-45). An open question, complaint, withdrawal or objection holds nothing: a withdrawal asks Hearth to stop, so holding for it would work against it.
    - **A refused copy** (`outcome 'refused'`, `closed_kind 'access'`; s22A and regulation 8 of the PDP Regulations 2021) holds with no end while a PDPC review is open, and otherwise through the later of `closed_on` + 30 + 14 and the day the last review ended. The refusal's day is day 0, so "at least 30 days after the refusal" holds through `closed_on + 30`, and the 14-day margin (LH-46), for a review applied for in time that the PDPC notifies late, through `closed_on + 44`; exactly 30 days was the alternative. The figures are `refused_copy_hold()`'s, which `REFUSED_COPY_HOLD` equals (check 7f). A reconsideration or an appeal belongs to the open review episode: the DPO records "The PDPC review ended" only once the review, any reconsideration and any appeal have all ended (HOLD-01); `add_case_step()` allows a new start after an end, for a fresh application, and the latest end then counts. A refused correction or objection holds nothing once closed, since s22A is about access.
    - **What it covers (LH-61).** A linked requester's whole person scope: their person row, a former employee's included, and everything anchored on it (their numbers, devices, sign-in sessions, passkeys and acknowledgements; every audit row naming them as actor, as the person acted for, as the person acted on, as the DPO who decided a clearance (`details.decided_by`), as a person a flag's cohort change added or removed (`details.people_added`, `details.people_removed`), or as the requester of a case the row names; every access-log row about them or by them; their own requests to the DPO), and from C1.1 everything LH-55 lists; plus the case itself, its row and steps. That keeps more than s22A strictly asks, against retention limitation (PDPA s25), until C6.1 can narrow it to the data the request concerns, and keeping too much fails safe. "The data the request covers" is, in C0, `access-request.md` step 1's list.
    - **Outsiders and leavers.** A requester without an account holds only the case; the runbook tells the DPO to hold, once C1.11's console exists, whatever else the request covers that they identify. A hold reaches a requester's records only through the case's linked person, so a leaver's request is logged against their former person (listed with "left on <day>" in the log dialog), never as someone without an account (`access-request.md`). A case is never linked after it is logged (`cases_fixed()`), so a requester the log dialog can't tell apart from another person (PD-76's safeguard, PPL-13), which it says to log as someone without a Hearth account, holds only the case: the runbook tells the DPO to note that on the case, keep who it is in the DPO's own files and, once C1.11's console exists, place a legal hold on their person, released once the request's own hold would have ended (at the closure, or for a refused copy the later of 44 days after the refusal and "The PDPC review ended"), with a reason citing the case's reference. A partial refusal (an answered copy with items withheld) starts no hold; the DPO places a legal hold once C1.11's console exists (LH-54).
    - **What C0 doesn't preserve (LH-54).** s22A asks the organisation to "preserve a complete and accurate copy", which C0 does by blocking deletion only. In-place edits (a profile's name or title, a device's label, working hours) aren't versioned, and the person on call's working extract under `local-data/` is deleted once the reply is sent, since the production records are what the hold keeps. C0.15 decides whether a held person's profile edits keep their earlier values; C6.1 revisits versions, and whether a held person's access request may withhold a hold under the PDPA's exception for an investigation not yet complete.
    - **The mirror (LH-18).** `heldUntil()` in `packages/shared/src/retention.ts` computes the same from the same raw facts, the kind now worked out as `case_kind_now()` does. LCS-06 and LCD-14 run one sample table through both (`packages/shared/src/retention-samples.json`, which `192_access_request_holds.sql` holds verbatim between its `-- samples: begin` and `-- samples: end` markers, and LCV-08 holds equal), each day an offset from the org's today, in two orgs whose zones (Pacific/Kiritimati, UTC+14, and Pacific/Pago_Pago, UTC−11) make a computation in the wrong zone fail. It is a test oracle in C0.10; C2.9's "kept until", shown to the DPO alone, and C6.1's console will read it.

12. **`isHeld()` (LH-18, LH-56).** BUILD-PLAN's and CLAUDE.md's `isHeld()` is `is_held(kind, id)` in SQL, a SECURITY DEFINER with `search_path = ''`, granted to nobody, for the deletion code's definers to ask inside their own transactions.
    - **It refuses a read-only transaction** by its own first check (25006, "Whether something is held is asked only in the transaction that deletes it."), so no GET can call it and the refusal never depends on whether a lock statement happens to run (LCD-13). It takes the org from `app.org_id` (42501 when none is set) and raises 22023 for a kind `lifecycle_kinds()` doesn't list (`person`, `phone_number`, `device`, `signin_session`, `passkey`, `notice_acknowledgment`, `case`, `legal_hold`, `data_access_event` and, from 0014, `derived`; `HOLD_KINDS`, check 7f). A row of `sessions` is a `signin_session`, never a `session`, which is C2.4's recorded room session (T14 item 17).
    - **It answers one boolean,** never which hold, why, until when, or a case id, so it is never an oracle a route could ask whether someone is held. T13 item 13 keeps holds out of `can()`.
    - **`lifecycle_held()` is its core**, the one place a hold is decided, which the guard and `delete_case()` call too. It is true when an active legal hold covers an anchor person, the item itself, any container it sits in or any source in its closure (by kind and id); for a legal hold, itself while active; for a case, an active legal hold that cites it; an access-request hold of an anchor person (a case of theirs whose `case_hold_until()` is today or later); or, for a case, its own `case_hold_until()`.
    - **Locks before reads (LH-21),** in the lock order: the anchor people `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, never `FOR SHARE`, by id, so a request being sent or logged for them (`submit_case()` and `log_case()` read the requester `FOR SHARE`) can't commit unseen behind it, and it takes turns with a hold being placed on them and with their deactivation (DB-01); then the item's containers `FOR SHARE`, through `lifecycle_lock_target()`, in (kind, id) order; then their open and refused requests `FOR SHARE`, and a case itself, which a closure, reclassification or review step holds `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`. It reads holds only in later statements, each of which takes a new snapshot under READ COMMITTED and so sees what committed while the locks waited. Under any other isolation level those reads would see the transaction's first snapshot and miss a request or hold that committed behind the locks, so `lifecycle_held()` refuses first (25000, `invalid_transaction_state`), and with it `is_held()`, `delete_case()` and the guard (HOLDS-03; `db/tests/198_lifecycle_isolation.sql`). A release in progress reads as still active, so a deletion that meets one refuses, which fails safe and needs no lock.
    - **Fail closed every way.** An unknown top-level kind raises; a source of a kind Hearth can't judge yet holds the item; a container kind `lifecycle_lock_target()` can't lock raises. An unknown kind reading as not held was the alternative: a forgotten resolver would delete held data.
    - **The containment seam (LH-56).** `lifecycle_containers_of()` (what an item sits inside, every level, nearest first; none in PR 1) and `lifecycle_lock_target()` (a person and a case in PR 1, each lock strength a fixed branch, never dynamic SQL) are the seam C1.1 (a message's conversation), C2.4 (a recorded session's room) and C4.1 (a file's folder chain) fill, each by replacing them with the same signatures and reading the item's stored creation context, so a move never releases a hold. With `lifecycle_kinds()`, `lifecycle_persons_of()` (the people an item is about, by stored creation context: LH-55), `lifecycle_row_anchors()` and `lifecycle_sources_of()` (from 0014 the closure over live edges through derived-mode items, ended by `UNION`), every later kind plugs in by replacement and `lifecycle_held()` never changes; LCG-03 refuses its replacement unless reviewed. LCD-41 proves the seam with replaced functions in a rolled-back transaction. For a derived-mode item, each of those steps' replacements of `lifecycle_containers_of()` also returns every live source in its closure whose kind is one of the step's own hold targets (C1.1's `channel`, `dm` and `group_dm`; C2.4's `room` and `session`; C4.1's `folder` and `file`), as well as the containers of every live source in its closure (a message's conversation, a session's room, a file's or a folder's folder chain), and keeps the earlier steps' branches; and each step adds its kinds to `lifecycle_lock_target()` (C1.1 the conversations, C2.4 a room and a session, C4.1 a folder and a file), since a kind it can't lock raises (22023). So `lifecycle_held()` and the lock-ahead lock those sources and containers `FOR SHARE` before the hold is read, with no change to `lifecycle_held()`: a hold on a conversation holds what was made from it or from a message in it, and a hold being placed on a source itself (C1.11 takes its target `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, item 18) takes turns with a cascade and with `provenance_forget()`'s leftover path, as LCR-12 shows for the person a source is about, who is an anchor and needs nothing more (LH-50, LH-56). Returning only the sources' containers was the alternative: a hold placed on a channel, room, session, folder or file that a derived item was made from could then commit unseen behind a cascade that deletes the item. C1.1, C2.4 and C4.1 each prove it for their own kinds with a race of a hold placed on a source against a cascade, and against `provenance_forget()`'s leftover path, in both orders.
    - **The caller's order.** A content step's definer reads its item plainly to find the people (author, owner), calls `is_held()`, which locks them, then locks the item `FOR UPDATE`, re-reads it and gives up if the people changed (C1.2, C4.1).
    - **No TypeScript `isHeld()` until C1.2.** A runtime role that could ask whether an item is held would be an oracle for who is under investigation. C1.2, its first runtime caller, adds `apps/server/src/lifecycle/holds.ts` (a `select public.is_held($1, $2)` inside the caller's `withOrg()` transaction, importable only from `apps/server/src/lifecycle/` and an allow-list) and grants `hearth_app` `is_held()` if its edit path needs it directly; C2.9 adds set-based definers its sweep role runs for a batch.
    - **Never an AI feature's.** No AI feature reads holds, the deletion log or `is_held()` (Rule 9), no role an A-stage adds gets a grant on them, and no event carries a hold.

13. **The deletion code's path for cases (LH-22, LH-23, LH-24).** `delete_case(case, cause, replay)` is the only way a request leaves: C2.9's sweep, and C0.17's replay of such a deletion. It is granted to nobody until C2.9 grants its job role.
    - **Who.** It acts only as the org's Hearth principal (42501 for anyone else; LCD-27 pins every persona's refusal), so its audit row names nobody, and skips `can()`, since it acts for no person. The org is `app.org_id`'s, never an argument (DB-06). The cause must be `retention`, the only cause a request has, and the replay flag set either way (22023 otherwise); a later cause is added by replacing it with the same signature.
    - **Answers, not refusals,** so a sweep counts each answer and never fails a batch on one: `gone` (no such request, idempotent for a replay), `open`, `not_due` (until `closed_on` plus `retention_period_days(org, 'cases')` has passed, in the org's zone, computed here whatever a sweep selected; a replay obeys it too), `held`, `cited` (a processing restriction names it, lifted or not; C2.11 decides whether a lifted one lets go) or `deleted`. Raising for a kept case was the alternative, which would fail a batch on one case. The guard on `cases` re-checks as a backstop and keeps raising, since a raise there means a bug. A period, day or closure day it can't read (null, as C2.9's replacement of `retention_period_days()` could return) refuses with 22023 rather than reading as due (HOLDS-02; LCD-27).
    - **Locks.** The requester's person row `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, then the request `FOR UPDATE` (people → cases), so two deletions for one requester, a hold being placed on them and a request being sent or logged for them take turns (LCR-03, LCR-11), and so does a hold citing it, whose writer reads it `FOR KEY SHARE` (LCR-16).
    - **What it writes.** One log row for the request, since its steps belong only to it; the request, then its steps; and one audit row, `case.deleted`, target `case`, details `{class, cause, replayed, steps}`, which `read_audit_events()` returns only to a reader who holds `dpo` (OD-23).
    - **No outbox event (LH-23).** Requests are never indexed, derived from or retrieved (T22 item 62), and C0.14's alert re-reads the case and passes over one that is gone (T16 item 9), so no consumer acts on a request's deletion: `outbox_events_type` isn't widened, the migration takes no lock on the outbox, and `coverage.ts` exempts the function (`CASE_DELETED`). Reserving `case.deleted` now was the alternative, an ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock on the outbox for an event no consumer reads; if C2.9's sweep ever gains a consumer, it reserves the event in its own migration. Nothing is derived from a case (`provenance_sources_kind` refuses one), so it calls no cascade.
    - **The index (LH-24).** `processing_restrictions_by_case` on `processing_restrictions (org_id, case_id)`, named apart from the constraint `processing_restrictions_case`, makes the key's check on every request's deletion, and `delete_case()`'s own read, a lookup (T22 item 62). The table has no rows and no writer, so the build was free.

14. **The provenance registry (LH-39 to LH-43, LH-50; built in PR 2, 0014).** BRAIN-08's shape, for Rules 3 and 7, with no runtime writer before its producers (LH-43):
    - **Two tables (LH-39).** `provenance_items`, one per item made from or citing others, in a `derived` mode (an AI summary, an index row, a suggested task: withdrawn on Rule 7 and inheriting holds; item kind `derived` is read as the intersection of its sources, Rule 3 through `derivedFacts()`, while another derived-mode kind keeps its own access rule, a suggested task its owner-only one, Rule 5) or a `cited` mode (a draft, an accepted insert, a slide copy: a record only); states `live`, `stale` and `withdrawn` (withdrawn final, with its reason: `source_deleted`, `source_redacted` or `source_corrected`); a fingerprint seam (the prompt, model and pipeline version: B.5); and, on a derived item, its number of sources (`source_count`, fixed) and `aclOf`'s stored row in the outbox's token shapes, nobody's once withdrawn, by constraint, so C1.7's and A2's SQL pre-filters can select derived items, re-checking each with `filterReadable` and `indexClassOf`. `provenance_sources`, its polymorphic edges (kind, id, a version that is a uuid, a whole number or a dotted code, and an anchor of ids only, spans in seconds or segment numbers), numbered 1 to 100 in order, each with `gone_at`, set once when its source is deleted (a source redacted or corrected in place stays, and so does its edge). A request or a legal hold is never an item or a source (RQS-11; item 10). `provenance_items (org_id, id)` is unique, for C4.3's and C4.4's composite keys. Ids, codes and the ACL row only, never content, and no grant.
    - **Acyclic by construction, one transaction at a time, and cited growth (LH-40).** Every writer's insert is checked (23514): an item takes its transaction from the database and starts live; a derived item's edges are written only in its creating transaction, all `source_count` of them by its commit (deferred) and none numbered above it ("A derived item has only as many sources as it was made with."), so the count holds even when a writer fires the deferred check early with `SET CONSTRAINTS`, and they never name a derived item made in that transaction; a derived-mode item is never made for something already a source, a gone edge included, which the edges' whole index by source (`provenance_sources_source`, with no `gone_at` predicate) serves, as it serves the walks over live edges; a source takes its item's next number; a withdrawn item is never a source, read `FOR SHARE` so a withdrawal in progress is waited for; and a source of kind `derived` must still have its record, a withdrawn source and a `derived` source with no record refused by one statement with one message and SQLSTATE ("That item is no longer available to be a source.", 23514), so a `derived` item a hold keeps withdrawn reads as one the cascade deleted (item 10, LH-55). Kind `derived` is the only kind whose withdrawn records are deleted in C0 (the cascade, below); the step that first deletes another derived-mode kind's records widens the trigger for that kind (the producers' contract, below), and until then, for any other kind, LH-43's own check is what keeps a hold from showing. So no one transaction can close a derived cycle. Two producers writing at once rely on the producers' contract (LH-43, below): each locks its sources live before it writes, so neither can name a record the other hasn't committed. A producer breaking it could still close a cycle in a race, and for that the walks' `UNION` (which ends it), the 32-link cap (which leaves what it reaches withdrawn for C2.9) and the access core's cycle rule (which reads it as nobody's, R3-20) are the backstops (item 20's residuals). The deferred completeness check is a definer that only refuses, reading the org from the item it checks (item 18), because Postgres 17 fires a deferred trigger at the commit as whichever role is active then, which holds no grant on either table; the other four triggers are plain. A cited item is a record, and may gain sources later (a second AI insert into one draft). An item keeps its identity, count and maker; it moves only from live to stale, from live or stale to withdrawn, and from stale back to live (a regeneration, which alone may set a new fingerprint); its ACL row changes only with a rising `acl_epoch`; an edge changes only by `gone_at`, once.
    - **The cascade (LH-41, LH-50).** No ON DELETE CASCADE from a source. `lifecycle_cascade(source_kind, source_id, parent, batch)`, which a deletion function calls after it deletes or redacts a source, with that deletion's log row as `parent`, withdraws everything made from the source, transitively, in its transaction (nobody's, by its stored row; the reason from the parent's cause: `redaction` gives `source_redacted`, `correction` `source_corrected`, any other `source_deleted`). A deleted source's edges are marked gone; after a redaction or a correction the source stays in place, and so do its edges, so a hold on it, on its people or on its container still keeps what was made from it. It then decides, deepest first (by the longest chain from the source), up to `batch` (1 to 500) withdrawn items of kind `derived`, a kept one counting as a deleted one does, so the batch bounds its hold decisions as well as its deletions, and deletes those nothing holds, logging each with `parent` and marking the edges that named it gone. A held item stays withdrawn and kept: it is held while its own kind's people or any source in its closure is held, and kept while it has a source Hearth can't judge yet. An item past the batch stays withdrawn for C2.9, and so does everything made from the source if any chain from it reaches 32 links (a chain that deep, or a cycle a producer breaking LH-43 left). A derived-mode item of another kind (C3.5's suggested tasks) keeps content in its own table, so the cascade never deletes it: it stays withdrawn and kept until the step that stores that content replaces `lifecycle_cascade()` and `provenance_forget()`, with the same signatures, to delete the content first (and `provenance_sources_insert()` for its kind: the producers' contract, below), logged in its own class with the same parent and through its table's guard (kind `derived` has no content before A2.3, which does the same for its own). The withdrawal and its lock-ahead cover the whole closure, whatever the batch. No event: no consumer of a derived kind exists yet.
    - **An item's own record (LH-41).** Every item's delete path calls `provenance_forget(item_kind, item_id, cause, parent)` for the item it deletes, derived or cited, with cause `cascade` and its own deletion's log row as `parent`, which must be the org's (22023 otherwise): it logs the record, cascades to what was made from it with that log row as parent, marks every edge still naming the record gone, then deletes the record, whose guard decides its hold through its own kind's people and its still-live edges, and then its edges. An item with no record of its own (C1.1's messages) only cascades. Check 8 holds every `guarded` delete path to it (item 9): one whose rows can be a source or an item declares `forgets`, and its body must call `provenance_forget(`. A withdrawn leftover of kind `derived` goes on its own with the cause its withdrawn reason gives (`correction` for `source_corrected`, `redaction` for `source_redacted`, `retention` for `source_deleted`), any other cause refused before its kind or hold is read, so a held leftover and an unheld one refuse alike (22023, "A withdrawn item's provenance goes with the cause its withdrawal gave it."), and no parent (C2.9), answering 0, and logging nothing, while it is held; a leftover of another derived-mode kind answers 0 until its content's step replaces the function.
    - **Withdrawal and staleness (LH-42).** A deletion, a redaction or a correction withdraws what was made from its source, transitively, in its own transaction (`withdraw_dependants()`, with reason `source_corrected` for a correction: PRD-110, PDPA-07); an edit or an ACL rewrite marks every live derived item made from it stale, still readable (`provenance_mark_stale()`, `source_edited` or `source_acl_changed`): an edit's definer calls it (C1.2's message edits, C4's document edits), and, for an ACL rewrite, what follows a source's stored ACL being rewritten (C1.1's and C4.1's handlers of `acl.changed` `rows`, C4.1's re-ACL job), which acts for no person (item 18). No caller in C0.
    - **Locks (LH-21).** The cascade, a withdrawal and `provenance_forget()` lock ahead (`provenance_lock_ahead()`) with the hold decision's own steps for everything they touch: the people every live source of those items is about, and, for a derived-mode item and the source's own record, the people its own kind is about (through `lifecycle_sources_of()` and `lifecycle_persons_of()`, the resolvers `lifecycle_held()` reads) `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` in id order, those items' containers `FOR SHARE` (from C1.1 with their closures' hold-target sources, item 12), those people's open and refused requests `FOR SHARE`, then the items themselves (and the source's own record) `FOR UPDATE` in id order; then they walk again in a later statement until nothing new appears (at most eight rounds, then 40001 and 409 `try_again`), so a derive that committed while they waited is withdrawn too. `lifecycle_held()`'s locks are then re-locks. Edges change only after their items. Stale-marking locks only the items. Each definer refuses outside READ COMMITTED (25000).
    - **PR 1's functions replaced,** with their signatures: `retention_class_defaults()` (both tables in `provenance`), `lifecycle_kinds()` (gaining `derived`), `lifecycle_persons_of()` (a derived item is about nobody itself: its sources give its people), `lifecycle_row_anchors()` (a provenance item anchors as its item, with its own kind's people when Hearth knows the kind; a source as its item) and `lifecycle_sources_of()` (the closure); `lifecycle_held()` and `is_held()` don't change. The guard on both tables; `derivedFacts()` in `packages/access` (R3-01b running, LCA-04, LCA-05; LCV-07); `scripts/check-access-sql.ts` over the stored registry; `db/tests/200_provenance.sql` (LCD-34 to LCD-40); LCR-12 to LCR-14 and LCR-17 to LCR-20; and LCU-10, riding PR 1's upgrade check, with LCU-04 holding the guard to five tables.
    - **The producers' contract (LH-43).** A producer's migration adds a checked writer granted to its role, with its coverage entry, which names an event for its inserts into `provenance_items` and `provenance_sources`: check 1 refuses an exempt entry that inserts into either (`contentProblems`), a cited producer's (C1.2, C4.3, C4.4) included, before `CONTENT_TABLES` gains `provenance_items` and after. In the transaction that writes an item and its provenance (or adds a source to a cited item), it takes its locks before it writes anything: first each content source's row `FOR KEY SHARE`, requiring it live and at the version the item was made from (the edge's `source_version`) and refusing otherwise, so an item made from data read earlier, even outside the transaction, never commits after that data was corrected or redacted; then every `provenance_items` row it touches, the cited record it grows `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` and each derived source's record `FOR KEY SHARE`, one at a time in id order, the lock-ahead's order, requiring each derived source live. A deletion, a correction, a redaction or a hide of a source takes that source's row `FOR UPDATE` (a DELETE does so itself; the others by an explicit lock before they change it) before it calls `withdraw_dependants()` or `lifecycle_cascade()`, so a producer's read waits for it and then sees it, and a producer that read first is waited for and then withdrawn; a redaction in place calls `lifecycle_cascade()` with its `redaction` log row, so the source's edges stay live (C1.2, C2.11 and C6.1 each bring the race that proves their part). A derived item has at least one source, every one recorded then, and stores and carries `aclOf`'s row. It moves its table into a class and its kinds into `lifecycle_kinds()`, with their resolvers, its own kind's people included (a suggested task's owner, so a hold on them keeps it: LH-55); it calls `provenance_forget()` on delete and `withdraw_dependants(…, 'source_corrected')` on a correction. The step that first stores content for a derived-mode kind (C3.5 for suggested tasks, A2.3 for kind `derived`, each later A-step for its own) replaces `lifecycle_cascade()` and `provenance_forget()`, with the same signatures, so they delete that content, logged in its own class with the same parent and through its table's guard, before the record; until then they keep such an item withdrawn. Except for kind `derived`, which 0014 already covers, the same migration replaces `provenance_sources_insert()`, the edge trigger's function, with the same signature, so an edge naming a source of its kind is refused by the same statement, message and SQLSTATE as one naming a withdrawn source on every path where a hold decides whether the item survives: with no record (deleted with its content), withdrawn and kept, and hidden in its own table while a hold keeps it (its owner's dismissal or delete awaiting the sweep: the trigger reads the kind's hidden state, or the hide marks the record unavailable). That holds only if every item of the kind has its record from when it is made; a step that lets one exist without a record has the trigger refuse a source with neither a record nor a live row in the kind's table instead. Refusing only a source with no record would move the difference to the dismissal path, not remove it. A2.3 does the same for kind `derived` if its items can be hidden while a hold keeps them. The step pins it with a DB test that a held source and a deleted one are refused alike, as LCD-37 does for kind `derived`. Every reader of the registry (A0.4's sources, C3.5's inbox, C1.2's drafts, C4.4's inserts) shows a withdrawn item as a deleted one (gone), and a source that is gone, withdrawn or not the viewer's to open alike (withheld by type), never a source's state or whether its edge is gone, so whether a hold keeps something never shows (item 10, LH-55). The first producer of a derived-mode item (C3.5's suggested tasks, unless A2.3 comes first) adds `provenance_items` to `CONTENT_TABLES` and gives the cascade, withdrawal and stale-marking their events, so check 1 refuses `PROVENANCE`'s exemption from then. C1.2 (drafts, cited), C3.5 (suggested tasks), C4.3 and C4.4 (versions and inserts, cited) and A2.3 (derived items) are its producers.

15. **The clean-up's exemption (LH-26).** T16 item 6 asked C0.10 to route the clean-up through holds and the deletion log or record its exemption. C0.10 records it, and the owner, as the DPO, confirmed it on 28 Oct 2026 (LH-48):
    - routing would break the outbox's floor: a consumer at or above it "has missed nothing", and one held subject's event kept below it would stall every consumer's horizon;
    - the 14 days are a maximum as well as a minimum, because `subject_ids` are personal data, so holding them longer would keep more personal data, not less;
    - the clean-up writes one audit row per batch, never one per row (T15 item 17), over ids of facts the base tables and the audit log keep, and a restore that brings old rows back is cleaned again within a day.

    It is recorded in the registry (`outbox`, `jobs` and `schedule_runs`, exempt under LH-26), in check 8 (the three functions and the two keys inside `purge_schedule_runs()`) and here. Not in `service_operation`'s basis reference, as T22 item 20 had said: a basis reference exists only for the legitimate-interests, deemed-consent-by-notification and other-exception bases, the DPO may choose a basis for `service_operation` that takes none, and the notice prints the reference in every employee's notice, where it says where an assessment is kept, not a retention exemption. The DPO may say it in `service_operation`'s free-text retention too. Declining it would make the clean-up a guarded path: a redesign of the outbox's floor in its own PR.

16. **OD-27's revoke (LH-27).** 0013 revokes `outbox_events`' table SELECT from `hearth_job_offboarding` and `hearth_job_revocation` and grants each every column but `actor_id` and `actor_kind`, since Postgres doesn't narrow a table-wide grant by revoking a column; a column added later is unreadable by both until a migration grants it, which fails closed. `actor_kind` goes too: no consumer reads it, and for a `case.opened` sent from the form it says a person acted. Every statement both roles run reads only granted columns (`readBatch()`, `startOf()` and `futureRows()`, and the revocation sockets job's read), and `count(*)` and `select 1` work under column grants. A grant names its table without locking it, so a consumer round in progress reads on. 032's pin of who can read the actor is now empty (LCD-31), and LCU-05 runs the statements over upgraded events. OD-27 put the revoke in "the first migration after PR 3b's worker is deployed", so that a worker still running during a deploy wouldn't fail every round; nothing is deployed anywhere, and C0a's first deploy ships PR 3b's worker and 0013 together, so no worker older than PR 3b ever runs against it (LH-49 (a)). C0.14 no longer carries it.

17. **The notice (LH-28, retired).** No change in C0.10: no generator, re-pin or DPO confirmation (C010-SPEC-12, NTC-01). `c09.3`'s "What was already collected is kept only as long as each one says" stays true in C0: nothing deletes anyone's data before C2.9, so every purpose's data is already kept past its stated period, and the access-request runbook has told the DPO to keep a refused copy's data since C0.9 PR 3b. Before its first sweep, with the C2 notice re-version, C2.9 prints a paragraph at the head of "Why your data is processed" and drops "only" from that sentence ("What was already collected is kept as long as each one says, apart from the cases under "Why your data is processed"."). Its starting text, held to `AI_COPY`:

    > Each purpose below says how long its data is kept. Records can be kept longer than that while a request for a copy of someone's data, or to correct it, is being handled, and after a request for a copy is refused, including records of yours that concern the person who made it: for at least 30 days after the refusal and, if they apply to the Personal Data Protection Commission (PDPC) for a review, until that review, any reconsideration and any appeal have all ended.

    The legal-hold clause ("It can also be kept longer while a legal hold covers it, for an investigation or legal proceedings.") joins once C1.11's `legal_holds` purpose key is active. The DPO confirms both texts then; LH-52, which asked for that confirmation in PR 1, is retired with LH-28.

18. **Writes, refusals, audit, events, logs, counts, the lock order, the lock window and access (LH-21, LH-25, LH-30, LH-31, LH-37, LH-59).**
    - **Writes (LH-30).** Every function that writes, decides or refuses a write has a coverage entry, granted or not, so check 1 reads its body: the definers `log_deletion()` (`DELETION_LOG`), `delete_case()` (`CASE_DELETED`) and `is_held()` (it only reads), and the four trigger functions, of which only `lifecycle_guard()` is SECURITY DEFINER (it must read `deletion_log`, `cases` and `legal_holds` whichever role deletes); `lifecycle_no_truncate()`, `deletion_log_kept()` and `legal_holds_kept()` are plain functions that only refuse. The three callable definers read the org from `app.org_id` and raise 42501 when it is unset, never from an argument, so no future grantee can log, delete or ask in another org (DB-06); `lifecycle_guard()`, a trigger, reads it from the deleted row instead, so a delete with no `app.org_id` (the owner by hand) is still checked and never meets 42501 (item 8). `db/tests/190_lifecycle_guard.sql` (DB-06) pins the definers whose bodies never read `app.org_id`: the pre-org lookups and `worker_org_ids()`, `create_data_access_partitions()` (it acts in no org), `transfer_cleared()` (through `transfer_send_state()`, which reads it) and `lifecycle_guard()`. Only the plain helpers, which no role may run, take `org`. `is_held()`, `log_deletion()` and `lifecycle_guard()` take no acting user and skip `can()`: the first answers one boolean to the deletion code that called it, the second only appends an ids-only row for a deletion function that checked its own caller, and the third only decides whether a delete already under way may proceed. No content table is exempt (`CONTENT_TABLES` stays empty), nor, from PR 2, any insert into `provenance_items` or `provenance_sources`, a producer's (check 1, item 14). No runtime role gains a grant: every new function has its execute revoked from PUBLIC and is granted to nobody, and no role holds a grant on `deletion_log` or `legal_holds` (LCD-04). From PR 2 (0014): the four provenance definers, `withdraw_dependants()`, `provenance_mark_stale()`, `lifecycle_cascade()` and `provenance_forget()`, are exempt under `PROVENANCE` with the writes each body makes, read the org from `app.org_id` (42501 unset), refuse outside READ COMMITTED (25000), take no acting user and write no audit row of their own, since the deletion, redaction, correction or edit that calls them checked its own caller and audits the action (item 7); `provenance_mark_stale()` may also act for what follows a rewrite of a source's stored ACL (C1.1's and C4.1's handlers of `acl.changed` `rows`, C4.1's re-ACL job), which acts for no person, as the worker's definers do, after a rewrite its own writer checked and audited, and it only marks freshness, never access or anyone's data (it sets only the item's `state`, `stale_since` and `stale_reason`, leaving its stored ACL row as it was, and that row is only a SQL pre-filter: who may read a stale item is still decided by `packages/access` from its sources), so, like the fences, it re-checks no ACC-10 role and writes no audit row; of the five trigger functions on the two tables (`CHECK`), four are plain functions that only refuse, and the deferred `provenance_items_complete()` is a definer that only refuses, reading the org from the item it checks, never `app.org_id`, because Postgres 17 fires a deferred trigger at the commit as whichever role is active then, which holds no grant on either table (`db/tests/190`'s DB-06 list names it beside `lifecycle_guard()`); `provenance_dependants()` and `provenance_lock_ahead()` are plain helpers no role may run, so they have no entry; and no role holds a grant on either table (LCD-40).
    - **SQLSTATEs (LH-25).** Two new Hearth codes, never shown to anyone: HF009 (`HELD` in `apps/server/src/db/sqlstate.ts`), the guard refusing a held row; and HF010 (`UNLOGGED_DELETE`), a delete not through the deletion code, a step before its request, or a TRUNCATE. No refusal map maps either, so a route that ever met one answers a logged 500, never words about a hold (LCV-06), and C2.9's sweeps count HF009 inside their one kept count. One code for both was the alternative, which no test or log could tell apart. The rest are existing codes: 0A000 (a plain TRUNCATE of `cases`, the owner by hand); 42501 (`delete_case()` not as Hearth, or no org set; from C1.11, the actor check and a hold on yourself); 22023 (an unknown kind or table, a kind `lifecycle_lock_target()` can't lock, `log_deletion()`'s class or cause, an S3 class or key with a class that has no S3 objects included (PRIVACY-01, R2DB-01), a cause or replay flag `delete_case()` refuses, a retention period it can't read (HOLDS-02); from C1.11, an unknown person, case or hold); 25000 (`lifecycle_held()`, and so `is_held()`, `delete_case()` and the guard, outside READ COMMITTED: HOLDS-03); 25006 (`is_held()` read-only); 23514 (`deletion_log_kept()`, a new row's time or transaction included (GUARD-01), `legal_holds_kept()` and the tables' checks, the object id's shape included (PRIVACY-01)); and 55P03 and 40P01 (409 `try_again`). From C1.11, HF006 and HF005 for the writers, and 2201W for the read's bound. From PR 2: 23514 (the provenance tables' checks and their triggers' refusals), 22023 (a kind, id, reason, cause, parent or batch the provenance definers refuse, a leftover's cause its withdrawn reason doesn't give included), 25000 (each provenance definer outside READ COMMITTED), 42501 (no org), 40001 (what was made from a record kept changing through eight rounds of the lock-ahead; 409 `try_again`), HF009 and HF010 from the guard on both tables, and, for the owner by hand, 0A000 (a plain TRUNCATE of `provenance_items`) or 55006 (a TRUNCATE in a transaction that wrote either table through its triggers).
    - **A person's own delete (LH-37)** behaves the same, held or not: it hides the item, appends the same tombstone and audit code, and the physical deletion waits; CHAT-05 keeps held versions out of view, reachable only through Rule 9 until the hold ends. "Held items refuse deletion" binds the deletion code, never what a person sees, since a visible refusal would tip the held person off. No count or quota a person sees depends on hold state, and RMS-65's "kept until" shows the policy date and, past it, "Kept while needed", never a hold (LH-55; C2.8, C2.9).
    - **Counts (LH-59).** Sweep and replay log lines, and audit rows readable by anyone who isn't a hold reader (C2.9's batch rows, like `maintenance.purge`'s), carry `deleted` and one `kept` count that folds `held`, `not_due`, `cited` and `open` together, never a kept item's id. A held count, and deletion-log counts by cause (whose `request` cause and case ids mark the DPO's request work), come only through a definer for holders of `dpo`, and of `compliance_officer` for holds. C0.16's viewer and export apply the same rule. Counts by answer for everyone was the alternative: an Owner or the person on call would learn that holds and refused copies exist.
    - **Audit, events and logs.** `delete_case()` writes `case.deleted` (item 13); from C1.11, `hold.placed`, `hold.released` and `hold.read` (item 10). No event in C0.10, no new log line, and none names a hold.
    - **The lock order (LH-21):** CLAUDE.md's, with two slots: … `sessions`, then `role_assignments` → containers (each content step's, from C1.1: conversations, then rooms, then recorded sessions, then folders, then files) → `cases` → `legal_holds` → `processing_register` → …, and, from PR 2 (0014), `provenance_items` → `provenance_sources` after `legal_holds`. The containers slot takes the steps' kinds in step order, each container before what sits in it: a recorded session (never a sign-in session) after its room, and a file after its folders, which item 12 adds for derived items' sources; a content step's definer takes the rows it locks more strongly than `FOR SHARE` in that order, and locks its own item only after `is_held()` has taken the item's people and its containers `FOR SHARE` (item 12's caller's order), claiming no slot order for those. The hold decision and the lock-ahead take containers only `FOR SHARE`, in (kind, id) order (item 12), which agrees with the slot for C1.1's and C2.4's kinds, but from C4.1 takes a file before its folders, a folder chain by id, so a folder can come before the folder it sits in, and folders and files before group DMs, rooms and recorded sessions, for every file's and folder's hold decision and lock-ahead: those `FOR SHARE` locks never wait on each other, and a writer that takes two such rows more strongly, in the slot's order, can deadlock with one, which answers 409 `try_again`. `deletion_log` rows are only inserted, so the log needs no slot. The sequences:
      - a deletion of a person's data (`delete_case()` for a linked requester, C2.9's person-scoped sweeps): the person `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` first; then `lifecycle_held()`'s locks (a re-lock of the person, containers `FOR SHARE`, their requests `FOR SHARE`); then the rows it deletes, parents before children. It takes turns with a new request, a hold placed on them, a sign-in and a deactivation;
      - every hold decision (`lifecycle_held()`, `is_held()`): the anchor people `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, then containers `FOR SHARE`, then the people's requests `FOR SHARE` (item 12);
      - a deletion of a request that isn't about a person (an outsider's): the request `FOR UPDATE`, then `lifecycle_held()`'s locks, which take no person; a derived item's deletion goes through `provenance_forget()`'s lock-ahead (below);
      - the guard re-takes the anchors `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, a no-op inside the deletion code, and out of order only in an ad hoc delete by the owner, where a deadlock answers 40P01;
      - placing a hold (C1.11): the placer's person and role rows `FOR SHARE`, then the target through `lifecycle_lock_target()` `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` (an admin action on someone else), then a cited case `FOR KEY SHARE`; releasing: the placer's rows, then the hold `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`. Two admins placing holds on each other at once can deadlock, and one gets 409;
      - a closure, reclassification or PDPC review step (`add_case_step()`, unchanged): the DPO's rows, then the case `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, which a deletion reading the requester's requests `FOR SHARE` waits for;
      - from PR 2 (0014), the cascade, a withdrawal and `provenance_forget()` lock ahead (item 14): the anchor people `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` in id order, the items' containers `FOR SHARE`, the people's open and refused requests `FOR SHARE`, then the items `FOR UPDATE` in id order, then their edges; a derive that commits during that lock-ahead brings people its next round locks after `provenance_items`, and a content step's delete path that locked its author and container before calling `provenance_forget()` (C1.1, C1.2, C4.1) lets the cascade lock the dependants' people after them, and so does a correction, redaction or hide that took its source's row `FOR UPDATE` first, a deadlock either way answering 409 `try_again` (DB-11; neither can happen before a producer of derived items exists); stale-marking takes only the items, `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` in id order; an edge's insert reads its source's record `FOR SHARE`, after its item; a producer (from C1.2) takes its content sources' rows `FOR KEY SHARE`, then every `provenance_items` row it touches, the cited record it grows `FOR NO KEY UPDATE` and each derived source's record `FOR KEY SHARE`, one at a time in id order, the lock-ahead's own order, before it writes the item and its edges, so the two never wait on each other in a cycle within a round.

      `FOR SHARE` in the hold decision was the alternative, which a request sent or logged, also `FOR SHARE`, could commit unseen behind.
    - **The lock window (LH-31).** 0013 locks the two tables live requests touch only last, together, and never waits while it holds one: each attempt asks for `cases` SHARE ROW EXCLUSIVE (CREATE TRIGGER's mode, which lets the DPO's reads and `FOR SHARE` reads through) and `case_steps` ACCESS EXCLUSIVE (ALTER CONSTRAINT's), each NOWAIT, 0.1 s apart, up to 100 tries (about 10 s, then 55P03, and the migrate step is run again: 0012's loop). A request that arrives meanwhile waits at most its 2 s `lock_timeout` and answers 409 `try_again`. The new tables' keys take SHARE ROW EXCLUSIVE on `orgs` and `principals` (which nothing writes at run time in C0 but the bootstrap's set-up), the index SHARE on `processing_restrictions` (no writer before C2.11), and OD-27's grants no lock on `outbox_events`. Nothing is locked on `sessions`, `devices`, `passkeys`, `phone_numbers`, `notice_acknowledgments`, `audit_events`, `data_access_events`, `outbox_events` or `people`. The levels were read from Postgres 17's source, and LCR-10 measures them through `pg_locks` while 0013 runs under load. 0014 adds tables and functions only: its keys take SHARE ROW EXCLUSIVE on `orgs` and `principals`, as 0013's did; replacing a function takes no table lock, and no runtime role runs any it replaces; so it needs no window, and no race measures it.
    - **Access.** No access action in either PR (PR 2's `derivedFacts()` is a pure function of stored rows, item 14): `requests.ts`, `admin.ts`, `roles.test.ts` and the Rule 9 fixture are unchanged. C1.11's three are designed in item 10.

19. **What CI checks (LH-32 to LH-35).**
    - **Check 7** (`apps/server/scripts/check-catalogues.ts`; LH-04): (a) `RETENTION_CLASSES`' Postgres and S3 classes equal `retention_class_defaults()` on every column, both ways; (b) every table of `public` that isn't a partition, the ledger aside, is in exactly one class, and every name listed is such a table; (c) each `fixedIn` interval equals its function body's, comments stripped; (d) the guard sits on exactly `GUARDED_TABLES`, each BEFORE DELETE row trigger naming its own table (its parent's, for a partition, every partition carrying the clone) beside its BEFORE TRUNCATE statement trigger, `deletion_log` carries `lifecycle_no_truncate` and `deletion_log_kept` (BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE OR DELETE, GUARD-01) and `legal_holds` carries `legal_holds_kept`, each trigger named as its function, with its type, enabled `O` and with no WHEN condition or UPDATE OF list (GUARD-04), no other table carries any of them, and every guarded table is in a holds-apply class; (e) every AI class is finite, and none is kept; (f) `HOLD_TARGET_KINDS`, `DELETION_CAUSES`, `HOLD_KINDS` and `REFUSED_COPY_HOLD` equal the database's, and `retention_period_days()` every `days` class's default; (g) in a rolled-back transaction with a scratch org, every resolver is called for every kind, container kind and holds-apply table without raising, `lifecycle_row_anchors()` returning exactly one row, so a step that moves a table into a holds-apply class, or adds a kind, without its resolver fails CI before its guard is attached.
    - **Check 8** (LH-14): every delete path decided (item 9), plain helpers included (GUARD-03), a `checked` path only into a table a leaver's reduction marks and logging each row (GUARD-02), every `guarded` path's place in the provenance registry declared, a `forgets` one calling `provenance_forget()` (item 14), and no TRUNCATE, DROP TABLE, DETACH PARTITION, replica setting or guard switched off or replaced, or a guard trigger replaced or conditioned, in a function body or a `DYNAMIC_DDL` function's strings, and every `DYNAMIC_DDL` body the one pinned with it (`bodyDdlProblems`, GUARD-05, R2CODE-01), and no function check 1 doesn't reach with a body check 8 can't read (`helperBodyProblems`, GUARD-03), with planted cases in `catalogues.test.ts` (LCV-03 to LCV-05).
    - **The composite-foreign-key check (LH-32)** in `scripts/check-migrations.sh`: every foreign key between two tables that both have `org_id` names `org_id` on both sides, at the same place in its column lists (a key to `orgs` itself aside, a partition's copy checked like its parent's), so a reference, and the cascade or hold lookup that follows it, never crosses orgs (ACC-11). It held by convention before; convention alone was the alternative. `CHECKS_ONLY=1` runs the script's checks on a database as it stands, so `scripts/check-schema-digest.sh` proves the check refuses two planted keys that leave `org_id` out and passes their composite twin.
    - **DB tests:** LCD-01 to LCD-20, LCD-27 to LCD-33 and LCD-41, and from PR 2 LCD-34 to LCD-40 (the files above), each as the role it tests, the owner's and the superuser's refusals included, and `198_lifecycle_isolation.sql` run under REPEATABLE READ (its first line, `-- test-db: repeatable read`; HOLDS-03), and from PR 2 the four provenance definers' refusals, with the definers' cases for every persona of `fixtures/two_orgs.sql`. The fixture gains, per org, an active hold on the departed DPO (`ec`), a released one (`ed`) and a `deletion_log` row, all inserted as the owner; a8, whose device 070 and 100 delete, is held by nothing; from PR 2, per org, a derived item made from two made-up messages (`ee`) and a cited draft citing one (`ef`), written through the triggers; from PR 2, 190's DB-06 list names `provenance_items_complete()`. LCD-21 to LCD-26 are C1.11's.
    - **Races (LH-21),** in an org of their own, each in both orders where one can wait: LCR-01 (a hold placed, against a request's deletion), LCR-02 (a PDPC review step, against it), LCR-03 (a request for a copy and one for a correction sent, against the requester's other request's deletion), LCR-04 (a release not yet committed), LCR-10 (0013 applied under load, locking none of the busy tables), LCR-11 (two deletions for one requester), LCR-15 (`is_held()`, against a request being sent) and LCR-16 (a hold citing a request, against its deletion). From PR 2, in an org of their own: LCR-12 (a cascade against a hold placed on the person another source of its item is about, the cascade waiting in its lock-ahead on the person's row while holding no item), LCR-13 (a derive against its source's deletion: no live item made from a deleted one either way; and a cascade against a derive in progress, whose next round waits on the new item's other source's person while the new item is still free), LCR-14 (two cascades with a shared dependant; and a producer reading two items in id order against their message's cascade, which holds nothing of the higher while it waits on the lower, with no deadlock), LCR-17 (a person-scoped deletion against a cascade: while the cascade waits on the person, the deletion takes the item) LCR-18 (an edge's insert against its source's withdrawal: the withdrawal's next round holds the citing item `FOR UPDATE`), LCR-19 (two cascades over items made from one person's device, the first held up on a producer's read of its item: it holds the person `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, so the second waits on the person holding nothing of its own item, with no deadlock) and LCR-20 (a cascade over an item made from two people's devices against a transaction taking them in id order, as a hold decision does: the cascade waits on the lower holding nothing of the higher, so that transaction then takes the higher). `race()` runs its first step whole before the second starts, so it shows that a step waits but never the order it takes its locks in; where a case claims the lock-ahead's order or strength (people before items, people in id order and `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, items in id order, each round's people before its items), it drives the two steps by hand (`phased()`): the second starts while the first is part done, then the first takes its next lock under a 500 ms `lock_timeout`, below the 1 s `deadlock_timeout`, or a third connection tries a row `NOWAIT`, so a lock taken out of order or too weak shows as a timeout, or as a row held that should be free or free that should be held. Each of LCR-12 to LCR-14, LCR-17 and LCR-18 fails against a planted lock-ahead that does nothing, and against one that breaks the order it claims (items before people, no people, descending or unsorted ids, one round, or a weaker item lock); LCR-19 fails against one that does nothing or takes the people `FOR SHARE` (where the two cascades would deadlock) or `FOR KEY SHARE`, and LCR-20 against one that takes them in descending id order or `FOR KEY SHARE`. A people lock left unsorted can't be told from the real one, since `people_org_id_id_key`'s scan returns them in id order anyway. LCR-05 to LCR-09 are C1.11's.
    - **The upgrade check (LH-34):** `UP_TO=0013 scripts/check-migrations.sh && scripts/check-lifecycle-upgrade.sh`, its data built at 0012 (`UP_TO` builds up to the migration before the one it names) over the seed and an org far from UTC holding refused copies (one under review, one past its window, an outsider's and a leaver's), an open correction sent in Hearth, old answered complaints, each member's sign-in records and acknowledgement, audit and access rows, and outbox events with actors. Every row is unchanged (LCU-01); the schema is a fresh build's (LCU-02); `is_held()` (LCU-03) and `delete_case()` (LCU-04) answer as items 11 and 13 say, with the guard on exactly its tables and no new DELETE grant; OD-27's column grants hold and the consumers' statements run (LCU-05); the outbox's types and every org's registers, flags and settings are as they were (LCU-07); and `retention_class_defaults()` equals the code's classes (LCU-08). The other upgrade checks apply 0013 over their own data and stay green (LCU-09). LCU-06 is C1.11's. 0014 (PR 2) rides this check, changing no row: LCU-10 asserts its two tables empty with RLS and no grant, no runtime role able to run its functions, `lifecycle_kinds()` equal to `HOLD_KINDS`, and the cascade and `is_held()` answering over the upgraded data; LCU-04 holds the guard to five tables. Riding `check-cases-upgrade.sh`, built at 0011 where no request exists, was the alternative.
    - **Unit:** LCS-01 to LCS-08 (`packages/shared/src/retention.test.ts`: keys and tables; purposes; AI purposes both ways; finite periods and `follows` chains that resolve, within their ranges; D12's classes never indefinite; the sample table through `heldUntil()`; CloudWatch's values; the vocabularies) and LCS-09 (no catalogue name in a forbidden namespace, in `packages/shared/src/catalogues.test.ts`); LCV-01 and LCV-02 (the Valkey pins), LCV-06 (`HELD` and `UNLOGGED_DELETE` unmapped) and LCV-08 (192's samples equal to the JSON file), in `apps/server/src/lifecycle/retention.test.ts`, and LCV-03 to LCV-05 (checks 7 and 8 on planted input), in `apps/server/scripts/catalogues.test.ts`; from PR 2 LCA-04 (`packages/access/src/provenance.test.ts`), LCA-05 (R3-01b) and LCV-07 (`apps/server/src/access/provenance-rows.test.ts`). LCS-10 is retired with the notice change.
    - **The rules suite (LH-35):** R7-02b moves to C1.2 and R7-05h to C4.1, their C0.10 part (`is_held()` and the guard) delivered; R7-11b is a new core todo for C0.17, "the deletion log replays after a restore", so R7-11 drops that clause; R3-01b and R7-11 run (PR 2).

No other suite changes: C0.10 has no route, job, consumer or event, every suite's write sweep still fails a runtime write without its event, and the `first-owner` suite is unchanged, since the set-up writes none of C0.10's tables.
    - **Guards (LH-33):** LCG-01 (the design ids, in the preamble); `scripts/guards/lifecycle.test.ts`, each case first proved on planted files: LCG-02, no S3 delete, lifecycle, versioning, legal-hold, retention or Object Lock command named in `apps/`, `packages/`, `infra/` or `scripts/`, and no lifecycle rule in `infra/` that expires objects without a tag filter, outside an allow-list that stays empty until C0.15 adds its S3 deletion module and C0.12 the tag-filtered backstop, each with its reason; LCG-03, no migration numbered above 0013 holds, outside its function bodies, a `truncate` (but in a trigger's event list), `drop table`, `detach partition`, `disable trigger`, `drop trigger`, `enable always trigger`, `enable replica trigger`, a `create or replace trigger` that names a guard's trigger or function, a guard's trigger created with a `when` condition or an `update of` list, a replacement of `lifecycle_guard()`, `lifecycle_no_truncate()`, `deletion_log_kept()`, `legal_holds_kept()` or `lifecycle_held()`, or a `delete from` a holds-apply or kept table or `deletion_log`, unless its file and statement are on the guard's reviewed list with why; LCG-04, no runtime file under `apps/server/src/` outside `apps/server/src/lifecycle/` deletes from or truncates such a table; LCG-05, no file in `apps/`, `scripts/` or `db/tests/` that sets `session_replication_role` to `replica` deletes from or truncates a guarded table before it sets it back to origin (or resets it), or, for a transaction-local setting (`set local`, `set_config(…, true)`), its transaction ends (a COMMIT or ROLLBACK, never a ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT; in `db/tests/`, where `scripts/test-db.sh` refuses a test that ends its transaction, never; R2CODE-02), or the file ends. Check 8 alone was the alternative, which can't see S3, a migration's top-level DROP, or code a superuser runs in replica mode. And LCG-06 (`later-steps.test.ts`, LS-03 to LS-05): every step item 1 leaves something to, and every step item 20's interface table names, has a bullet in item 20's inherit list, and each such step's BUILD-PLAN row names C0.10. LCG-07 (`design-ids.test.ts`, DOCS-07): every line of this document naming LH-45's or LH-46's confirmation gives it one deadline, before PR 1 merges, and no other; and RB-06 (`runbooks.test.ts`, PRIVACY-02): the runbooks name the log dialog's leaver label as it reads and say that a request is never linked after it is logged, with a legal hold on their person standing in for the link.

20. **Open points for the owner, accepted residuals, and what later steps inherit.** The owner answered LH-44 to LH-55, LH-60 and LH-61 on 28 Oct 2026, accepting every recommendation, and every default, LH-01 to LH-43 and LH-56 to LH-59, as written ("Accept all (Recommended)"):
    - **LH-44 (a), with (i) and (iii):** who places, releases and reads legal holds, and a hold nobody can release (item 10). Declined: Owners too (Owners and Admins would learn of investigations); a second approver for releases (a hold could be left unreleasable); a case required for every hold; refusing a placement that leaves no other reader (the sole DPO couldn't be held before a second Compliance Officer is appointed).
    - **LH-45 (a):** an open request for a copy or a correction holds, from receipt to closure (item 11). Declined: copies only, or a refused copy alone. The DPO's confirmation of whether the PDPA's offence of disposing of a record to evade an access or correction request applies is given before PR 1 merges, in PR 1's "Before merging" steps.
    - **LH-46 (a):** the refused-copy hold runs 14 days past the statutory 30, 44 in all (item 11). Declined: another margin; exactly 30, with a "no review notified" step 0012 doesn't have. The DPO's confirmation of regulation 8's current wording is given before PR 1 merges, in PR 1's "Before merging" steps.
    - **LH-47 (a):** requests kept 731 days after closure for every outcome, 90 days to 10 years, never indefinite (item 5). Declined: shorter for cases closed as logged in error, a duplicate or not a request; no default until set.
    - **LH-48, yes:** the recorded exemptions from holds (items 2, 6, 9 and 15). Declining one makes that path guarded.
    - **LH-49 (a):** OD-27's revoke in PR 1 (item 16). Declined: C0.14 carrying it.
    - **LH-50 (a):** a derived item is held while any source is held, and an item with a source Hearth can't judge yet is kept; cited items never inherit (item 14; built in PR 2, 0014). Declined: only direct holds on derived items.
    - **LH-51 (a):** replay and the restore marker (item 7). Declined: replay deleting regardless of holds (a bypass any role able to set it could use); the operator re-applying holds and releases from the archive as the owner (handling confidential hold records and writing them under someone else's name).
    - **LH-52: retired** with LH-28 (item 17).
    - **LH-53, confirmed:** the figures in item 2.
    - **LH-54 (a):** C0 preserves s22A's copy by blocking deletion only (item 11). Declined: keeping the extract under `local-data/` until the hold ends (a change to CLAUDE.md's access-request exception and the runbook); closing a partial refusal as refused.
    - **LH-55 (a):** what a hold on a person covers from C1.1, and held items looking like any other (items 10, 12 and 18). Declined: only what they authored; showing holds to participants (it would tip off the person under investigation).
    - **LH-60 (a):** the place-hold and release-hold action lands with C1.11: C1.4 keeps the internal alpha to the builder and C0 testers, on non-prod with synthetic content, until C1.11 and C1.13, so no real person's content can need a hold in between. Declined: C1.2 carrying it (about 15 files and a migration read more).
    - **LH-61 (a):** an access-request hold keeps the whole person scope until C6.1 (item 11). Declined: only sign-in records, acknowledgements and requests (content a refused requester asked about could be deleted during the hold).

    The DPO's confirmation of the two legal points, whether the PDPA's offence of disposing of a record to evade a request applies (LH-45) and regulation 8's current end wording (LH-46), is given before PR 1 merges, in PR 1's "Before merging" steps. Still open, for the DPO or a later step: the PDPA's exception for an investigation not yet complete (C6.1, LH-54); the remaining retention periods PDPA-09 proposes (PRD Q13, before the M1 pilot: the registry keeps them `proposed`, and C1.13 answers them for chat); whether `compliance_records` should name a purpose other than `accounts`; and revoking `hearth_job_offboarding`'s read of `sessions` for the retired sockets handler, which is C0.16's (T19 item 2).

    Accepted residuals, each in the PR: the owner's credential can switch a guard off by DDL, and a superuser can set replica mode, each a direct production change CLAUDE.md forbids (item 8); until C2.9 guards them, the owner's credential can delete a held person's sign-in records, acknowledgements and log rows by hand (nothing at run time can; LCG-03 and LCG-04 refuse it in migrations and server code); partitions are guarded only by guards and review (dropping, detaching or truncating one fires no trigger): LCG-03 refuses it at a later migration's top level and check 8 in any function body; the deletion log lives only in the database until C0.17's shipper runs, with nothing to lose before C2.9; the archived hold and case facts are ids, codes and dates the restore's operator passes on, never a reason; no hold can be placed before C1.11 (LH-60); a requester without an account holds only the case; an access-request hold keeps the whole person scope until C6.1 (LH-61); the guard can deadlock in an ad hoc delete by the owner (40P01); a hold decision makes that person's sign-in wait for the deleting transaction (bounded by 30 s, normally milliseconds), which C2.9 measures for large purges; from PR 2, a cascade, a withdrawal or `provenance_forget()` can deadlock with a derive that commits during its lock-ahead, or, from C1.1, with a content step's delete path that locked its author and container first, or a correction, redaction or hide that locked its source first (409 `try_again`; none before a producer of derived items exists); from C4.1, a writer that locks a folder or a file and another container more strongly than `FOR SHARE`, in the lock order's containers slot, can deadlock with a hold decision or a lock-ahead, which take containers in (kind, id) order (409 `try_again`; item 18); stale-marking can miss a derive that commits during an ordinary edit of its source, since producers read sources `FOR KEY SHARE`, which an edit's lock lets through (staleness is freshness, never access: A2.3 compares each edge's `source_version`; a correction, redaction or hide takes the source `FOR UPDATE`, so none of those is missed); what was made from a deleted source is withdrawn but left for C2.9 when any chain from it reaches 32 links (a chain that deep, or a cycle a producer breaking LH-43 left) or the cascade's batch of hold decisions runs out; a closure so large that its withdrawal runs past 30 s rolls back the deletion that called it, whole; a derived-mode item of a kind whose content no step deletes yet stays withdrawn and kept; from C1.11, holds reveal themselves to their readers by absence (a reader never sees a hold on themselves, so with one DPO who is also the founder a hold on them is known only to Compliance Officers, and a held reader could notice a gap in the audit ids, which rolled-back transactions make an unreliable signal); a hold change with no other reader sends no alert; a hold nobody can release keeps its target's rows until the Owner appoints another Compliance Officer (LH-44); Valkey, logs, backups and quarantine copies are outside holds by name (LH-48, LH-57); a cited item keeps a gone source's id, shown withheld by type; a producer that breaks LH-43 could close a derived cycle in a race, which the walk's `UNION` ends and the access core reads as nobody's (R3-20); the guard's per-row cost on large purges is C2.9's to measure; and the lock levels were read from Postgres 17's source, which LCR-10 and LCD-10 then measured.

    PR 1's "Before merging" steps also cover the owner's read of migration 0013 (the two new tables, their grants (none) and triggers; the guard on `cases`, `case_steps` and `legal_holds`; `case_steps`' key made deferrable; `delete_case()`, `retention_period_days()`, `is_held()` and the hold decision with its containment seam; OD-27's revoke; the lock window); the DPO's confirmation of the recorded exemptions (LH-48), the retention defaults (the cases default, LH-47, and LH-53's figures), the margin (LH-46), the open-request holds (LH-45) and their scope (LH-61), and the current wording of s22A and regulation 8 of the PDP Regulations 2021; and the DPO's read of the three changed files in `docs/runbooks/`.

    - **The consumer-by-consumer interface:** each later step, and the C0.10 seam it relies on, so the two can be checked against each other. A core step brings its own migration anyway; what C0.10 fixes is stable SQL signatures, vocabularies extended by replacing a function (never by altering a check), the declared classes, and AI seams that need no migration.

    - **What later steps inherit,** each also in its BUILD-PLAN row:

C0.11 decides each in TECH-SPEC T24, and its PRs build them: the trail and the break-glass log are kept 731 days under Object Lock (GOVERNANCE for the first week, then COMPLIANCE), and one lifecycle rule per bucket expires each version once its lock has passed, so PR 2 makes `infra/src/foundation/log-archive.ts` LCG-02's one `LIFECYCLE_ALLOWED` entry (C11-D18, C11-D19); the break-glass log and the network logs are logs classes of their own, exempt from holds, as the DPO confirmed (C11-D22, C11-Q10, T24 item 9; PR 1); PR 2 creates the archive bucket with Object Lock from creation, its own key and no reader until C0.17 (C11-D21); PR 2's `break-glass.md` puts replica mode in its *Don't* (C11-D68); the event trigger is weighed and not built, since it couldn't stop the migrate login, which acts as `hearth_owner`, the master user could drop it and `scripts/schema-digest.sql` doesn't see event triggers, so C0.12's parameter group logs every DDL statement and connection with its login instead (C11-D67); and the Blue/Green runbook is written in C0.11, with the shipper's step in words until C0.17 adds its *Code paths* entry (C11-D71).
      - **C0.12:** a bucket per S3 class, versioned, with Object Lock enabled at creation for `session_media`; lifecycle rules that only change storage class, the tag-filtered backstop joining LCG-02's allow-list; ElastiCache's snapshot retention 0, no persistence and slow-log delivery off, or each joins the registry with its period; realtime's ACL gains `XTRIM` on its streams.

      - **C1.1:** `messages`' tables move into their class; its delete path is a definer that locks the author, then the conversation, calls `is_held()`, logs and calls `provenance_forget()`, declared `forgets` in `DELETE_PATHS` (check 8, item 9), and its tables join `GUARDED_TABLES`; `message`, `channel`, `dm` and `group_dm` join `lifecycle_kinds()`, with their anchors by stored creation context; `lifecycle_containers_of()` returns a message's conversation and `lifecycle_lock_target()` locks a conversation (LH-56), with a race of a conversation's hold against a message's deletion; `CONTENT_TABLES` gains them; `lifecycle_containers_of()` also returns, for a derived-mode item, every live `channel`, `dm` or `group_dm` source in its closure and every live message source's conversation (item 12), with a race of a hold placed on a source against a cascade, and against `provenance_forget()`'s leftover path; its delete path's call of `provenance_forget()` comes after it locks the author and the conversation, so the cascade's people come after them (item 18); its handler of `acl.changed` `rows` marks what was made from a source whose stored ACL was rewritten stale (`provenance_mark_stale(…, 'source_acl_changed')`, item 14).
      - **C1.2:** edit and delete call `is_held()` in their definers, people before the item (item 12), with `apps/server/src/lifecycle/holds.ts` and its import guard; held earlier versions and deleted content go to a table of the `held_versions` class with no `hearth_app` SELECT, deleted when the hold ends; drafts record cited provenance, which may gain sources later, through a writer whose coverage entry names an event for its inserts (check 1 refuses an exempt one, item 14); an edit that corrects, and a hide, withdraw what was made from the message, each taking its row `FOR UPDATE` first (item 14); its drafts show a source that is gone or withdrawn as one the viewer can't open, withheld by type (item 14); R7-02b; it updates `docs/runbooks/README.md`'s "Keeping and deleting" section. C1.11's hold action stays with C1.11 (LH-60 (a)).
