      - **C2.4:** `room` and `session` join `lifecycle_kinds()`, with a session's room as its container and its participants as its anchors (LH-55, LH-56), and `lifecycle_lock_target()` locks a room and a session; its replacement of `lifecycle_containers_of()` keeps C1.1's branches and also returns, for a derived-mode item, every live `room` or `session` source in its closure and every live session source's room (item 12), with a race of a hold placed on a source against a cascade, and against `provenance_forget()`'s leftover path.
      - **C2.6:** its segments are objects of the `session_media` class (120 days after the session ended, decided, in C0.12's bucket with Object Lock), which C2.9 deletes version by version through the deletion code.
      - **C2.8:** "kept until" on a participant's session page is the policy date from `retention_period_days()`, worked out by the server in the org's zone, and past it the same "Kept while needed" as any session past its date, never a hold; no count a participant sees depends on hold state (LH-55, item 18). This replaces RMS-65's "and legal holds".
      - **C2.9:** the sweeps over every class through the deletion code, with set-based hold functions to pre-filter batches and its own job role; the guard on each table its sweeps delete from (check 8 forces it), each such path declared `notASource` with the id that records why, or `forgets` (item 9), measured for `audit_events` and `data_access_events`, where a statement-level guard with a transition table may replace the row trigger; the per-org settings table and setters (ADM-07) within C0.10's ranges, replacing `retention_period_days()`, under the period-change rule (LH-38); the cases sweep calling `delete_case()` and counting its answers (`gone`, `open`, `not_due`, `held`, `cited`, `deleted`); sweep logs and non-hold-reader audit rows carrying only `deleted` and one `kept` count (LH-59); a leaver's profile reduction as a held-checked definer that takes the person `FOR NO KEY UPDATE`, asks `lifecycle_held()`, skips a held person, audits each reduction and logs each working-hours row it deletes (a `checked` path, which check 8 lets delete from no other table: GUARD-02), with LCG-04 extended to it (LH-58); `deletion_log`'s purge after 731 days; `legal_holds`' purge 731 days after release, its age in its body; `data_access_events` deleted by row, and a month's partition dropped only when empty; withdrawn derived items swept; "kept until" for the DPO only; the Object Lock worker re-reading holds through its own definer; no sweep while a restore marker is set (LH-51); the reminder for classes kept while the org exists, with C0.14; and before its first sweep, with the C2 notice re-version, a new generator printing item 17's paragraph, and the legal-hold clause once C1.11's purpose key is active.
      - **C2.11:** a restriction citing a case keeps it (`delete_case()` answers `cited`), and C2.11 decides whether a lifted restriction lets go of its case; a correction or redaction it carries out withdraws what was made from its source; it takes the source's row `FOR UPDATE` before it withdraws or cascades, and a redaction in place calls `lifecycle_cascade()` with its `redaction` log row, so the source's edges stay live and a hold on the source still keeps what was made from it (item 14).
      - **C3.5:** the producers' contract (item 14), its suggested tasks derived items that keep their owner-only rule, `suggested_task` joining `lifecycle_kinds()` with its owner as its people; it replaces `lifecycle_cascade()` and `provenance_forget()`, with the same signatures, so a withdrawn suggestion nothing holds goes with its content, and a withdrawn one leaves its owner's inbox as a deleted one does, never reading as kept (item 10); and it replaces `provenance_sources_insert()`, with the same signature, so an edge naming a suggested task refuses one deleted with its record, one withdrawn and kept, and one hidden while a hold keeps it alike, with one message and SQLSTATE (item 14's producers' contract, with its DB test); as the first producer of a derived-mode item, unless A2.3 comes first, it adds `provenance_items` to `CONTENT_TABLES` and gives the cascade, withdrawal and stale-marking their events.
      - **C4.1:** `drive_items` and `drive_trash` move in; `folder` and `file` join `lifecycle_kinds()`, reading the owner and the stored creation context, with the folder chain in `lifecycle_containers_of()` and folders and files in `lifecycle_lock_target()`; its replacement of `lifecycle_containers_of()` keeps C1.1's and C2.4's branches and also returns, for a derived-mode item, every live `folder` or `file` source in its closure and every live file or folder source's folder chain (item 12), so a hold on a folder holds what was made from a file in it, with a race of a hold placed on a source against a cascade, and against `provenance_forget()`'s leftover path; the purge and the Trash's clean-up through the deletion code, each logging a file's row and its object as one `drive_trash` (or `drive_items`) row with `s3_class` and `s3_key`, which `log_deletion()` already takes (R2DB-01), then calling `provenance_forget()` for the item it deletes, with cause `cascade` and that row as parent (item 14), after `is_held()` has locked the owner and the folder chain (item 12's caller's order), so the cascade locks the dependants' people after them, a deadlock answering 409 `try_again` (item 18); its handling of a rewritten stored ACL (`acl.changed` `rows`, the re-ACL job) marks what was made from the item stale (`provenance_mark_stale(…, 'source_acl_changed')`, item 14); R7-05h.
      - **C4.3:** the producers' contract (item 14): snapshots carry a provenance id, cited, with a composite key to `provenance_items`.
      - **C4.4:** the producers' contract (item 14): inserted AI text is cited provenance, which grows with each insert.
      - **C4.5:** version history under holds: its versions are in the `derived_caches` class, deleted with their source, and its garbage collection is a delete path check 8 decides that calls `is_held()` in its definer, people before the item (items 9 and 12).
      - **C5.8:** its purge handlers for sheets and decks are delete paths check 8 decides (item 9), each through the deletion code, and its collectors join the registry C6.1 builds on.
      - **C6.1:** the DPO's deletion-log read, "was this deleted, and when", by object id, never by person (item 7); collectors narrowing an access-request hold to the data the request covers (LH-61, item 11); revisiting held versions, and whether a held person's access request may withhold a hold under the PDPA's exception for an investigation not yet complete (LH-54); its console reading `heldUntil()` (item 11); and the legal-hold export from stable hold ids and their history.
      - **A0.1:** decides who sets the AI retention classes C0.10 declared (ADM-05, ADM-07); nothing to lift, since they have no setter.
      - **A0.4:** sources on AI output from `provenance_sources`, filtered per viewer through `filterReadable`, a source the viewer can't open shown withheld by type; thumbs-up and thumbs-down feedback in the `ai_feedback` class; a source that is gone or withdrawn is shown as one the viewer can't open, never its state (item 14).
      - **A1.3:** transcripts go in the `transcripts` class C0.10 declared (1,095 days after the session ended, finite, never indefinite, holds apply; item 4), with their tables, resolvers and cascades its own.
      - **A2.3:** the producers' contract (item 14) for derived items, and corrections withdrawing what was made from their source; it replaces `lifecycle_cascade()` and `provenance_forget()`, with the same signatures, so a derived item's content goes with its record, and, if a derived item can be hidden while a hold keeps it, `provenance_sources_insert()` so an edge refuses a hidden one as it refuses a deleted one (item 14's producers' contract); if C3.5 hasn't, it adds `provenance_items` to `CONTENT_TABLES` with those events; its re-check reads `indexClassOf` live as well as the readers, the stored `index_class` being a pre-filter like the stored ACL row.
      - **A3.3:** "who knows about X" (SRCH-05) stores its people inferences in the `people_inferences` class.
      - **A3.4:** entities (BRAIN-05) store their people inferences in the `people_inferences` class, withdrawn with their sources as derived items.
      - **Never:** an AI feature reads holds, the deletion log or `is_held()` (Rule 9); no grant to any role the A-stages add, and no event for holds.

## T24: the AWS landing zone, network, compute and the deploy pipeline (C0.11), defaults for the owner to confirm or change

The C11 ids in this section are C0.11's: C11-D01 to C11-D79 are the decisions C0.11 takes, each a default weighed against the alternative its item names, and C11-Q01 to C11-Q11 are the questions only the owner can answer (and, as D35 makes the owner the DPO, the DPO). They are not C0.10's LH ids (T23), C0.9's PD or OD ids (T22), C0.8's AQ or Q ids (T20) nor the PRD's decisions, and this file always writes them whole, as "C11-D22", never "D22", which is the PRD's. `C11-Q01` is never read as T20's `Q01`, and `C11-` ends in neither `PD-`, `OD-`, `AQ-` nor `LH-`, so no other id guard reads one. Every C11 id that code cites is one this section records, and the decision index below has a row for every one from C11-D01 and C11-Q01 to the last the index row names, with no gap, which the guard reads from the rows alone (`scripts/guards/design-ids.test.ts`). PR 1 writes the whole index, so that holds from the first PR while the numbered items fill in PR by PR. Test ids: IAC (the CDK assertion tests, IAC-01 to IAC-24), IG (the infra source guard, `scripts/guards/infra.test.ts`, IG-01 to IG-12), DPW (the deploy-workflow guard, DPW-01 to DPW-14), RPB (the readiness probe), SMK (the smoke checks, with SMK-T for their unit tests) and LS-06 (a later-steps case).

One T24 covers C0.11's five PRs. None adds a migration or touches the Owner bootstrap script:
- **PR 1,** `feat(infra): CDK workspace, infra guards and log classes (C0.11)`: the `infra/` workspace with its dependencies, both entry stubs, its configuration, fixture and test helpers; the infra guard's cases that apply so far and the design-ids C11 case; the two log classes; CLAUDE.md's owner's-read extension and the Infrastructure section's workspace bullets; this decision index and items 9, 10 and 31; T23's edits (items 2, 6 and 20).
- **PR 2,** `feat(infra): landing zone: organisation, guardrails, identity, trail, break-glass and alarms (C0.11)`: items 1 to 8, 11, 17, 25, 29's runbooks, 30 and 32.
- **PR 3,** `feat(infra): workload network, Valkey, ECS services, load balancer and restore stage (C0.11)`: items 12 to 16, 18, 19, 24, 26 and 33, with LS-06, and the T18, T19, T21 and T22 edits.

The owner decided on 30 Oct 2026, in the Claude Code session that day, each as the design recommended:
- **C11-Q01** ("Yes, switch on (Recommended)"): a single-node `noeviction` Valkey and a generated `AUTH_SECRET` are pulled forward from C0.12 (C11-D35, item 14), and every service runs live in non-prod and prod at its own size, so approved promotion and a prod rollback are proved inside C0.11. Declined: desired count 0 until C0.12 PR 1, with C0.11's Done moved after it in BUILD-PLAN; a minimal RDS pulled forward too, which would blur C0.12, whose parameter group and rotation come before any personal data; prod at non-prod's shape until C0.12.
- **C11-Q02** ("Live account, sealed VPC (Recommended)"): four accounts, with the restore stage an isolated VPC in the prod account, which keeps RDS point-in-time restore's 15-minute RPO (C11-D01, C11-D45; items 1 and 18). The stacks are account-agnostic, so C0.17 can move it without a redesign. Declined: a fifth `restore` account, where a restore needs a point-in-time restore in prod first, then a snapshot share or copy, and a carve-out in the snapshot-sharing deny; a sixth, security account.
- **C11-Q03** ("Approve the wording (Recommended)"): CLAUDE.md's changes as the design wrote them (the deploy pipeline's carve-out, C0.9 OD-31's sentence, the S3 exception, the Infrastructure section and the owner's-read extension), each landing with the PR that builds what it describes, so CLAUDE.md never states a control the code doesn't have yet. PR 1 lands the owner's-read extension (item 31) and the Infrastructure section's workspace bullets. Three of those differ from the design's text, and PR 1's "Before merging" step names them for the owner's read: the model bullet is shortened to what holds before PR 2 (no model action allowed in `infra/`, and every deny rendered from `models.ts`'s two lists), since `hearth-no-models`, the bootstrap template's deny, IAC-01, IAC-24 and IG-10 are PR 2's, which restores the approved wording; the workspace bullet says an agent synthesises only with no AWS credentials or profile, since the CDK CLI calls STS for a default account whenever it finds credentials; and IG-01's bullet names the organisation's, OUs' and Identity Center's ids, which IG-01 refuses too. Declined: other wording; keeping "CI never connects to production" literal and moving deploys to an AWS-side pipeline, at the cost of a second CI system.
- **C11-Q10,** as the DPO ("Confirm both (Recommended)"): the two new log classes, `break_glass_log` for 731 days and `network_logs` for 90 days, each exempt from holds (item 9).

Still open, each until the PR or step that needs it: C11-Q04 and C11-Q05 before PR 4 merges; C11-Q06's premises before PR 2 merges, its disclosure before C0.12 builds the DPO's access to the bootstrap secret, and C0.13's dashboards' path (read through break-glass, recommended, or standing metrics-only access) before C0.13; C11-Q07, C11-Q08 and C11-Q09 before PR 2 merges (until C11-Q08 is answered, `infra/src/config/profiles.ts` names `.invalid` placeholder domains, which the landing-zone entry refuses for a real target); and C11-Q11, which the build follows as recommended (plain HTTP between the load balancer and the tasks inside the private network for C0, TLS on the internet hop, and to the data stores from C0.12) and C0.19 closes.

They are pinned by, each PR adding its own (item 28): from PR 1, `packages/shared/src/retention.test.ts` (LCS-07 and the C11-D22 case), `scripts/guards/infra.test.ts` (the IG cases that apply so far), the design-ids C11 case in `scripts/guards/design-ids.test.ts`, and `infra/`'s own unit tests of its configuration; from PR 2 and PR 3, the IAC assertion tests and the remaining IG cases; from PR 3, RPB-01 to RPB-05 and LS-06; from PR 4, DPW-01 to DPW-14 and SMK-T01 to SMK-T08; and the smoke checks SMK-01 to SMK-07 in every deploy.

9. **Retention classes, with the DPO's confirmation (C11-D22, C11-Q10).** C0.11 adds two classes to `RETENTION_CLASSES` (`packages/shared/src/retention.ts`) and widens two others' `covers`, all `store: 'logs'`. A logs class has no SQL mirror: `retention_class_defaults()` lists only the Postgres and S3 classes (`RETENTION_DB_STORES`), so check 7 is unchanged, C0.11 adds no migration and the flow logs needn't wait for one (C11-D32). LCS-07 pins the logs classes' keys, now six, and the C11-D22 case pins the two new classes' facts. The registry now holds 54 classes (T23 item 2).
    - **`break_glass_log` (new).** The break-glass log (C11-D13, built in PR 2): each use's open and close entries, holding the environment, the reason reference, the runbook followed, the modes run and the ids a run printed, a case reference and a DPO's person id among them, never a name, number or address, in the log-archive account's `hearth-break-glass-log-*` bucket. 731 days from each entry's time, under the bucket's Object Lock (GOVERNANCE for the first week, then COMPLIANCE: C11-D18), deleted by the bucket's own retention, once each version's lock has passed (C11-D19). The figure equals `cloudtrail`'s on purpose: the entries explain the CloudTrail events of the same sessions, so they must last as long. Purpose `security_audit`; set by code from C0.11; never indefinite. Exempt from holds as security audit, recorded here (decision `T24 item 9`): this extends LH-48's list, which names CloudTrail but not this log (T23 item 20).
    - **`network_logs` (new).** The VPC flow logs of every VPC (C11-D32, built in PR 3), to `/hearth/<env>/vpc-flow`: the address of every client that reaches the internet-facing load balancer, and internal addresses, ports and accept or reject, never content. 90 days from each record, by CloudWatch's retention; purpose `security_audit`; set by code from C0.11. Exempt from holds, as LH-48's CloudWatch logs are (decision `T24 item 9`): a sign-in's address is also in the audit log, which holds cover. They show attempted egress, which GuardDuty's findings don't show and C0.19's egress check reads, for a few dollars a month.
    - **`cloudtrail`, its `covers` widened** to "the organisation trail in the log-archive account, with Object Lock, and its CloudWatch Logs copy in the management account for the alarms and the quarterly review" (C11-D17), and its `deletedBy` to "the log-archive bucket’s retention, and CloudWatch’s for its copy", since the copy's log group keeps the same 731 days; its 731 days, setter and exemption are unchanged. `app_logs`' `covers` names the migrate, probe and bastion tasks' log groups (C11-D40).
    - **Both new classes stay `proposed`,** like every other C0.11 logs class, and `infra/` reads them from the registry, never from a second copy, through `infra/src/config/retention.ts`, which refuses a class not stored in the logs and a figure CloudWatch doesn't accept: the break-glass bucket's default retention and expiry read `RETENTION_CLASSES.break_glass_log.period.days` (IAC-06, PR 2) and the flow-log group's retention `RETENTION_CLASSES.network_logs.period.days` (PR 3), each a value CloudWatch accepts (`CLOUDWATCH_RETENTION_DAYS`, LCS-07). The break-glass log's figure is S3's Object Lock days, which CloudWatch never reads, but it keeps to the list anyway.
    - **The DPO's confirmation (C11-Q10, 30 Oct 2026).** Both classes keep people's data: a case reference and a DPO's person id for 731 days under a lock nobody can lift, and client addresses for 90 days. So the DPO confirmed both periods, their purpose and their exemption from holds on 30 Oct 2026, before PR 1 merges, as its "Before merging" steps ask. Declined: flow logs for 30 days, or none, which would leave C0.19's egress check nothing to read; another period for the break-glass log, which would still have to cover the 731 days of the CloudTrail events it explains.
    - **Not a class yet:** the load balancer's access logs. They would go to an S3 bucket that needs an untagged lifecycle expiry, another LCG-02 entry, and they record client addresses and paths, so C0.13 decides them with its other observability and its own class.

10. **The CDK workspace, its entries and its configuration; people deploy from a credential-free synth (C11-D23 to C11-D25).**
    - **One workspace (C11-D23).** `infra/` (`@hearth/infra`) is one CDK workspace: one `cdk.json`, one dependency set and one test suite, with two entry points run by Node 24's type stripping, as the server runs (T2). `bin/workload.ts` is the pipeline's entry and `cdk.json`'s app (`node bin/workload.ts`), for the account-agnostic non-prod, prod and restore stages. `bin/landing-zone.ts` is a person's entry, for `Hearth-Foundation`, `Hearth-LogArchive` and the Baselines, with explicit accounts. In PR 1 both are stubs that define no stack. The CDK CLI refuses an app with no stacks, so `check` has no synth step yet: `synth:lz` joins with PR 2's first landing-zone stack and `synth` with PR 3's first workload stack (C11-D62). Relative imports end in `.ts`, there are no enums or parameter properties (`erasableSyntaxOnly`), and aws-cdk-lib is imported as a namespace or default from its subpaths (`import ec2 from 'aws-cdk-lib/aws-ec2'`), because its lazy CommonJS exports may defeat Node's named-import detection; `infra/bin/entries.test.ts` runs both entries under plain Node now, and the synth steps will prove each stack loads. `cdk.json` sets `versionReporting: false` (the CLI otherwise forces the version-reporting metadata on), `cli-telemetry: false`, and aws-cdk-lib 2.274.0's recommended feature flags for a new project, with no plugin key and no id (IG-03). Two CDK apps were the alternative: twice the upkeep for one person.
    - **People deploy the way the pipeline does.** In a shell with no AWS credentials, a person synthesises `bin/landing-zone.ts` into `cdk.out` (`cdk synth --app "node bin/landing-zone.ts"`), reads the diff and runs the asset check (C11-D76); only then, in the credentialed shell, do they run `cdk deploy --app cdk.out`. So no repository code runs where `HearthOrgAdmin`, `OrganizationAccountAccessRole` or break-glass credentials exist, which is C11-D48's rule applied to people. The landing-zone entry, its configuration reader and the aspects are on the owner's-read list (item 31). IG-08 keeps `bin/workload.ts`'s import graph out of `src/foundation/`, `src/baseline/`, the configuration reader (`src/config/env.ts`) and the fixture (`src/testing/`), so the pipeline's synth never needs a person's configuration. `infra/package.json` has no `deploy`, `bootstrap` or `destroy` script (IG-07), and coding agents synth and test, and never deploy or bootstrap (CLAUDE.md, Infrastructure).
    - **Account-agnostic workload stacks (C11-D24).** Every workload stack has `env: { region: 'ap-southeast-1' }` and no account; its AZs come from `Fn::GetAZs`, and values cross stacks by SSM parameter name (`valueForStringParameter`), never `Fn::ImportValue` or a lookup. Synth runs with `--no-lookups`, `cdk.context.json` is git-ignored, and IG-02 and IG-03 refuse a lookup and a tracked context file, so a cloud assembly and a diff hold no account id. Explicit accounts appear only in `Hearth-Foundation`, `Hearth-LogArchive` and the two Baselines, which a person synthesises and deploys on their own machine. The deploy jobs' own CLI output does print account ids, so masking there is load-bearing (C11-D61, item 23).
    - **Configuration, validated (C11-D25).** `src/config/env.ts` reads the `HEARTH_*` variables (organisation and OU ids, account ids, the Identity Center instance and its store and group ids, the repository's OIDC subject format and ids, the alert email, the budget amounts) through a zod schema, for the landing-zone entry only; a problem names its variable and what it must be, never the value. The subject format, rendered for each GitHub environment, must start with `repo:` and the repository `HEARTH_GITHUB_REPOSITORY` names (or its immutable form, `owner@id/name@id`, from the two ids) and name that repository's `deploy.yml` on `main` as its `job_workflow_ref`, so a format naming another repository, such as one under a near-identical owner, is refused before any trust is written. It takes the Identity Center instance's id, never its ARN, which PR 2 forms at synth (`Stack.formatArn`), so no ARN is written down. A person keeps them in `infra/.env.local`, which `.env*` ignores, and `infra/.env.example` documents each one with a comment and an empty value. `src/testing/fixture.ts` builds a complete configuration whose account ids are made at run time (`fixtureAccount(n)` pads `n` to 12 digits), so none is written in the repository, and marks it `fixture: true`. `bin/landing-zone.ts` builds one target a run (`HEARTH_TARGET`: `foundation`, `log-archive`, `nonprod-baseline` or `prod-baseline`, each deployed into its own account under its own credentials; `all` is the fixture's alone), and refuses a fixture configuration when `HEARTH_TARGET` names a real deploy, and a real one under `HEARTH_FIXTURE=1`. `src/config/profiles.ts`, committed, holds what reviewers should see: domain names, CIDRs, sizes, counts and flags, `holdsRealData` among them (false for non-prod through C0.11, C11-D69). Until C11-Q08 is answered its domains are `.invalid` placeholders, which `bin/landing-zone.ts` refuses for a real target. `src/config/models.ts` holds both model lists (C11-D06, item 2).
    - **Dependencies.** `aws-cdk-lib` 2.274.0 exactly and `constructs` 10.8.1, with the repository's `zod` and `@hearth/shared`, in `dependencies`, and the `aws-cdk` CLI 2.1146.1, schema-compatible with that library, in `devDependencies`; `cdk-nag` 3.0.2 (rule packs only) joins with the first stack it checks, and `@hearth/server` in PR 3, for IAC-21 only. Each was installed with `npm install --save-exact --before=2026-10-23T00:00:00Z`, so caret-ranged dependencies of dependencies also resolve to releases at least 7 days old, and PR 1 states that date; aws-cdk-lib 2.275.0 is under 7 days old until 2 Nov 2026. No `@aws-sdk/client-*` is added: `infra/` reaches AWS only through CloudFormation, and the pipeline through the AWS CLI on the runner. npm's `overrides` can't reach aws-cdk-lib's bundled packages, so a high advisory in one blocks merges until AWS ships a fix, which a Renovate security PR brings without the 7-day wait. Renovate's "CDK" group moves `aws-cdk-lib`, `aws-cdk`, `constructs` and `cdk-nag` together. Each `npm ci` grows by about 160 MB; the server image, which installs only `@hearth/server`'s production dependencies, doesn't.

Items 11 to 30 are written by PRs 2 to 4, as the table above gives them.

31. **The owner's-read extension (C11-D74).** CLAUDE.md's Git section gains, beside the existing list (migrations, the privileged module's allow-list, the Owner bootstrap script and the two files that silence the secret scan): a PR that adds or changes `infra/bin/`, `infra/bootstrap/`, `infra/src/foundation/`, `infra/src/baseline/`, `infra/src/aspects/`, `infra/src/config/env.ts`, `infra/src/config/models.ts`, `infra/src/release/floors.ts`, `scripts/guards/infra.test.ts`, `scripts/guards/deploy-workflow.test.ts` (or the files those two guards read their rules from), `.github/workflows/deploy.yml`, `.github/environments/`, `scripts/protect-environments.sh` or `.claude/settings.json`, or that changes `holdsRealData`, `logLockMode` or `smsProvider` in `infra/src/config/profiles.ts`, lists an unticked "Before merging: @ashtonatnormal has read it" step, which only the owner's own tick, or the owner saying they have read that change, clears. The owner approved the wording with C11-Q03, and PR 1 is the first PR it binds.
    - **Why each path**, by what each holds once its PR builds it (PR 1 builds `infra/bin/`'s two entries, `env.ts`, `models.ts`, `profiles.ts` and the infra guard; PR 2 to PR 4 the rest). `infra/bin/` holds the break-glass helper, which runs with prod break-glass credentials, and the landing-zone entry, which runs, through its synthesised assembly, under `HearthOrgAdmin`; `env.ts` and the aspects shape every template a person deploys; `infra/bootstrap/` holds the bootstrap template, which carries the model deny (C11-D75); `src/foundation/` and `src/baseline/` hold the guardrails, the evidence floor, identity, break-glass and the OIDC trust; `models.ts` holds the model lists every model fence is rendered from; `floors.ts` holds the rollback floors, so removing one permits an unsafe rollback (C11-D58); the two guards hold the rules everything else is checked against (the files they read their rules from include `scripts/guards/infra.ts`, and IG-08's rule in `scripts/guards/boundaries.ts`); `deploy.yml`, the environments and their script hold the pipeline and its approval; `.claude/settings.json` will hold the deny rules that keep AWS sessions out of an agent's reach (C11-D77, PR 4); `holdsRealData` brings production's rules to non-prod (C11-D69), `logLockMode` makes the log buckets' lock permanent (C11-D18), and `smsProvider` starts real texts.
    - **Later in C0.11:** PR 4 adds the environments paragraph that mirrors `main`'s ruleset (the GitHub environments live in `.github/environments/`, are applied by `scripts/protect-environments.sh`, change only by PR, and the deploy gate refuses while they aren't live as written).

## Tooling that enforces these

- `npm run check` (typecheck of every workspace, Biome, Vitest and the demo's own tests) and `npm run build` (`apps/web` and `demo/`), in CI's `check` job. From C0.8 `check` also installs Playwright's browsers, runs `node scripts/check-web-dist.ts` straight after the build, and runs `npm run e2e` last, uploading its report, traces, failure screenshots and servers' logs when a step fails or the job is cancelled (T21 items 11 and 12); it runs on `ubuntu-24.04`, with a 45-minute limit that leaves the steps before the suite and the upload after it room beside the suite's own 15.
- `scripts/guards/ai-free.test.ts`: no banned model, speech, ML or OCR package (by name, alias, override or lockfile entry), no use of the browser's speech recognition or built-in AI, no request for `voiceIsolation`, and no code from the public npm CDNs it lists, before A0 (D41). Its list of model and speech hosts (`MODEL_HOSTS` in `scripts/guards/ai-free.ts`) also serves the CSP guard and the web bundle's scan (T21).
- `scripts/guards/sms-provider.test.ts` (C0.8): Twilio Verify's host appears only in `apps/server/src/auth/sms/twilio.ts`; only `main.ts` calls the guarded factories `createTwilioVerify()` and `createFakeVerify()` (their own tests aside); the unguarded `TwilioVerify` and `FakeVerify` classes are built only in their own files, the server's `testing/` folder and tests; and the loopback Twilio stand-in is loaded only by tests (T20 items 1 and 10).

From C0.9 it also keeps the api's transfer gate, `Api.transfers`, set only in `api.ts` (`registerApi`), the `testing/` folder and tests, and never named in `main.ts`, so production always asks Postgres (T22 item 8); and from C0.9 PR 2 the gateway's `admissionForTests` named only in `apps/server/src/realtime/index.ts` (which defines it), the `testing/` folder and tests, never in `main.ts`, so production always wires the notice's admission (T19 item 10, T22 item 30), and `publish_notice()` called only by the publish route (`compliance/notice.ts`), the `testing/` folder, tests and CI's checks in `apps/server/scripts/`, because it takes the words as given and only the route regenerates them and holds the DPO's free text to `AI_COPY` (PD-62). and from C0.6 the queue's, the outbox's and the flags': a fence against a flag change either way, a narrowed cohort, an enqueue that doesn't block a change, the lease sweep, heartbeats and give-backs against a finishing job, two claimers, two schedulers firing, two consumer rounds, a reset during a round, the clean-up during a round, the horizon, and a flag change by an Admin being deactivated or losing the role; and from C0.8 a code-only sign-in's finish that waits for a role grant holding the person's row, then reads the role and issues no session, T20 item 5; and from C0.9 the registers': a code's clearance check and a withdrawal, each waiting for the other, a "Save and keep cleared" that never leaves a check reading not cleared, a clearance and a change to its facts, two changes from one version of a purpose, a DPO being deactivated or losing the role while changing a register, and a restriction and a basis leaving consent, T22 items 8 and 13; and from C0.9 PR 2 the notice's: an acknowledgement and a publish, a publish against a purpose change, a "Save and keep cleared", a withdrawal and a clearance, two publishes from one version and two first publishes, an acknowledgement against a deactivation and a device sign-out, a publish against the DPO leaving or losing the role, and a send against a publish, T22 item 35; and from C0.9 PR 3b a submit against the requester's deactivation, two submits with one client key, two references drawn alike, the DPO leaving or losing the role during a case write or read, two steps from one revision, a log against its member's deactivation, a targets change against a publish, a contact change or another targets change, an audit read against a `dpo` revocation, and 0012 applied while a publish, a register write and `GET /api/notice` run, T22 item 61; and from C0.10 PR 1 a hold placed, a PDPC review step, a request for a copy or a correction sent, a release and a hold citing a request, each against a request's deletion, `is_held()` against a request being sent, two deletions for one requester, and 0013 applied under load, locking none of the busy tables; and from C0.10 PR 2 a cascade against a hold on another source's person, a derive against its source's deletion, two cascades with a shared dependant, a person-scoped deletion against a cascade and an edge's insert against its source's withdrawal, T23 item 19), `apps/server/scripts/check-catalogues.ts` (C0.6: every write grant of every runtime role, every function one can run and every `SECURITY DEFINER` and trigger function is decided in `apps/server/src/outbox/coverage.ts`, no content table is exempt, every column it matches exists, and the event catalogue, the flag registry and the worker's job roles equal 0007's and 0008's lists, T16 item 7; from C0.7, every consumer the worker runs is bound in `outbox_checkpoints_own_row()` to exactly its role and every bound name is one of them, read from the function's body and tried in a transaction it rolls back, T16 item 3; from C0.9, check 6: `PURPOSES` and `PROVIDERS` equal 0009's defaults functions both ways, with each purpose's `ai`, `pinned`, `needs_switch` and starting status, and every org in the scratch database has exactly their rows, T22 item 18, and from PR 2 that `NOTICE_TRANSFER_FACTS` equals `notice_facts()`'s keys, T22 item 27; from C0.10, check 7: `RETENTION_CLASSES` and `retention_class_defaults()` equal both ways, every table of `public` that isn't a partition, the ledger aside, in exactly one class, the fixed ages equal to their functions' bodies, the guard on exactly `GUARDED_TABLES`, the AI classes finite, the vocabularies equal, and every resolver answering, and check 8: every delete path the database allows decided in `apps/server/src/lifecycle/deletes.ts`, T23 item 19), `npm run db:types` leaving `apps/server/src/db/schema.ts` unchanged, and `scripts/check-sign-in-upgrade.sh`, run once for each of 0006's two session rules (0006 applied on top of sessions and accounts 0005 allows, including sessions longer than 90 days, ones that ended for reasons outside 0006's codes, and a leaver's open sessions, one made after they left: every row kept, the leaver's sessions and devices ended no earlier than each began, sessions not revoked capped at 90 days, revoked ones keeping their times and reasons, the rule a revoked one breaks left NOT VALID and the others validated, new rows held to both rules, and the schema matching a fresh build's apart from that rule), and `scripts/check-revocation-upgrade.sh` (C0.7: 0008 applied on top of the checkpoints 0007 allows, a C0.6 deployment's offboarding checkpoint among them. It is refused while a checkpoint breaks the binding, naming each one: a `revocation` checkpoint the offboarding role started in its own name, or an `offboarding` one naming another role. Nothing of it stays. Once those rows are gone it applies, and every other checkpoint keeps its position. Afterwards each job role can start and move only its own consumer's checkpoint, and the schema matches a fresh build's. From C0.9 it also applies 0009, which has no step of its own, over one outbox event of an existing type, and checks that the event is unchanged under a validated `outbox_events_type` and that every org, the seed's and one with no principals, has exactly the registers' defaults, T22 item 18), and `scripts/check-notice-upgrade.sh` (C0.9 PR 2: 0010 applied on top of the notice rows 0003 to 0009 allow, the seed's and orgs holding stand-in versions, acknowledgements made in the app, one outliving its device, and one the DPO recorded, and Twilio cleared with no version and under a stand-in. Every notice row is unchanged and nothing is linked, `hearth_app` writes neither notice table any more, an older version is refused and the latest acknowledged, and `transfer_cleared()` keeps PR 1's answers before the first generated version, NTU-01 to NTU-05, T22 item 35; from C0.9 PR 3a 0011 rides it, and NTU-06 to NTU-08 check the acknowledgement the DPO recorded before it, the notice's new link and publisher, and the clearances under the widened checks, T22 item 47), and `scripts/check-cases-upgrade.sh` (C0.9 PR 3b: 0012 applied on top of data built at 0011 through 0011's own functions, the seed's and two made-up orgs'. Every org has `request_handling`'s version 1 and every other register row is unchanged, every `dpo_settings` row reads the targets' defaults and is otherwise unchanged, both settings writers work over upgraded rows, the restriction foreign key and the outbox's type check are validated, a Compliance Officer reader's audit rows are unchanged, and a `c09.2` version still states no targets, RQU-01 to RQU-08, T22 item 61), and `scripts/check-lifecycle-upgrade.sh` (C0.10 PR 1: 0013 applied on top of data built at 0012 through 0012's own functions, the seed's and an org far from UTC holding refused copies, an open correction and outbox events with actors. Every row is unchanged, `is_held()` and `delete_case()` answer over the requests already there as T23 items 11 and 13 say, the guard is on exactly its three tables, and from PR 2, which 0014 rides, on five, with 0014's two tables empty and ungranted, `lifecycle_kinds()` equal to `HOLD_KINDS` and the cascade answering (LCU-10), the consumers' statements run over OD-27's column grants with the actor refused, and `retention_class_defaults()` equals the code's classes, LCU-01 to LCU-05, LCU-07, LCU-08 and LCU-10, T23 item 19). Each upgrade check compares the upgraded schema with a fresh build's through `scripts/schema-digest.sql`, the only check of the upgraded schema as a whole (the role, row level security and grant checks in `scripts/check-migrations.sh` run only on fresh and partial builds). It lists, one line each: schemas other than the system ones (owner and grants) and default privileges; every relation in `public` with its kind, owner, row level security, grants, sorted options (a view's `security_invoker`, `check_option` and `security_barrier`, a table's storage options), persistence, parent, partition bound and partition key; views' bodies; sequences' parameters and owning columns; enum, domain and composite types with their collations and defaults; custom collations; columns with their collations, defaults, identity, generated expressions, storage and grants; rules (constraints); indexes and whether each is valid; triggers and rewrite rules (`CREATE RULE`) and whether each is on; policies; functions, procedures and aggregates (with their declared arguments). Partitions are compared like any table under a name with the month folded (`data_access_events_yyyy_mm`, folded the same way in the names and text of its rules, indexes, triggers, rewrite rules, policies and column defaults, in foreign keys into it, and in its bound; the fold is reversible, escaping text that already reads like a folded name or month first, so a literal `'data_access_events_yyyy_mm'` can't pass for a folded month while a rule naming its own month folds alike in every month), and a partition's copies of its parent's indexes and primary key, unique and foreign key rules are listed by the parent's names, so a copy left unattached reads differently and Postgres shortening a long index name changes nothing; a month's own index, attached to none, is listed as `<partition>.(local)`, since Postgres may shorten and number its name. Identical lines are listed once, so builds made in different months compare equal, and each month partition also gets a hash of its own lines, so a month that lacks an index, rule, trigger or policy the others have still shows. It fixes the session settings deparsed text follows (time zone UTC, date and interval styles, float digits, bytea output, string escapes, identifier quoting, `search_path`) and deparses a domain's default afresh, so a database set to New York reads as one in UTC. A rule's NOT VALID is compared except for the rules the calling check names, as the digest lists them, in the psql variable `not_valid` (the sign-in check names the rule it leaves NOT VALID). It leaves out data and which months have partitions, the database's own settings, owner and extensions, roles (which `scripts/check-migrations.sh` checks on fresh and partial builds), tuning that changes no result (TOAST options, replica identity, column compression and statistics targets, extended statistics), Postgres's internal triggers (only a superuser can turn them off), names that carry a month other than through the partition's whole name (which make months differ and fail the check rather than hide anything), comments, column order, collation versions, and object kinds no migration makes yet (range types, operators, casts and the like; the migration that first makes one adds a line for it). `scripts/check-schema-digest.sh` (CI, after the upgrade checks) probes it with pairs of throwaway databases. Each respect the digest was widened for makes the digests differ, in a line the probe names: partitions' row level security, grants, owners, policies, indexes, rules, triggers, bounds and attachment, a month lacking what the others have, a rule literal or list bound that reads like the folded name or month, an index predicate holding text like the index's name, an invalid partitioned index, partition keys, options, persistence, sequences, identity and generated columns, column storage, collations, default privileges, schemas, NOT VALID, rewrite rules and aggregates (their grants, definitions and declared arguments). Equivalent builds compare equal: partitions for other months (including months whose index names Postgres shortened, months with their own indexes whose names it shortened and numbered, months with policies named for them, months whose rules or column defaults name their own month, and a foreign key into months held in different numbers), a database in New York or with other date, interval, float, bytea, string-escape, identifier-quoting or `search_path` settings, a domain made in New York, and, in copies of the fresh build, an older month's partition made by 0005's function with its clock set back, the months taken from the build's own partitions. From C0.10 it also proves, through `CHECKS_ONLY=1`, that `scripts/check-migrations.sh`'s composite-key check refuses two planted foreign keys that leave `org_id` out and passes their composite twin (T23 item 19).
- `packages/access/rules.test.ts`: the Rule 1–9 suite, printed case by case in CI's `check` job, with `roles.test.ts` (the per-role matrix for every admin action, `CLAUDE.md` Database) beside it; and `scripts/check-access-sql.ts` in CI's `migrations` job, which checks that `sqlReadableBy` on Postgres selects exactly what the in-memory rule allows for people, assistants and the system principal, before and after membership changes, that a NULL `acl_more` is nobody's, and that agents (who read by scope; A2 brings their filter) match no row, and, from C0.10 PR 2, that derived items written into `provenance_items` and `provenance_sources` reload through `derivedFacts()` as written, that the filter over their stored rows selects what `filterReadable` keeps, and that none is selected once a cascade has withdrawn it (T23 item 14).
- `apps/server/scripts/check-owner-bootstrap.ts` in CI's `migrations` job, after `scripts/check-access-sql.ts` and before `npm run db:types` (the bootstrap PR, T22 item 70; BSS-01 to BSS-16, BSR-01 to BSR-05): the Owner bootstrap script run as a person runs it, a child process with its input in the environment, as a throwaway NOINHERIT login that can only act as `hearth_owner`, against throwaway copies of a database it migrates, with fictional values only and never Twilio. Each mode's plan keeps nothing; the set-up writes exactly BSC-02's rows in one transaction as `hearth_owner` (the only check of a write by the owner), and refuses bad input before it connects and an existing org, a wrong target, environment marker or login, and migrations other than the checkout's after; two set-ups at once leave one org, and a set-up and a migration take turns; the recovery mode re-clears Twilio on the facts the latest version states and refuses as the function does; the correction replaces only a number nobody has signed in with; a failure to connect names its cause; and no output holds an input value.
- `/ready` (`apps/server/src/db/pool.ts`) fails when `DATABASE_URL` connects as a role that skips row level security or can use a job role, or when `WORKER_DATABASE_URL` connects as one that can become a role that skips row level security or can't take each of the worker's job roles (T18 item 4); the worker's also fails while any of its loops hasn't had a good round lately (T18 item 9). Realtime's has no storage check, which it never uses, and adds its gateway's (T19 item 12); its `/ready/local`, which fails only for reasons local to the process, is the load balancer's health check.
- `npm run test:integration`, in CI's `check` job after the local database: every `*.integration.test.ts` against the Compose stack, listed case by case, each run on its own throwaway database and Valkey key prefix (the pool's one needs neither). Today that is the sign-in, device and socket-revocation tests (`apps/server/src/auth/sign-in.integration.test.ts`, BUILD-PLAN C0.5's "Done when"), and C0.6's: the outbox (`apps/server/src/outbox/outbox.integration.test.ts`), the job queue, the scheduler and durable offboarding (`apps/server/src/jobs/jobs.integration.test.ts`), flag changes and their reach to every process (`apps/server/src/flags/flags.integration.test.ts`), the worker's database roles and transaction bounds (`apps/server/src/db/roles.integration.test.ts`), and the pools' statement bound against a peer gone silent, through a TCP proxy it freezes (`apps/server/src/db/pool.integration.test.ts`, T18 item 7), with worker instances from the harness (`apps/server/src/testing/stack.ts`); and C0.7's realtime gateway (`apps/server/src/realtime/gateway.integration.test.ts`, with two realtime processes: the protocol, topics, re-authorise and refetch (no replay, and no stream position in any frame), fan-out and the reader's resets, the heartbeat, the flags nudge, and revocation within 60 s by the push, durable and re-check paths, with Valkey or Postgres down, T19 item 17); and C0.8's: sign-in by code for everyone and the PIN steps for PIN roles, re-authentication by method, the floor and the breaker, and the transfer gate (in the sign-in suite, on the `FakeVerify` provider), the code challenge's scripts on the stack's Valkey (`apps/server/src/auth/codes.integration.test.ts`), the dormant passkeys, which only this suite of the integration run switches on (`apps/server/src/auth/passkeys.integration.test.ts`, T20 item 8), and the web shell's two GETs (`apps/server/src/web/routes.integration.test.ts`, T21 item 10); and C0.9's: the registers (`apps/server/src/compliance/registers.integration.test.ts`, which owns the `register.*` events: the DPO's reads and writes with their events and audit rows, the refusals and the PIN re-authentication, and a worker seeing each change), the transfer gate on the real register in the sign-in suite (each case in its own org), and the purpose interlock in the flags suite (T22); and C0.9 PR 2's notice suite (`apps/server/src/compliance/notice.integration.test.ts`: no access before acknowledging, on every `/api` route the harness collects; a new version forcing re-acknowledgement over REST and sockets; the pre-v0 exemption; publishing, acknowledging and `GET /api/notice`; each case in its own org, with the gateway's admission on, which the harness otherwise keeps off), the send state in the sign-in suite (XFR-I4 to I7) and PGT-I1, in its own org, in the flags suite (T22 item 35); and C0.9 PR 3a's: the DPO's contact, before v0 and after (NTI-03, NTI-08, NTI-09), recorded acknowledgements (NTI-10), deemed consent by conduct (NTI-11), the contact's rules in both places (NTI-12), and in the sign-in suite the break-glass re-clear and the revert of a stated fact (XFR-I8, XFR-I9) (T22 item 47); and C0.9 PR 3b's cases suite (`apps/server/src/compliance/cases.integration.test.ts`, RQI-01 to RQI-12: a request reaching the DPO's queue, the gate and who may read it, intake, steps and closure, a resend and the cap, no request text in a log line, audit row or outbox row, due days over the api, the rules on both sides, the count, the withdrawal template and the targets), with NTI-13 and NTI-14 in the notice suite (T22 item 61); and the bootstrap PR's `first-owner` suite (`apps/server/src/auth/first-owner.integration.test.ts`, BSI-01 to BSI-03), whose stack's org the Owner bootstrap script sets up before write tracking starts: the founder's code before v0 and the PIN step, a member's `skipped_no_notice`, and v0 stating the set-up's clearances (T22 item 70); and from C0.10 the Valkey keys' expiry, every key the sign-in and realtime suites leave under their run's prefix living no longer than its family's figure in the retention registry (LCI-01, LCI-02, T23 item 6). Each suite on the harness sweeps every write it committed and fails on one without its event (T16 item 7), and fails on any Node warning, pg's for a query sent to a client already running one included.
- `scripts/check-server.sh`, in CI's `check` job: every role starts under plain Node against the Compose stack (the worker as `hearth_worker`), answers `/health` and then `/ready` (polled for up to 20 s), logs nothing at error level, logs its subscription to the flags channel (`channel_subscribed`), and realtime also its subscriptions to the `revoked` and `access` channels and a 200 on `/ready/local` (T19 item 12), serves only its own routes (a code request and a cross-origin upgrade to `/ws`), and stops cleanly on SIGTERM; then the same for the production image, which also proves the image holds `@simplewebauthn/server`.
- CI's `secrets` job (`.github/workflows/secrets.yml`, also run on every push to any other branch: gitleaks with its default rules, plus any reviewed allowlists, over every commit on every branch, two-parent merges included and octopus merges refused) and `audit` job (`npm audit` and registry signatures), which `check` waits on.
- Renovate (`renovate.json`): weekly dependency updates, including the gitleaks version, for releases at least a week old (security fixes exempt; lockfile refreshes too, while every pinned version is that old); major versions wait for the owner's approval on the Dependency Dashboard; Node stays on 24 and Postgres on 17.
